# Changes

## 2026-11-25
- Fix (stage 3 task 1, `crates/ipc`): `err_log!` and `debug_log!` (and so the lister `dlog!` and the iconview `dbg_log!` wrappers) no longer panic when stderr cannot be written. They called `eprintln!`, which panics on a write error such as EPIPE (a stderr pipe whose reader is gone, with SIGPIPE ignored by the Rust runtime). Both macros now call the new `opusdm_ipc::write_log_line(tag, args)`, which writes `[tag:pid] message\n` with `writeln!` on the locked stderr and ignores a failed write. The output is byte-identical (same format, same single-line write). Only the closed / broken stderr case changes. This is the one stage 3 change that touches every OpusDM process (the log macros are shared); the other `eprintln!` calls in the workspace are untouched. Verified: new tests `log_tests::logging_to_a_closed_stderr_does_not_panic` (RED first: the child exited with status 101; GREEN after) and `log_tests::log_line_format_is_unchanged` (passed before and after the change), `cargo test -p opusdm-ipc` 378 passed, `cargo build --workspace` ok, `cargo clippy -p opusdm-ipc --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean before and after. Rule 4 by hand on a private Xvfb :7 (sandbox bus, private config copy, `~/.config/opusdm` sha256 and picom.conf mtime unchanged): `run-x11.sh` (Xephyr :8): Lister Enter in the path bar, double click on a folder, Backspace, parent button, Alt+Left / Alt+Right, second Lister with SOURCE / DEST roles, a Copy job (toolbar button), Alt+1 / Alt+2 desktops, Ctrl+Shift+space (Run window) all behaved; Ctrl+Print showed the screenshot overlay (Escape left it up, no file written to the private shot dir); `run-wayland.sh` (labwc) and `run-native.sh`: started, hub / panel / dock / desktop up, a Lister opened with a double click; the log lines have the same `[tag:pid]` form. NOT verified: F3 / F5 (xdotool F keys do not reach the Lister in the sandbox, known from stage 2; F4 closed the focused Lister here, looks like the same Alt-held keymap artifact, not compared with the base commit); Ctrl+backslash (an `opusdm-lister` window was created but no terminal was visible in the screenshot, known sandbox failure from stage 2, not compared with the base commit); Ctrl+Print writing a file; the wayland and native sandboxes were only started and a Lister opened (no path bar / hotkey / copy checks there); the live session (`scripts/live-update.sh`), a real closed stderr in the running desktop.
- Compositor (stage 3 task 2): `compositor.json` gains one key per built-in binding (`switcher_next` `Alt+Tab`, `switcher_prev` `Alt+Shift+Tab`, `switcher_all` `Ctrl+Alt+Tab`, `close` `Alt+F4`, `desktop_left` / `desktop_right` `Ctrl+Alt+Left` / `Right`, `desktop_1`..`desktop_4` `Super+F1`..`Super+F4`, `send_left` / `send_right` `Shift+Alt+Left` / `Right`, `show_desktop` `Super+d`, `lower` `Alt+Escape`, `focus_left` / `right` / `up` / `down` `Super+Shift+arrows`, plus the existing `window_menu` and `quit`), `snap_threshold` (default 10, clamped 0..=100, a clamped value is reported) and `title_layout` (default `close,iconify,max:`). The per-key fallback is kept; an empty key now disables its binding (before, an empty `window_menu` / `quit` fell back on the default with an error). Unknown keys are now also part of the error list (same log line as before). New `WmSettings` (`src/wm/settings.rs`: wrap, snap, transparency, active / inactive opacity clamped 20..=100) read from `desktop.json`; `State::config` and `State::wm` hold the last read values. New IPC `reload` (re-reads both files read only, applies the desktop count with `set_desktop_count`, rebuilds the built-in bindings keeping the hub table, replies `{"ok":true,"errors":[...]}` with the `compositor.json` problems) and `bindings/list` (`[{"name","key"}]`, enabled built-ins only, canonical key text, quit only when enabled). The new built-ins are parsed, stored and listed only: `lookup` does not match them yet (Task 3 wires their actions), so Alt+Tab, Alt+F4 and the rest still reach the clients and a hub hotkey on one of those keys is not refused. Verified: new unit tests in `config.rs`, `hotkeys.rs`, `wm/settings.rs` and 4 new `tests/ipc.rs` tests (`bindings/list` defaults, `reload` with a new `compositor.json` and `desktop.json`, `reload` keeps the hub hotkeys, a broken `compositor.json` is reported); `cargo test -p opusdm-compositor` 124 + 84 + 21 passed; headless + ipc integration tests looped 5 times, all green; `cargo clippy -p opusdm-compositor --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean. NOT verified: no manual sandbox run (no visible behaviour yet); the `desktop.json` problems are logged by `opusdm_ipc` but are not in the `reload` reply, and a broken `desktop.json` on `reload` resets the desktop count to the default (as at startup).
- Compositor (stage 3 task 3): the built-in window manager bindings of `compositor.json` now run (new `src/wm/actions.rs`, `Action` / `Direction`, `Binding::BuiltIn`). Lookup order: quit, window menu, the other built-ins (first in `BUILTIN_DEFAULTS` order wins on a shared key), hub. `Alt+F4` asks the active window to close; `Ctrl+Alt+Left` / `Right` step the desktop and `Shift+Alt+Left` / `Right` send the active window there and follow it (both wrap per `desktop.json` `wrap_desktops`, default true, not the `wrap=no` of openbox-rc.xml; at an edge without wrap nothing changes); `Super+F1`..`F4` show desktop 1-4 (an index past the count is logged with `err_log!`, nothing changes); `Super+d` toggles show desktop (minimizes the shown windows of the current desktop and remembers their ids in `State::show_desktop`; the second press restores those still alive and focuses the topmost; activating a window, unminimizing one, a desktop switch or a newly mapped window leaves the mode without restoring); `Alt+Escape` lowers the active window to the bottom of the stack and the new topmost shown window gets the keyboard (Openbox Lower + FocusToBottom + Unfocus); `Super+Shift+arrows` activate the nearest shown window whose centre lies in that direction (ties: upper window). An action that needs a window with no active window only writes a `debug_log!` line. `Alt+Tab`, `Alt+Shift+Tab`, `Ctrl+Alt+Tab` call `State::open_switcher`, a `debug_log!` stub until task 4: until then these keys are swallowed and do nothing (before this task they reached the client). While the window menu is open a built-in does not run and its key goes to the menu as before (`Alt+Escape` still closes the menu). The hub table is kept whole (`hub_entries`) and resolved again on every `rebuild_builtins` (startup, IPC `reload`): a hub hotkey whose key a built-in takes (any of the 20, quit and window menu included) is logged with `err_log!` (`hotkey K: F conflicts with the built-in N, the built-in wins`) and the message is in the `reload` reply; the entry comes back when the built-in moves away. `set_hub` errors keep their entry order. `reload` reads `desktop.json` itself (serde_json + `sanitized()`, read only, no `.bak` written): a file that exists but cannot be read or parsed keeps the current desktop count and `WmSettings` and puts the problem in `errors` (with `err_log!`); a missing file gives the defaults, as at startup (startup itself is unchanged). IPC `debug/key` gains `"state": "press" | "release" | "tap"` (default tap; press = modifiers then key, release = key then modifiers, all through `keyboard_key`). Verified: unit tests (`relative_desktop`, `directional_target`, `action_for`, built-ins in lookup, hub entry shadowed then back after rebuild; the stage 2 test pinning that the built-ins pass to the clients was replaced), 10 new headless tests (Alt+F4 close, Ctrl+Alt+Left wrap 0 -> 3 and back, Super+F2, Shift+Alt+Right send and follow, Alt+Escape lower, Super+d twice, Super+d then a new window, Super+Shift+Left, no active window, debug/key press / release) and 2 new ipc tests (reload reports a shadowed hub hotkey and gives it back; broken / unreadable / missing desktop.json); `cargo test -p opusdm-compositor` 130 + 94 + 23 passed; headless + ipc looped 5 times, all green; `cargo clippy -p opusdm-compositor --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo build --workspace` ok. NOT verified: no manual sandbox run (nested backend, real keyboard): every action was exercised only through the headless backend and `debug/key`; directional focus with overlapping or fullscreen windows; show desktop with an exclusive keyboard layer mapped; the actions on the live session (the compositor is not the owner's session).
- Compositor (stage 3 task 4): Alt+Tab window switcher (new `src/switcher/`: `model.rs` pure, `render.rs` cairo/pango, `mod.rs` the `State` side) and three stage 2 window menu fixes. Focus history `State::mru` (most recent first) is updated wherever the active window changes (`focus_window`, `_set_active_under_layer`) and pruned of dead windows by `refresh`. `switcher_entries(mru, windows, all_desktops, current)`: windows of the current desktop (every desktop with `switcher_all`), minimized ones included, in focus order, windows never focused after them topmost first. `switcher_next` (`Alt+Tab`) opens with the second entry selected, `switcher_prev` (`Alt+Shift+Tab`) with the last one, `switcher_all` (`Ctrl+Alt+Tab`) over every desktop. The hold modifiers are those of the configured combo (`HotkeyTable::combo_of`), not a hard-coded Alt. While it is open every non-modifier press goes to the switcher before any binding: the combo key moves the selection (Shift toggled against the opening combo: the other way), Escape cancels, any other key (bindings included, quit too) is swallowed with a `debug_log!`; modifiers still reach the focused client. When the last hold modifier is released the selected window is activated with `activate_window` (shows its desktop, restores, raises, focuses). Entries of destroyed windows are dropped (the selection moves to the entry that followed), an empty switcher closes, an exclusive keyboard layer closes it with no action (in `refresh` and before each key) and refuses to open it. Nested backend: a centred box `SWITCHER_WIDTH` 480 wide, rows of `SWITCHER_ROW_HEIGHT` 28 (title ellipsized on the left, app id dimmed on the right, at most 35% of the row), the selected row in the window menu active colours (`MenuTheme`), drawn above the window menu, cached by rows + selection + scale. Stage 2 menu fixes: the "Move to desktop" submenu opens on its first enabled entry (keyboard and click; ruling S3-R4: the headless test `the_window_menu_moves_the_window_to_another_desktop` lost its extra Down, the unit tests that expected the disabled entry were changed); new `src/draw/mod.rs` holds the shared cairo helpers (`paint_buffer`, `fill_gradient`, `set_rgb`, `components`) and `RenderCache`, a one-entry cache that also caches a failed draw, so a persistent render error is not redrawn every frame (a new state is tried again; the stage 2 unit test `a_bad_scale_is_an_error_and_nothing_is_cached` became `a_render_error_is_not_drawn_again_for_the_same_state`); one constant `draw::DRAW_SCALE` replaces `MENU_SCALE` and the literal `1.0` of the damage tracker and of the menu / switcher element placement (value unchanged, 1). Verified: TDD (RED: 14 unit and 8 headless tests failed on stubs; GREEN after), `cargo test -p opusdm-compositor` 145 unit + 102 headless + 23 ipc (1 ignored) passed, 5 runs in a row all green, `cargo clippy -p opusdm-compositor --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean. Manual (Step 5): PASS. Release binary `--nested --size 1280x800` in a private Xvfb :7, sandbox D-Bus (`dbus-run-session`, live bus unset), private `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` (default compositor.json) and `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, three foot windows A, B, C driven with the real keys through xdotool on :7: Alt held + Tab showed the switcher centred with C / B / A, B highlighted; a second Tab moved it to A; releasing Alt raised and activated A (`windows/list`: A activated); Alt+Shift+Tab opened on B (the last entry), Escape then Alt release changed nothing (A still active); Alt+Tab tap went back to C. `~/.config/opusdm` sha256 and picom.conf mtime identical before and after. NOT verified: the window menu submenu fix and the cached render error by hand (unit / headless only); a scale other than 1; the switcher with many windows (a box taller than the output sticks to its top edge, rows are not scrolled); pointer input while the switcher is open (clicks still reach clients, not specified by the plan); losing the host window focus while it is open (the nested backend releases held keys, which activates the selection); run-native.sh with the hub (the binary was run alone).
- Compositor (stage 3 task 4, review fix 1): while the window switcher is open the quit binding (`Ctrl+Alt+BackSpace`) runs again, as with the window menu open (the earlier entry above said every key, quit included, was swallowed: that was wrong for quit). A hub hotkey pressed while the switcher is open is still swallowed, but now logged with `err_log!` naming the hub function (was `debug_log!`); the other built-ins are still swallowed with `debug_log!`. Test harness: `TestClient::modifiers_depressed()` (last `wl_keyboard.modifiers`). New headless tests: quit ends the compositor with the switcher open; a hub hotkey is swallowed and logged; Alt held + Tab tapped twice from C activates A (the brief's exact case); the destroyed-window and exclusive-layer switcher tests also check that the keyboard target sees no modifier held after the Alt release. Verified: the quit and hub tests failed before the `input.rs` change and pass after; the two modifier checks and the double-Tab test already passed on the old code (they pin behaviour); `cargo test -p opusdm-compositor` 145 unit + 105 headless + 23 ipc (1 ignored) passed; clippy clean. NOT verified: quit while the switcher is open by hand in the nested sandbox.
- Compositor (stage 3 task 5, part 1): edge snapping. New `src/snap.rs`: pure `SnapZone { Left, Right, Top }`, `snap_zone(pointer, area, threshold)` (within `threshold` of the usable area's left / right / top edge, or past it; left / right win in a corner; threshold 0 = no zone) and `snap_rect(zone, area)` (left / right half, the right half takes the odd pixel; `Top` = the whole area); `State::snap_zone_at` (none when `desktop.json` `snap_enabled` is false; threshold `compositor.json` `snap_threshold`, default 10), `State::snap_target` (the only place the snap rectangle is computed: preview, snap, usable area change; Task 6 applies the insets there) and `State::snap_window`. `MoveGrab` (Alt + left drag and client `xdg_toplevel.move`) shows the zone under the pointer in `State::snap_preview` while dragging and, released in a zone, configures the window to the half and moves it there (`WindowInfo::snapped`, size before the snap in `restore`) or maximizes it for `Top` (stage 1 maximize path, with `restore` set to the size the grab knows, since the client may not have committed its restored size yet). Starting a move grab on a snapped window restores its size like a maximized one (kept under the pointer); a resize grab or an unmaximize ends the snapped state; maximize / fullscreen of a snapped window keep the size before the snap to restore (`shell/xdg.rs` `fill_window` / `unfill_window`); a usable area change re-fits snapped windows to their half (`usable_area_changed`). The nested backend draws the preview as a translucent light blue `SolidColorRenderElement` above the windows and layers, below the window menu and the switcher. New headless-only IPC `debug/pointer {"x","y"}` (absolute motion through `State::pointer_motion`) and `debug/button {"button": "left"|"right"|"middle", "state": "press"|"release"}` (through `State::pointer_button`: click focus, Alt grabs, window menu), `input::BTN_MIDDLE`. Test harness: `Compositor::start_with_config(files)` writes config files before the start. Smithay / plan differences: `snap_zone` takes `(i32, i32)` instead of a `Point` (as `geometry.rs`); files outside the brief list touched: `shell/xdg.rs` (maximize keeps the pre-snap size, area change), `input.rs` (`BTN_MIDDLE`), `main.rs`. Verified: unit tests for `snap_zone` / `snap_rect` (RED then GREEN), headless Alt + drag to the left half (window 0,0 960x1080 in `windows/list`), right half, top (maximized), a snapped window dragged away gets 400x300 back, a snapped window dragged to the top restores the pre-snap size after unmaximize, `{"snap_enabled": false}` only moves the window, `debug/pointer` + `debug/button` deliver a press to the client at the right surface position; the full crate suite 5 times in a row, clippy clean. Manual (nested, release binary alone in a private Xvfb :7, sandbox D-Bus, empty private config, two then three foot windows): PASS for the left, right and top edge (preview drawn during the drag, result in `windows/list`), PASS for dragging a maximized-by-snap foot away (696x494 back). foot rounds the half to its cell grid (636x793 inside 640x800), as clients do. NOT verified: `run-native.sh` with the hub and panel (the binary ran alone), the usable area change re-fitting a snapped window with a real panel (code path only), a client `xdg_toplevel.move` (title bar drag) snap (foot was dragged with Alt only), scale other than 1.
- Compositor (stage 3 task 5, part 2): window opacity and `window/set-geometry`. New `src/opacity.rs`: pure `window_opacity(settings, active, override_percent)` (the override wins; else 100 when `transparency_enabled` is off; else `active_opacity` / `inactive_opacity`, defaults 100 / 88, as picom's `active-opacity` / `inactive-opacity` on X11) and `OPACITY_OVERRIDE_RANGE` 20..=100. `WindowInfo::opacity_override`; `State::effective_opacity` reads `State::wm` at draw time, so an IPC `reload` re-applies it with no extra hook (ruling S3-R3); "active" is the xdg `Activated` state (the same flag `windows/list` reports, so a window stays opaque while the panel has the keyboard). The nested backend draws each toplevel surface tree with that alpha and its popups opaque (it renders the popups itself instead of `Window::render_elements`, same placement); layer surfaces, the window menu, the switcher and the snap preview are opaque. `WindowView` (`windows/list`, watch events) gains `"opacity"` (effective percent). New IPC `window/set-opacity {"id", "opacity": 20..=100 | null}` (null clears; out of range, not an integer, missing or unknown id: error) and `window/set-geometry {"id","x","y","width","height"}` (`State::set_window_geometry`, the one place it is computed: leaves maximized / fullscreen / snapped and drops `restore`, configures the size, moves a shown window or sets the position a hidden one shows at; a finished resize track is reset so its commits do not move the window; empty size or unknown id: error). Test-only `WindowView` constructors in `protocols/foreign_toplevel.rs` and `switcher/model.rs` got the new field. Verified: unit tests for `window_opacity` (RED then GREEN: defaults 100 / 88, override 50 wins, transparency off gives 100); IPC tests: two windows report 88 (inactive) and 100 (active), `window/set-opacity` 50 then null, bad values refused, a watcher sees the change, `reload` after writing `{"inactive_opacity": 60}` reports 60 and `{"transparency_enabled": false}` reports 100, `window/set-geometry` moves and resizes (client configure has the size) and unmaximizes, bad requests refused; full suite 5 times, clippy clean. Manual (same nested sandbox): PASS, an inactive foot is drawn at 88%: its title bar pixel is 153 against 170 when forced to 100 over the 26 clear colour (0.88 x 170 + 0.12 x 26 = 153), the window buttons of a foot below show faintly through an inactive foot above it, at an override of 50 its prompt text shows through clearly; the active foot is opaque; `window/set-geometry` placed two foots. NOT verified: popup opacity by hand (foot opens no popup; the code path renders popups at alpha 1), the hub side of `set-opacity` / `set-geometry` (Task 11), the opacity of a fullscreen inactive window, a GTK client.
- Compositor (stage 3 task 5, review fix 1): a move grab changes nothing until the pointer has moved `MOVE_DRAG_THRESHOLD` = 8 px (on either axis, Openbox's drag threshold) from the press (`grabs.rs`: `past_drag_threshold`, `MoveGrab::dragging`, new `State::restore_for_drag`). Behaviour change, also for maximized and fullscreen windows: before, any move grab (Alt + press, or a client `xdg_toplevel.move`, e.g. a CSD title bar press) restored a maximized / fullscreen window to its old size and placed it under the pointer at the press; now that happens only on the first motion past the threshold, so a focus click on the title bar of a maximized or snapped window keeps it maximized / snapped. A grab released before the threshold leaves the window exactly as it was (no move, no restore, no snap); every move grab (also of a normal window) now ignores the first 7 px of motion, then jumps to the full pointer delta. Notes: `window/set-geometry` during an active resize grab is overridden by the grab's next motion (the grab keeps configuring its own size); snapped -> maximize -> unmaximize gives back the size before the snap, not the half (the window is no longer snapped). Verified: headless `an_alt_click_on_a_snapped_window_keeps_it_snapped` and `an_alt_click_on_a_maximized_window_keeps_it_maximized` (each with a press / release and a 5 or 7 px jitter; both FAILED on the previous `grabs.rs`, pass now), `an_alt_drag_past_the_threshold_restores_a_maximized_window`, `a_snapped_window_follows_a_usable_area_change` (a 40 px panel re-fits the left half to 0,40 960x1040), `set_geometry_ends_the_snapped_state` (no re-fit after set-geometry), unit `the_drag_threshold_is_8_pixels_on_either_axis`; full suite 158 / 117 / 30 (1 ignored) passed, clippy clean. Manual (nested, private Xvfb :7, sandbox D-Bus, owner config sha256 unchanged): PASS, a foot snapped to the left half stays at 0,0 636x793 after an Alt + click with a 4 px jitter, and a real Alt + drag still restores it to 696x494. NOT verified by hand: a client-initiated move (CSD title bar press) on a maximized window, the 8 px threshold on a fullscreen window.
- Compositor (stage 3 task 6): server side decorations, part 1 (protocol and frame geometry; nothing is drawn yet). New `src/decor/`: `mod.rs` implements `XdgDecorationHandler` (`zxdg_decoration_manager_v1` global, `delegate_xdg_decoration!`): a client asking `server_side` gets it and its window is decorated (`WindowInfo.decorated`); `client_side`, `unset_mode`, a decoration object with no mode and no decoration object at all stay client side, so GTK4 apps (the Lister) keep exactly the stage 1 / stage 2 sizes and positions (pinned by `a_client_side_window_keeps_the_stage_1_sizes_and_positions`). `geometry.rs` (pure): `Insets`, `insets(decorated, fullscreen, &DecorTheme)` (`TITLE_HEIGHT` 24 + border on top, border on the other sides, zero when not decorated or fullscreen), `client_rect` / `frame_rect`, `TitleLayout::parse` (labwc syntax, default `"close,iconify,max:"`, unknown or repeated names skipped with `err_log!`), `TitleButton`, `FrameZone`, `title_bar_rect`, `button_rects` (16 px buttons, 2 px apart, 4 px from the bar ends and centered), `title_text_rect`, `frame_zone`. `theme.rs`: `DecorTheme { border_width }`, default the OpusDMTheme value 4 (ruling S3-R1; Task 7 adds the colours and the themerc loading), stored in `State::decor_theme`. Every placement of a decorated window goes through the insets: the cascade places the frame, maximize / snap / `window/set-geometry` / a usable area change give the client the area minus the insets, fullscreen drops them, a hidden window shows again inside its frame, a maximized / snapped window dragged past the threshold keeps the pointer at the same relative place of its frame, and `windows/list` (so the foreign-toplevel and pager views built from it) reports the frame. `window/set-geometry` takes the frame rectangle and refuses one with no room for the client. A maximized or snapped window whose mode changes is fitted again; a window in the normal state keeps its client where it is. Space locations stay client locations (the frame is derived), so the move / resize grabs and the drawing are unchanged. Differences from the plan: `frame_zone` takes the point as `(i32, i32)` (as `snap_zone`), not a Smithay `Point`; the parsed `TitleLayout` is not stored in `State` yet (nothing uses it before Task 7, `backend/mod.rs` is not in this task); the title bar layout functions carry `#[allow(dead_code)]` until Task 7 uses them. Smithay 0.7.0 gives no callback when a client destroys its `zxdg_toplevel_decoration_v1` (the object lives in Smithay's private toplevel data), so such a window stays decorated until it asks another mode on a new decoration object (the same in anvil). Verified: unit tests (insets, layout, buttons, title text, `frame_zone` on a 400x300 frame: close button, title, right border, corners, client), headless tests (server side granted; client side / no mode / no object stay client side; cascade by the frame; maximize under a 40 px panel configures 1912x1008 and lists the usable area; fullscreen configures the output; snap left and set-geometry give the area minus the frame; a too small set-geometry is refused; a mode change while maximized refits; a decorated maximized window dragged away), clippy `-D warnings`, headless + ipc tests 5 times in a row. NOT verified: no manual sandbox run (nothing is drawn yet: the frame area of a decorated window is empty in the nested backend and clicks there reach whatever is below until Task 7); no real GTK / Qt / foot client checked for its decoration request.
- Compositor (stage 3 task 7): server side decorations, part 2: drawing and title bar input. `src/decor/theme.rs`: `DecorTheme` gains the frame colours (`active_title` / `inactive_title` gradients, `active_label` / `inactive_label`, `active_button` / `inactive_button` glyphs, `hover_bg`, `pressed_bg`, `active_border` / `inactive_border`, `border_width`), defaults the OpusDMTheme values (`#414141`/`#202020`, `#DEE0D8`/`#E0E0D8`, `#f8f8f8`/`#747474`, `#e0e0e0`/`#747474`, `#398dc6`/`#236d83`, `#235679`/`#154350`, `#181818`/`#404040`, 4), `DecorTheme::parse` (missing key = default, bad value logged and default kept) and `DecorTheme::load` (same search path as the window menu theme), loaded once at startup into `State::decor_theme`. `src/menu/theme.rs` now shares `themerc_entries`, `gradient_flag` (a texture without `gradient`, e.g. `flat solid` or `parentrelative`, draws its `color` alone), `read_themerc` and `theme_dirs` with the frame theme; the menu theme uses `gradient_flag` too (no change for OpusDMTheme, whose menu textures are gradients). New `src/decor/render.rs`: `render_title` draws a `frame width x (24 + border)` opaque image (top border and side borders beside the bar in the border colour, vertical title gradient, buttons in the layout order as glyphs: X close, bottom bar iconify, box with a thick top maximize; the hovered button of the active window on the hover gradient, a pressed button on the pressed gradient; the title centered between the buttons in `Sans Bold 9`, ellipsized), `border_rects` (left, right, bottom borders), `WindowDecor` (title image cached by `(TitleState { title, active, width, hovered, pressed }, layout, theme, scale)`, one `SolidColorBuffer` per border). `State::decor_views` lists the frames of the shown decorated windows (active = `Activated`, alpha = the window's `effective_opacity`); the nested backend draws them just below each window's surfaces (title image + three solid borders, with the window's opacity, so a translucent window has a translucent frame) and keeps one `WindowDecor` per window id, dropped when the window is no longer drawn with a frame. `src/input.rs`: the hit test now returns `Under::Surface` or `Under::Frame(window, zone)`: per window, topmost first, its surfaces exactly as Smithay's `element_under` (bbox + input region), then its frame, which covers what is below it (before, a click on the empty frame area reached the window underneath). The pointer on a frame has no client focus. Openbox mousebinds: left press on a title bar button arms it and the release on the same button runs it (Close = `close_window`, Iconify = `set_minimized`, Maximize = `toggle_maximize`; released off it nothing runs); left press on the title starts a `MoveGrab` (Task 5 threshold: a click on a maximized window leaves it maximized), a second left press on the same title within `DOUBLE_CLICK_MS` (500) toggles maximize; left press on a border starts a `ResizeGrab` from its edges; right press on the title or a border opens the window menu at the pointer (`open_window_menu_at`); middle press on the title or a border lowers the window (`lower_window`, no focus; Openbox binds it on every frame context, the brief names the title). Every left / right frame press raises and focuses the window first; with Alt held the Alt move / resize grabs run as on the client. Presses that start no grab are swallowed with their release (`swallowed_buttons`), so no client sees half a click. The hovered button (motion with no grab) and the pressed one live in `State::frame_pointer` (window ids only); the cursor over a frame is the resize arrow of the border / corner, the default arrow on the title (reset to the default when the pointer leaves the frames for no surface). On host focus loss the nested backend disarms a pressed title button before it synthesizes the releases. `compositor.json` `title_layout` is parsed into `State::title_layout` at startup and on IPC `reload` (unknown or repeated buttons go to the reload reply's `errors`, `TitleLayout::parse_with_errors`). From the Task 6 review: the module-wide `#![allow(dead_code)]` of `decor/geometry.rs` is gone (everything is used); a window in the normal state that becomes decorated while shown and whose title bar would be above the usable area moves down so its frame starts at the top of the area. `menu/mod.rs`: `toggle_maximize` made public (was `_toggle_maximize`), `open_window_menu_at`. `backend/mod.rs`: one line parses the title layout at startup. Smithay 0.7.0 names as in the plan (`Space::element_bbox`, `SpaceElement::is_in_input_region`, `MemoryRenderBufferRenderElement::from_buffer` alpha, `SolidColorRenderElement::from_buffer` alpha); no new feature. Verified: unit tests (theme of the repo themerc and of every key, solid textures, title image size at scale 1 and 2, active / inactive colours, hover / pressed backgrounds, close glyph, long title and tiny frame, border rects, cache redraws only on state change, double click time, frame cursors), 13 new headless tests through `debug/pointer` / `debug/button` (close button -> `xdg_toplevel.close`, released off the button -> nothing, iconify / maximize buttons, double click maximizes and a later single click keeps it maximized, right press on the title -> menu, `Down` `Return` minimizes, right press on the bottom border -> menu at the pointer, middle press lowers and A under B's title sees no click, a click on an inactive title raises and focuses, title drag moves the frame to 100,100, right border drag configures 500x300, a click in the client still reaches it surface-local, `reload` with `":close"` moves the close button right and reports `shade` as an error, client side -> server side under a 40 px panel keeps the title in the area), full suite, clippy `-D warnings`, headless + ipc 5 times in a row. Manual (`run-native.sh --debug 1280x800` in a private Xvfb :7, hub + panel + dock, foot server side next to a GTK4 Lister client side): PASS active title (dark gradient, light glyphs, bold white title), inactive title (`#DEE0D8`, grey glyphs and title, `#404040` border), hover (blue) and pressed (dark blue) close button, press on close released off it (nothing), iconify (minimized, restored from the tasklist), maximize button (frame fills the usable area under the panel), double click restores and maximizes again, single click on a maximized title keeps it, right click menu at the pointer (Escape closes), middle click lowers foot under the Lister, right border resize (704 -> 902 wide), bottom-left corner resize (right edge kept at 902), close button (foot exits), frame at 50% opacity override (title and border translucent with the window), Lister unchanged (CSD title, folder double click navigates, CSD title drag moves it). NOT verified: the disarm of a pressed title button on host focus loss (added after the manual run; code review only); the resize cursors (not visible in Xvfb screenshots); the inactive opacity of the owner's config (transparency is off there; checked with a 50% override instead); a themerc with solid textures in a real theme; scale other than 1; Qt or other server side clients than foot; rule 4 hotkeys (no hub, ipc or lister change).
- Compositor (stage 3 task 8): wlr-screencopy, so `opusdm-shot` works under the native compositor. New `src/protocols/screencopy.rs`: a hand-written `zwlr_screencopy_manager_v1` version 3 server (Smithay 0.7.0 has no helper). `capture_output` / `capture_output_region` (region clipped to the output; an empty one, or an unknown output, gets `failed` with an error line) create a frame that sends `buffer(Xrgb8888, w, h, w * 4)` then `buffer_done` (v3); no `linux_dmabuf` event; `overlay_cursor` is ignored with a debug line (the cursor is never captured). `copy` / `copy_with_damage` with a shm buffer of exactly that format, size and stride queue a `PendingCapture { frame, buffer, output, region, with_damage }` in `State::screencopy`; any other buffer (wrong size or format, not shm) gets `failed` plus `err_log!`. DEVIATION from the protocol: that case is the `invalid_buffer` protocol error in the spec, which would kill the whole client (`opusdm-shot`); a second `copy` on one frame is still the `already_used` protocol error. `copy_with_damage` does not wait for new damage: it is answered on the next tick with `damage(0, 0, w, h)`. `State::take_pending_captures` / `State::complete_capture(c, Result<pixels>)` (writes the shm buffer row by row, re-checking format, size and pool bounds, then `flags(0)`, `damage` when asked, `ready` with CLOCK_MONOTONIC; `Err` sends `failed` plus `err_log!`). A frame destroyed or a client dead before servicing leaves the queue in `Dispatch::destroyed` (and `take_pending_captures` drops any dead entry) with a `debug_log!`, not a failure. Backends service the queue once per frame tick, never blocking: headless fills `HEADLESS_CAPTURE_PIXEL = [0x20, 0x20, 0x20, 0xff]` (B, G, R, X); nested renders the scene into an offscreen `GlesTexture` (`Offscreen` + `Bind`, `Transform::Normal`, fresh damage tracker, age 0) and reads it back with `ExportMem::copy_framebuffer` + `map_texture`, one render per tick for all pending captures, each cropped to its region. `backend/nested.rs` refactor: `_elements` became `_scene_elements` (switcher, menu, snap preview, OVERLAY / TOP layers, windows with popups, decorations and opacity, BOTTOM / BACKGROUND) used by both the window and the capture, so they cannot drift; the window adds `_cursor_elements` in front, the capture does not. The snap zone preview IS captured (a capture is the screen as drawn, minus the cursor). The per-frame scene inputs (menu images, switcher image, preview, decoration draws) are built once per tick in `SceneParts` and shared. After a capture the next window frame is a full redraw (the GL context left the window surface, buffer age unknown, as already handled). Smithay 0.7.0 notes: GLES reads `Xrgb8888` / `Argb8888` only through `BGRA_EXT`, so the capture texture and read back are `Abgr8888` (RGBA, always readable) and converted to BGRX in `rgba_to_xrgb`; the timestamp comes from `smithay::utils::Clock<Monotonic>`. Verified: TDD RED (5 new headless tests failed on the missing global) then GREEN: whole output 1920x1080 (`buffer` Xrgb8888 stride 7680, `buffer_done`, no dmabuf, `flags(0)`, `ready`, first and last pixel = `HEADLESS_CAPTURE_PIXEL`), `copy_with_damage` reports `damage(0,0,1920,1080)`, region 10,10 100x50 gives 100x50 stride 400, a region across the corner is clipped to 20x20, one outside gives `failed`, a wrong size and a wrong format give `failed` and the next capture works with the client still connected, a client that dies right after `copy` and a frame destroyed right after `copy` leave the compositor running and a new capture works (debug log checked once: both dropped with "destroyed before its copy, dropped"); unit tests for `clip_region`, `crop_xrgb`, `rgba_to_xrgb` and the headless fill. `cargo test -p opusdm-compositor` 5 times in a row all green (193 unit, 144 headless, 30 ipc), `cargo clippy -p opusdm-compositor --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean. Manual check (private Xvfb :7, `run-native.sh --debug 1600x1000`, sandbox D-Bus only, `~/.config/opusdm` sha256 and picom.conf mtime unchanged): PASS `opusdm-shot` run by hand in the sandbox (own config dir, own `dbus-run-session`, save folder in the scratchpad): full screen (right click, Quick Save) saved a 1600x1000 PNG showing the panel, the dock, the desktop icons and a foot window with its server side title bar, right side up and with correct colours; PASS region (drag 1200x700 from the origin, Enter, Quick Save) saved a 1200x700 PNG of that part; PASS the sandbox hotkey Ctrl+Super+Print (the Ctrl+Print of the sandbox table) ran the hub's `exec:opusdm-shot`, the overlay showed the captured screen, cancelled with Escape (nothing saved). NOT CHECKED: a capture while the window menu, the switcher or a snap drag is open; a translucent (inactive opacity) window in a capture; a capture of a host window resized between `capture_output` and `copy` (the copy then fails with an error line, by design, untested); the GTK4 dmabuf path is irrelevant (shm only). `run-native.sh` still says "No screen copy (opusdm-shot)" in its header comment (not in this task's files).
- Compositor (stage 3 task 8, review fix 1): a queued screen copy whose frame is alive but whose `wl_buffer` was destroyed before the copy now gets `failed` plus `err_log!` (rule 3: its client waits for an answer); before it was dropped with only a debug line and the client waited forever. A dead frame (destroyed, or its client died) is still dropped with a `debug_log!` (shared `_still_wanted`, used by `take_pending_captures` and `complete_capture`). New headless-only IPC `debug/screencopy` (`{"hold"?: bool}` -> `{"pending", "hold"}`, refused without `--headless`): it reports the queue length and can hold the servicing (`Screencopy::hold`, read by `take_pending_captures`; never set outside the headless tests), so the tests see an entry wait in the queue and leave it. New headless tests: a buffer destroyed while its copy is held -> `failed` and the error line, queue back to 0, next capture works; a client that creates a frame, never calls `copy` and disconnects -> queue 0, a new client captures; "no leaked frame" asserted on the queue: with servicing held, a queued copy of a client that dies and a queued copy whose frame is destroyed both bring `pending` from 1 to 0. RED shown by temporarily restoring the old behaviour: the buffer test timed out waiting for an answer (the hang of the old code), and with the queue removal in `destroyed` disabled the leak test failed on "a dead client's frame leaves the queue"; both restored. Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-compositor` 193 unit + 147 headless + 30 ipc passed, clippy `-D warnings` clean. NOT CHECKED by hand in the sandbox (no behaviour change for a normal capture; `opusdm-shot` never destroys its buffer before `ready`). Extra file: `src/ipc/protocol.rs` (the debug request, as the review asked).
- Compositor (stage 3 task 9): clipboard, primary selection and drag and drop between clients. New `src/selection.rs`: the `SelectionHandler`, `DataDeviceHandler`, `ClientDndGrabHandler`, `ServerDndGrabHandler` (defaults: the compositor never starts a drag) and new `PrimarySelectionHandler` impls with their delegates (the data device handlers moved here from `state.rs`, where stage 1 had created `DataDeviceState`); `State` gains `primary_selection_state` (`zwp_primary_selection_device_manager_v1`, middle click paste) and `drag: Option<Drag>` (source and icon of the client drag in progress, set by `started`, cleared by `dropped`). Transfers are client to client only: the compositor hands the reader's pipe to the source client and never reads or writes a pipe. No wlr-data-control (GDK does not use it). Selection focus: the one choke point is `SeatHandler::focus_changed` (Smithay calls it on every keyboard focus change to a surface: `focus_window`, `focus_layer`, popup grabs, the refocus paths), which calls `State::set_selection_focus` (clipboard and primary); Smithay 0.7 does not call it when the keyboard goes to nothing, so `focus_window` calls `State::update_selection_focus` after `set_focus` (it reads the keyboard focus; it cannot run inside `focus_changed`, where the keyboard is locked). Drag icon: drawn by the nested backend with the cursor elements (`_drag_icon_elements`, below the cursor, at the pointer plus the icon's `wl_surface` offset tracked by `selection::handle_commit`), so like the cursor it is not in a screen capture; the icon gets frame callbacks in `send_frames`. Dead drags: `State::cancel_dead_drag` (called by `refresh` and at the top of `pointer_motion` / `pointer_button`) ends a drag whose `wl_data_source` is gone while its origin surface lives (a motion onto nothing sends `leave` to the target and deactivates its offer, then the grab is unset: no `drop`). A drag whose source CLIENT is killed is ended by Smithay itself: its pointer grab drops a grab whose origin surface is dead on the next pointer event, with a `drop` to the target first when it had accepted (then `leave`); the target's `receive` reads end of file at once (the source is dead), nothing hangs. wayland-backend marks every object of a killed client dead before any destructor runs, so no hook can cancel earlier (Smithay 0.7 limit, as the destroyed decoration object of task 6). The same holds for an origin window closed by a live client: the grab ends on the next pointer motion (`cancelled` to the source when the target had not accepted). Verified: new headless tests (RED first: no primary global, no icon frame callbacks, no `leave` after the source died; the two clipboard tests and the layer test passed already on the base, the stage 1 data device): clipboard to the client that takes the keyboard (and none before), primary the same, a keyboard layer gets the clipboard offer, a dead clipboard / primary source (stale offer reads end of file at once; focus away and back: clipboard cleared), drag from A drops "hello stage3" on B (icon frame callbacks, `dnd_finished` to the source), source client killed mid-drag (B gets `leave`, receive reads EOF, pointer free, compositor alive), killed before an accept (leave, no drop), source destroyed by a live client (leave, no drop, pointer free), origin window closed (leave and `cancelled` on the next motion); `cargo test -p opusdm-compositor` 194 unit + 157 headless + 30 ipc passed, 5 loops of headless + ipc all green, clippy clean. Manual, `run-native.sh --debug 1600x1000` on a private Xvfb :7 (sandbox bus, private config): Lister path bar Ctrl+A Ctrl+C, Ctrl+Shift+V in foot PASS (the path appears in foot); middle click paste of a double-clicked word from one foot into another PASS; drag of a scratch file (temp dir) from one Lister onto another PASS (the dragged file icon is drawn at the pointer, Lister log `op=Copy`, `JobDone ... copied: 1`, file identical); `opusdm-shot` editor Copy, then Paste in `gtk4-demo` Clipboard demo PASS (shows "Image" with the screenshot thumbnail); `opusdm-shot` killed with the image on the clipboard, Paste again: the demo's Paste button goes insensitive, nothing hangs PASS. `~/.config/opusdm` sha256 and picom.conf mtime unchanged. NOT CHECKED: a real GTK target when the source client is killed mid-drag (the headless test covers the protocol side); DnD between different apps other than two Listers; a drag icon with a non-zero `wl_surface` offset by eye. Extra file: `src/shell/mod.rs` (one line: the commit hook for the icon offset).
- Compositor (stage 3 task 9, review fix 1): deviation from the brief, accepted by the controller as a Smithay 0.7.0 limit: a drag whose source CLIENT is killed is NOT cancelled. Its origin surface is dead as well, so the first pointer event after the death (normally the motion `cancel_dead_drag` sends from `refresh`, not a later user event as the task 9 entry said) makes `PointerInner::with_grab` discard the grab through `DnDGrab::drop`; a target that had accepted gets `drop`, then `leave`, and its `receive` reads end of file at once. `ClientDndGrabHandler::dropped` now logs that case with `err_log!` ("the source client died, Smithay dropped on target ... (its receive reads nothing)") so the log no longer shows a plain "validated true" end. The headless test pins the exact sequence: the target's DnD events end with `drop`, `leave` (one drop, leave last) and the receive reads EOF. `selection.rs` docs corrected. Verified: DnD / selection headless tests 18 passed, `cargo test -p opusdm-compositor` headless + ipc passed, clippy clean. NOT CHECKED: how a real GTK target (the Lister) handles that empty drop; no manual run for this round (log and test changes only).
- Compositor (stage 3 task 10): strict xdg-activation (owner decision 2026-11-25). New `State::last_input: HashMap<ClientId, Serial>`: the serial of the last key press, keyboard enter or pointer button press sent to each client (`note_client_input`; key presses forwarded by `keyboard_key`, button presses in `_send_button` unless our move / resize grab takes them, so server side frame clicks and swallowed hotkeys do not count; enters through `SeatHandler::focus_changed` with `KeyboardHandle::last_enter`); the entry of a disconnected client is dropped by the next `refresh` (`prune_last_input`). `token_created` now takes the policy verdict and keeps it in the token's user data: a token is honoured only when it carries a serial, its client has a `last_input` entry, holds the keyboard or the pointer at creation, and the serial is not older than that entry. `request_activation` (after the stage 2 expiry and non-window checks, unchanged) refuses the others with a `debug_log!`: a mapped window gets the new `WindowInfo.urgent` (no focus change, no desktop switch; not set on the window that is already active), an unmapped one is left alone (S3-R2: it gets the focus when it maps). `urgent` is cleared in `remember_active` (every path that makes a window active) and shown as `"urgent"` in the IPC `WindowView` (`windows/list`, `window-added` / `window-changed`). Interpretations: releases (key and button) do not update `last_input`, since GTK sends the press serial of a click that acts on release; the event sent last wins over the highest serial, since a click on an unfocused window sends its enter (newer serial) before its press. Stage 2 tests changed: `activation_restores_a_window_on_another_desktop` (the token maker now holds the keyboard through a second window) and `an_activation_while_another_window_has_a_menu_open_moves_the_keyboard` (the token now comes from the client holding the keyboard, handed to B); none of the stage 2 tests used a serial-less token (the harness always sent the enter serial). Extra files outside the brief list: `state.rs` (fields), `switcher/mod.rs` (`remember_active`), `ipc/protocol.rs` (`WindowView` lives there), `protocols/foreign_toplevel.rs` and `switcher/model.rs` (test constructors). Verified: 6 new headless tests and 1 unit test (RED before the change, GREEN after), `cargo test -p opusdm-compositor` all passed, headless + ipc 5 times in a row, clippy clean. NOT verified: no manual sandbox run (none required by the brief; Task 13 covers the notification click); no real GTK client exercised; the panel notification-center click (token made by `opusdm-notify`, which holds neither keyboard nor pointer) is expected to be refused and only mark the window urgent; the Lister Sidecar refocus (`present()` after the viewer took the keyboard) and a hub bank / group window re-shown without hub input are expected to be refused unless the pointer is on that client; the panel tasklist does not show `urgent` yet.
- Client (stage 3 task 11): `opusdm_wayland::native` gains `reload() -> Result<Vec<String>, String>` (the `errors` of the compositor's `reload`), `builtin_bindings() -> Result<Vec<(String, String)>, String>` (`bindings/list`, `(name, key)`, an entry without a name or a key is an error), `set_opacity(id, Option<u8>)` (`window/set-opacity`, `None` sends `null`) and `set_geometry(id, Rect)` (`window/set-geometry`); `NativeWindow` gains `opacity: u8` and `urgent: bool` (missing keys parse as 100 / false, so an older compositor still works). Hub: `compositor::apply_native(ds)` (new, in `crates/hub/src/compositor.rs`) is what the desktop settings do under opusdm-compositor instead of the picom path (`compositor::apply` is untouched and never runs there, so no `picom.conf` is written): it waits (at most 1 s, 20 ms steps) until `desktop.json` holds the new settings (the hub saves it on its persist thread while the GTK event is sent at once, so a bare `reload` could read the old file; a file that never matches is logged with `err_log!` and the reload is asked anyway), then calls `native::reload()` and logs every returned error and a failed request with `err_log!`. `toolbar.rs::apply_desktop_settings`: when `under_native()` (the hub's cached value) one `reload_native_compositor` on the existing IoWorker (never the GTK or owner thread) replaces the picom call AND the `desktops/set-count` call when opacity, snap, wrap or the desktop count changed (`reload` applies the count from `desktop.json`); a change of `wrap_desktops` alone, which `desktop_diff` does not track, is checked in that function only under native (off native the early return is unchanged). Off native: the same calls in the same order as before (`apply_virtual_screens`, `apply_compositor`, `snap::set_enabled`); `desktop_diff.rs` is not touched. Startup is unchanged (the compositor reads `desktop.json` itself). Ownership (rule 2): no new long-lived object; the job owns a clone of the settings and runs once on the IoWorker thread that already owns the compositor requests; if the compositor is gone the request fails after at most 2 s and is logged. Verified: new tests (RED before the implementation: the opacity / urgent parse test failed with `(0, false)` against `(100, false)`, the other native tests were written with the code and pass): `native.rs` fake-server tests for `reload` and `bindings/list` parsing, the opacity / urgent default, the `set-opacity` / `set-geometry` wire shapes; `compositor.rs` tests for the save wait and the reload request (closures stand in for `desktop.json` and the socket); `cargo test -p opusdm-wayland -p opusdm-hub` all pass, clippy 14 warnings before and after. By hand (rule 4) in run-native.sh (private Xvfb, sandbox config copy): Settings > Windows, transparency on and inactive 30, Apply: the inactive Lister turned see-through at once (compositor log `reload: ... inactive_opacity: 30 ... 0 errors`), the private `picom.conf` unchanged; Lister path bar Enter, double click on a folder, Backspace, Alt+Left / Alt+Right, sandbox hotkeys (desktop next / prev, run, terminal, shot overlay), a second Lister with SOURCE / DEST, a Copy job (toolbar button), all as expected. run-x11.sh (Xephyr): Settings Apply saved `desktop.json`, Lister checks, Alt+1 / Alt+2, Ctrl+Shift+space all as expected. run-wayland.sh (labwc): Settings Apply saved `desktop.json` and the hub stayed up, Lister double click / Backspace / Alt+Left / Alt+Right as expected. NOT verified: that Settings opacity still rewrites `picom.conf` and signals picom on X11 (the sandboxes start the hub without `OPUSDM_PICOM_BASE`, by design, so that path never runs there; running it would signal the owner's live picom); a Copy job that succeeds on X11 (only a permission error against `/usr/share` was reached); F-keys (F8 / F7 in native did nothing visible, the known xdotool artifact); Ctrl+backslash on X11 (no terminal, known artifact); text typed in the path bar under labwc (xdotool text keys do not reach it in that nested session, Backspace / Alt keys do; the Lister is untouched by this change; not compared with the base commit); a successful `set-geometry` / `bindings/list` call against a real compositor (the hub does not call them yet: they are the client API for later tasks); wrap and snap changes by hand; the hub on the owner's live session.
- Final fix wave manual re-check, on head `06fc355` (release built from a clean tree; `./run-native.sh --debug 1280x800` on a private Xvfb :7, sandbox bus and sockets only, screenshots `final-NN-*.png`): a Lister context menu open, then the terminal hotkey Ctrl+Super+backslash: the terminal maps, the log shows "focus: ... ends the popup grab rooted at" the Lister, the menu closes, text typed reaches the terminal (`echo final-wave-ok` ran) and `windows/list` shows only the terminal activated PASS; a Lister context menu open, then the Run hotkey Ctrl+Shift+Super+space: the Run layer ends the grab and takes the typed text, the Lister stays the active window PASS; Lister Enter in the path bar, double click on a folder, Backspace PASS; window menu by keyboard (Super+Alt+space, Down, Return) Minimize PASS; tasklist restore / minimize / restore of the Lister PASS. `~/.config/opusdm` checksums and `picom.conf` mtime unchanged; no process or temp dir left.
  - NOT verified by hand: item 2 (tasklist activate while the Run window is open), item 3 (no client half-closes the IPC in the session), item 4 (the sandbox writes its own compositor.json), an xdg-activation (notification click) while a menu is open (covered only by the headless test); the hotkeys were sent after `xdotool windowfocus` on the winit window (without it Xvfb gave the keys to no window, as noted in task 11).
  - Seen, unchanged behaviour: after the tasklist minimizes the active Lister, the panel keeps the keyboard and the terminal below is shown but not activated (as `a_window_minimized_while_the_panel_has_the_keyboard_is_no_longer_active` expects).
- Compositor (final fix wave, item 4): `compositor.json` is parsed key by key. A key whose value has the wrong type (for example `"quit_enabled": "false"`) is logged with `err_log!` naming the key and takes its default, the other keys still apply; before, one bad key reset the whole file to the defaults. Unknown keys keep their `err_log!`; a file that is not JSON or not an object still gives the defaults with `err_log!`. Note: a bad `quit_enabled` still means the quit binding is on (its default), now with a log line that names the key. Unit tests in `config.rs` (the old assertion that a bad type fails the whole file is replaced). Not exercised by hand (the sandbox uses its own private config).
- Compositor (final fix wave, item 3): an IPC client that half-closes (`shutdown(Write)`) after its requests gets every reply. Before, on EOF the connection was removed right after its replies were queued, so the replies past the socket buffer were lost (18056 of 100000 arrived in the new test). Now at EOF the read source goes (nothing more is read) and a connection with replies pending is marked `eof`; `_flush` removes it (shutdown, hub forgotten, as before) once they are written; a write error still drops it with `err_log!`; `IPC_MAX_PENDING` still bounds a half-closed watcher that never reads. A connection with nothing pending at EOF goes at once, as before. A half-closed peer that stays alive and never reads keeps its connection (and one of the 64 slots) until it exits or its pending exceeds the limit; no timeout was added. Tests: ipc `a_client_that_half_closes_after_its_request_gets_the_reply`, `a_client_that_half_closes_gets_every_reply_even_past_the_socket_buffer` (also checks the fds go back), three unit tests in `ipc/mod.rs`; the fd-leak regression test stays green.
- Compositor (final fix wave, item 2): `activate_window` (tasklist, `window/activate`, xdg-activation) under an exclusive keyboard layer now makes the window the active one (`Activated`, `windows/list`, foreign toplevel) while the layer keeps the keyboard, as a desktop switch already did (shared helper `_set_active_under_layer`, the desktop switch log lines keep their text). A window that only maps under the layer is still not activated (unchanged). Headless test `activating_a_window_under_an_exclusive_layer_makes_it_active` (RED before: B stayed active). Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-compositor`, clippy. Not exercised by hand (no manual sequence activates a window from the tasklist while the Run window is open).
- Compositor (final fix wave, item 1): focusing a window while another window's menu (a client popup with a grab) is open no longer splits the keyboard from the active window. Smithay 0.7's popup keyboard grab ignores focus changes and gives the keyboard back to its root when it ends, while `focus_window` had already made the new window active. `focus_window` (non-exclusive branch: a new window maps, xdg-activation, tasklist activate, click) now ends a popup grab rooted at another surface first (`dismiss_popups_and_grabs`: the menu gets `popup_done`), the same remedy as tasks 6 and 8; a grab rooted at the target itself (its own menu) is left alone; the window menu (not a Smithay grab) and exclusive layers are unchanged. Headless tests: a window mapped while A's menu is open, an xdg-activation of B while A's menu is open (keys reach B, `windows/list` shows B active, the menu is dismissed), activating the window that owns the menu keeps it. - Fix (sandbox, task 13 review): `run-native.sh` copies the configuration with `cp -RL` (follows symlinks) instead of `cp -a`, which copied a symlinked `~/.config/opusdm` as a symlink and kept symlinked files inside it as links: the hotkey rewrite and the hub would have written the live files through them. - Fix (compositor stage 2 task 13 exit check): the first click after a menu closed went where the menu had been. Root cause: Smithay 0.7's `PopupPointerGrab::motion`, once the popup grab has ended (the menu is gone and `PopupManager::cleanup` ran in the housekeeping), only calls `unset_grab` and drops that motion; the compositor's pointer location stayed stale, so the next press was hit-tested and sent at the old position (the tasklist menu case: the first pager click landed at the menu item's position in panel coordinates and was lost; with a real mouse the next motion event usually hides it, xdotool sends one). `State::pointer_motion` now sends a motion again when it ended a grab; the second motion also corrects the pointer enter that Smithay's `unset_grab` sent with the stale coordinates, in the same frame. Our move / resize grabs also end on a motion (their window died or was unmapped), but they forward the motion first: there the re-send repeats one `wl_pointer.motion` with the same coordinates in the same frame (harmless); `debug_log!` "pointer grab ended by a motion: motion sent again".
  - Evidence: a temporary trace (removed, not committed) in `pointer_motion` / `pointer_button` in the native sandbox: the motion to the pager arrived with `grabbed=true`, the press after it reported the old location (365,121) and `under=` a window, `grabbed=false`. The same sequence (tasklist menu item, then one pager click) switched desktop on the first click under labwc (2/2) and X11 (1/1), so it was compositor-side.
  - Verified: headless test `the_first_click_after_a_menu_closed_goes_where_the_pointer_is` (the harness clients now bind `wl_pointer` and record each press with its surface-local position, `TestClient::pointer_presses`): failed first with the press at x 1050 (the menu) instead of 50; passes now. Compositor suite 109 unit / 80 headless / 15 ipc (+1 ignored), headless + ipc 5 times green, clippy clean. Native sandbox with the fix: one pager click after Move to desktop, after Force kill and after Close Window switched desktop each time (3/3; log "motion sent again").
  - NOT verified: a real mouse on the nested window (only xdotool); the move / resize grab ending on a motion because its window died or was unmapped (the repeated motion there is by review only).
- Fix (compositor stage 2 task 13 exit check, controller ruling): an xdg-activation request for a layer surface or an xdg popup is a debug trace (`activation of ... ignored: a zwlr_layer_surface_v1 is not a window`), not an error line. GTK asks an activation for every surface it shows, so the panel, the desktop, the dock, every notification popup and the Run window wrote `activation ... refused: not a toplevel` with `err_log!` (12 lines in the exit check run). Any other surface that is not a known window stays an error (`refused: not a toplevel (role ...)`), as do unknown / expired tokens. New `_is_known_non_window(role)` in `src/activation.rs`; the harness `request_activation` takes any surface.
  - Verified: unit test `layer_surfaces_and_popups_are_known_non_windows` (did not build first), headless `activating_a_layer_surface_is_not_an_error` (failed first: 2 error lines instead of 1) and `activating_a_layer_surface_is_a_debug_trace` (failed first); compositor suite 109 unit / 79 headless / 15 ipc (+1 ignored), clippy clean. The popup role is covered by the unit test only.
- Fix (compositor stage 2 task 13 exit check, controller ruling): the native tasklist keeps the map order. `wl_windows::native_events` sorts the clients by window id (ids are never reused, so this is the map order, as X11's `_NET_CLIENT_LIST`); the compositor's `windows/list` stays in stacking order. The pager draws its mini-windows from the same client list, so under native they are now drawn in map order too, as on X11. Before, the buttons followed the stacking order and moved on every activation, so a second click on the same spot hit another window. Only the native path changes (`native_events` runs only under opusdm-compositor).
  - Verified: new unit test `native_clients_keep_the_map_order_whatever_the_stacking` (failed first: `[2, 1]` vs `[1, 2]`), panel suite 200 passed, clippy `--no-deps` clean. Sandbox re-check: see the task 13 fix round entry.
