Verified: three later sandbox runs (Xephyr :7, the same kind of session: Listers, drags, resizes, notifications, menus, terminal drawer, fullscreen client) with 0 BAD_SURFACE lines (`task-8-compositor-run3.log`, `-run4.log`, `task-8-compositor.log`); `cargo test -p opusdm-compositor` and clippy clean. NOT verified: on a hardware GPU (DRI3) path; that the age is really reused (no counter, only the absence of the error); no unit test possible (needs an EGL context).
- Fix (compositor, stage 1, task 8 check): a GTK layer window that hid killed its client. GTK hides a layer surface by destroying the `zwlr_layer_surface_v1` and then committing a null buffer on the same `wl_surface`; Smithay 0.7 keeps its layer size check as a pre-commit hook on the `wl_surface` forever and resets the state to size 0 / no anchor on destroy, so that commit failed with `invalid_size` ("width 0 requested without setting left and right anchors") and opusdm-notify died when its first notification went away (found in the `run-native.sh` sandbox). Now `src/shell/layer.rs` has its own `Dispatch<ZwlrLayerSurfaceV1>` (instead of `delegate_layer_shell!`) that forwards every request and the destroy to Smithay and then anchors the role-less pending state to every edge (the check passes, nothing reads it); `new_layer_surface` puts the default state back (with the requested layer) when the `wl_surface` gets a layer role again. No ownership change. New integration test `a_layer_surface_hidden_by_destroying_its_role_keeps_the_client_alive` (failed before the fix with a broken pipe). Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-compositor` (30 unit + 15 integration) and clippy clean; in the sandbox (Xephyr :7) two `notify-send` notifications in a row, the second after the first had hidden, opusdm-notify kept running (`task-8-11-notification.png`, `task-8-12-notification-again.png`). NOT verified: a client that re-creates a layer surface on the very same `wl_surface` (GTK used new surfaces in the run; not covered by a test).
- Feature (compositor, stage 1, task 7): nested backend with GLES rendering and real input (`src/backend/nested.rs`). `winit::init_from_attributes::<GlesRenderer>` opens the host window `OpusDM compositor (nested)` with the physical inner size `--size`; one output `NESTED-1` (make OpusDM, model Nested) whose mode follows the window: `WinitEvent::Resized` changes the mode, arranges the layer map again and calls the new `State::output_area_changed` (maximized windows follow the usable area through `usable_area_changed`, fullscreen windows get the new output size; the shared reconfigure is the new private `refill_window`, `usable_area_changed` keeps its behaviour). Shared setup factored into `backend/mod.rs`: `setup_common(output)` (event loop, display, State, SIGTERM / SIGINT flag, keyboard and pointer, socket), `new_output`, `announce` (prints `WAYLAND_DISPLAY=...`, starts `--startup`), `stop_on_quit`, `housekeeping` (reap, refresh, flush); the headless backend now uses them with the same order and behaviour (only the debug trace "quit on signal" became "quit requested, stopping the loop"). Rendering: a 16 ms calloop timer owns `Frame` (the `WinitGraphicsBackend`, an `OutputDamageTracker` rebuilt when the window size changes, the host cursor state, an error throttle); each tick answers the queued dmabuf imports, renders, sends frame callbacks (and the cursor surface's) and does the housekeeping. Elements front to back: client cursor surface, OVERLAY, the topmost window when fullscreen, TOP, windows topmost first, BOTTOM, BACKGROUND; popups come with their parent (Smithay's `Window` / `LayerSurface` render elements), layer origins as in the hit test (`layer_geometry` minus the bbox offset), so render and hit order match. Cursor: `SeatHandler::cursor_image` stores `State::cursor_status`; a client surface is drawn at the pointer minus its hotspot (host cursor hidden), `Named(icon)` sets the winit window cursor, `Hidden` hides it, a dead surface goes back to the default. Input: the winit events go to `keyboard_key`, `pointer_motion` (position scaled to the output size), `pointer_button`, `pointer_axis` (frame built as anvil); `WinitEvent::Focus(false)` releases every pressed button (an Alt + drag grab ends) and every pressed key (no endless autorepeat), each with a `debug_log!`; `CloseRequested` sets the quit flag, the loop ends like on SIGTERM (exit code 0). A failed `bind`, `render_output` or `submit` is logged with `err_log!` at most once per second per kind (`ErrorThrottle`, 3 unit tests) and the loop goes on. `linux-dmabuf`: v4 with a default feedback on the render node of the EGL display (from `render_device_path`, else `drm_device_path`, `dev_t` with `stat`) and the renderer's dmabuf formats; imports are queued in `State::pending_dmabufs` and checked by the renderer on the next frame (the renderer is owned by the frame timer). Removed the now useless `#[allow(dead_code)]` on the input entry points. API differences from the plan: `winit::init_from_attributes` instead of `winit::init` (title and size); the damage tracker is `OutputDamageTracker::new(size, 1.0, Flipped180)` (the GL framebuffer is upside down, smallvil / anvil set `Flipped180` on the output instead; here the output advertised to clients stays `Normal`); `EGLDevice::try_get_render_node` needs the `backend_drm` feature, so the render node path is read with `render_device_path` instead and no Smithay feature was added; winit 0.30 in Smithay 0.7 does not forward `CursorLeft`, so "pointer leaves the host window" cannot be seen (on X11 the implicit grab still delivers the button release outside the window); the buffer age is asked only after a bind and after the first submit (before that Mesa answers EGL `BAD_SURFACE`, logged by Smithay as ERROR). Bug found and fixed during the manual run: with Xephyr (software llvmpipe, no DRI3) gtk4-demo handed dmabufs, Mesa printed "dmabuf import failed to mmap: Operation not permitted" and the llvmpipe threads of the compositor segfaulted (kernel log), killing the session; now no `linux-dmabuf` global is offered when the EGL device is software (`EGLDevice::is_software`) or has no render node, logged once with `err_log!` ("linux-dmabuf not offered ...: clients fall back to shared memory"). Apps tried (Xephyr :7, 1400x900, never the live :0): foot, gtk4-demo. Manual checks: startup app shows (foot, gtk4-demo) PASS; typing reaches foot and gtk4-demo search PASS; client cursor surface drawn PASS; Alt + left drag move PASS; Alt + right drag resize (bottom-right) PASS; title bar drag (foot CSD, GTK header bar) PASS; maximize / restore (foot and GTK buttons) PASS; GTK menu opens under its button and a click outside closes it PASS; host window resize changes the output and a maximized window follows it (1280x800 -> 1100x700 -> 1280x800) PASS; host focus lost during an Alt drag releases the button (grab ends) and Alt PASS; host window close (WM_DELETE_WINDOW) ends with exit code 0 and no ERROR line PASS; SIGTERM ends with exit code 0 PASS; screenshots and logs in `.superpowers/sdd/2026-11-25-compositor-stage1/task-7-*`. NOT verified: the GL path with real dmabufs (Xephyr has no hardware EGL device, so `linux-dmabuf` v4 and GTK4's GL renderer on dmabufs never ran), the render order of layer surfaces and a fullscreen window above TOP (no layer-shell client and no fullscreen toggle were run), layer popups, named cursors other than the default and the `Hidden` status, scrolling, a resize from the top / left edges, the error throttle on a real frame failure, high-DPI host scale. Known limits: the keymap is the xkb default (`us`, or `XKB_DEFAULT_*`), not the host layout (in Xephyr with the owner's layout `-` arrived as `/`); the hover state of a client is not updated when a window moves under a still pointer (next motion fixes it); if winit's own loop exits the event source is removed by Smithay without a log. Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-compositor` (30 unit + 14 integration) passes, `cargo clippy -p opusdm-compositor --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, the headless integration tests passed 5 runs in a row. Rule 4 Lister / hub / hotkeys checks do not apply (only `crates/compositor` changed).
- Fix (compositor, stage 1, task 6 review): a fast top / left resize could leave the right / bottom edge off by the last delta: the track went `Idle` on the first commit after the release, usually still an older size, and the real final-size commit was never placed (smallvil has the same flaw). Now, as anvil (`WaitingForFinalAck` -> `WaitingForCommit`), `ResizeGrab::unset` sends the final configure with `send_configure` and stores its serial (`ResizeTrack::WaitingFinalAck`); the new `XdgShellHandler::ack_configure` moves the track to `WaitingFinalCommit` when that serial (or a later one) is acked (`grabs::handle_ack`); that commit is placed and the track goes `Idle`. Commits before the ack are still placed. A client that never acks cannot keep the track: after `RESIZE_ACK_COMMIT_LIMIT` (30) commits without the ack it goes `Idle` with a `debug_log!`; a dead client loses its `windows` entry. The decision is the pure `resize_track_on_commit` / `resize_track_on_ack`. Rule 3: a move or resize grab whose window is alive but no longer mapped (null-buffer unmap) now ends on the next motion with a `debug_log!` (it moved / configured nothing, silently); `ResizeGrab::unset` on an unmapped window only clears the pending `Resizing` state (no configure to an unmapped toplevel) and resets the track, logged; `grabs::handle_commit` resets a track whose window is not mapped, logged; `start_move_grab` refuses with `err_log!` when the toplevel has no `WindowInfo` (it used `unwrap_or_default` silently). Tests (in `grabs`): `commits_before_the_final_ack_are_placed_and_keep_waiting`, `an_older_ack_does_not_end_the_wait`, `the_final_ack_then_its_commit_end_the_resize`, `a_later_ack_also_ends_the_wait`, `a_client_that_never_acks_cannot_keep_the_track`, `commits_while_resizing_are_placed_and_idle_is_left_alone`; a mutation (`acked >= serial` replaced by `true`) makes `an_older_ack_does_not_end_the_wait` fail. Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-compositor` (27 unit + 14 integration) passes, clippy `-D warnings` clean, the headless integration tests passed 5 runs in a row. NOT verified: no resize ran at all (no input in the headless backend): the ack / commit order with a real GTK client, the give-up path, the unmapped-window grab end; checked in task 7 / 8.
- Feature (compositor, stage 1, task 6): pointer, keyboard, focus and move / resize grabs. New `src/input.rs` (backend independent, called by the nested backend in task 7): `State::surface_under` hit-tests in `HIT_ORDER` = OVERLAY, the topmost window when it is fullscreen (above TOP), TOP, windows topmost first (`Space::element_under`), BOTTOM, BACKGROUND, with `WindowSurfaceType::ALL` so popups and subsurfaces are hit before their parent (as anvil; the layer surface origin is computed from `layer_geometry` minus the bbox offset, as `root_position`); `pointer_motion` (focus = the surface under the pointer, an active grab decides instead), `pointer_button` (a press with no active grab focuses: a window is raised and gets the keyboard through `focus_window`, a layer surface with `OnDemand` or `Exclusive` keyboard interactivity (the BACKGROUND desktop too) gets it through `focus_layer` unless another exclusive keyboard layer is mapped, which keeps it; a layer with no keyboard interactivity or an empty spot leaves the focus as it is, with a `debug_log!`; with Alt held (`keyboard.modifier_state().alt`) on a window, `BTN_LEFT` starts a `MoveGrab` and `BTN_RIGHT` a `ResizeGrab` on `edges_for_point`, the quadrant of the window the pointer is in; the grab is set before the press is delivered, with `Focus::Clear`, so the press and its release are consumed and never reach the client), `pointer_axis`, `keyboard_key` (forwarded to the keyboard focus, no compositor bindings). New `src/grabs.rs`: `MoveGrab { start_data, window, initial_loc }` (moves with `move_window`, the stacking order is kept), `ResizeGrab { start_data, window, edges, initial }` (plus a private `last_size`: each motion computes `resize_rect(initial, edges, dx, dy, MIN_WINDOW_SIZE)` and sends the size with the `Resizing` state; `unset` sends the last size without `Resizing`), both end when the button that started them is released; `State::start_move_grab` / `start_resize_grab`; a maximized or fullscreen window that is moved is first restored to its `WindowInfo::restore` size (states cleared with a configure, `restored_origin` keeps it under the pointer at the same relative position); a resized one leaves the state at its current geometry and is resized from there (choice: the brief only specified the move). New `WindowInfo::resize: ResizeTrack` and `grabs::handle_commit` (called by the commit handler): while resizing by the top or left edge, and until the final configure is acked and committed (see the task 6 review fix above), the window is placed so the opposite edge stays where it was, with the size the client committed (smallvil's `ResizeSurfaceState`). xdg: `move_request` / `resize_request` start the same grabs only when the pointer has an active grab with the request serial whose focus is the toplevel or one of its subsurfaces (smallvil's `check_grab`, with the root surface instead of "same client"); every refusal is logged with `err_log!`. The scoped `#[allow(dead_code)]` on `Edges`, `resize_rect`, `MIN_WINDOW_SIZE` is gone (now used); the new input entry points carry one until task 7. Seat focus types unchanged (`WlSurface`). Tests: `the_resize_edges_follow_the_quadrant_of_the_pointer` (the brief's, failed first with "cannot find function `edges_for_point`"), `the_other_two_quadrants_pick_the_mixed_corners`, `a_resize_by_the_top_left_keeps_the_bottom_right_corner`, `a_resize_by_the_bottom_right_keeps_the_origin`, `a_restored_window_stays_under_the_pointer`, `restoring_from_an_empty_geometry_puts_the_origin_at_the_pointer`, and the hit order as a pure function (`first_hit` over `HIT_ORDER`: 5 tests). API differences from the plan: none in names; the `PointerGrab` trait of 0.7.0 also needs `relative_motion`, `frame` and the gesture methods (forwarded), `start_data` and `unset`; a release that ends a grab is not forwarded (smallvil forwards it, but the focus is already cleared, so nothing reached the client either way). No extra Smithay feature was needed. Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-compositor` (21 unit + 14 integration) passes, `cargo clippy -p opusdm-compositor --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, the headless integration tests passed 5 runs in a row. NOT verified: none of the pointer or keyboard paths ran (the headless backend has no input; they are exercised from task 7 / checked by hand in task 8): click focus, Alt + drag move and resize, the consumed press / release, GTK title bar and border drags (`move_request` / `resize_request`), restore of a maximized window on a drag, the top / left resize placement on commit, key delivery, scrolling; the pointer position is not clamped to the output (the backend gives output coordinates); the fullscreen window is above TOP only when it is the topmost window (the render order of task 7 must match); the rule 4 checks do not apply (Lister, hub, ipc, panel untouched).
- Feature (compositor, stage 1, task 5): layer shell, exclusive zones, maximize and fullscreen. New `src/shell/layer.rs`: `WlrLayerShellHandler` (`new_layer_surface` maps the surface in `layer_map_for_output(&output)`, a requested output is ignored with a `debug_log!` since there is one output; `layer_destroyed` unmaps it; `new_popup` unconstrains a popup adopted with `zwlr_layer_surface_v1.get_popup`) and `layer::handle_commit` (called by the commit handler: arranges the map on every commit of a layer surface, sends the initial configure on the first one, as anvil). `State`: `layer_shell_state`, `exclusive_layer` (the mapped TOP / OVERLAY layer with `KeyboardInteractivity::Exclusive`: it takes the keyboard when it commits a buffer, `focus_topmost` and `focus_window` leave the keyboard on it while it is mapped (a window mapped meanwhile is raised and not activated), and when it commits a null buffer or is destroyed the keyboard goes back to the topmost window; transitions traced with `debug_log!`), `usable_area()` = output geometry + `non_exclusive_zone()`, new `output_area()`, `focus_layer`, `move_window` (moves a window without changing the stacking order, `map_element` would raise it), layer surfaces get frame callbacks in `send_frames`, `refresh` also cleans the layer map and drops a dead exclusive layer. `WindowInfo` gains `maximized` and `fullscreen` (fullscreen = "above the TOP layer", state only: the render and hit-test order come in tasks 6-7). xdg: `maximize_request` / `fullscreen_request` store `restore` (the normal geometry, only when leaving the normal state), set the xdg state, configure the usable area / whole output size and move the window to its origin; `unmaximize_request` / `unfullscreen_request` fall back to the other fill state when it is still on, else restore the stored geometry with its size sent explicitly (so the client goes back to 400x300, not to a size of its choice); a window maximized or fullscreen before its first buffer maps at the area origin; every request is answered with a configure. When the usable area changes (layer commit, destroy, cleanup) every maximized, not fullscreen, window is reconfigured to the new area. `State::root_position` now also finds layer surface roots (layer map geometry minus the surface tree bbox offset), so layer popups are unconstrained and grabbable. Fixes from the task 4 review: the early grab refusals (unknown seat, dead root, root not mapped) now send `popup_done` as the protocol asks, and the refusal is logged once (by the caller, `root_position` no longer logs). Harness: `TestClient::map_layer`, `layer_configure`, `destroy_layer`, `keyboard_focus` (raw `wl_seat` / `wl_keyboard` dispatch, enter / leave only, no xkbcommon), `TestLayer` (`HasSurface`, `PopupParent` with the new `adopt_popup` hook used by `map_popup`), `Anchor` / `Layer` / `KeyboardInteractivity` re-exported from SCTK, the xdg positioner anchor renamed `PositionerAnchor` in the harness. The client keeps every layer surface alive for its lifetime: the brief's tests drop the returned `TestLayer` of the panel at once (`c.map_layer(..);`), which destroyed the panel before the maximize. Tests: the 5 of the brief (`a_top_panel_spans_the_output_width`, `a_maximized_window_leaves_room_for_an_exclusive_zone`, `a_fullscreen_window_covers_the_whole_output`, `an_exclusive_keyboard_layer_gets_the_keyboard`, `a_popup_at_the_bottom_right_corner_stays_inside_the_output`) plus 3 wiring regressions (`the_keyboard_goes_back_to_the_window_when_the_exclusive_layer_goes`, `a_window_going_away_does_not_steal_the_keyboard_from_an_exclusive_layer`, `a_maximized_window_grows_when_the_panel_goes_away`). API differences from the plan: none in names; the popup of a layer surface reaches the compositor as an xdg popup with no parent (`XdgShellHandler::new_popup`, only tracked) and gets its parent later through `WlrLayerShellHandler::new_popup`, where it is unconstrained; Smithay has no public `layer_state`, so the layer origin is computed from `layer_geometry` and `bbox`. No extra Smithay feature was needed. Verified: the new tests failed first (no `zwlr_layer_shell_v1` global; then, with the implementation in place, the maximize test got 1920x1080 because the panel was destroyed, fixed in the harness); `cargo test -p opusdm-compositor` (10 unit + 14 integration) passes, `cargo clippy -p opusdm-compositor --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, the integration tests passed 5 runs in a row; a mutation (the exclusive layer check removed from `focus_window` / `focus_topmost`) makes `a_window_going_away_does_not_steal_the_keyboard_from_an_exclusive_layer` fail. NOT verified: no real panel, dock or Run window (no real layer-shell client was run), nothing is rendered (headless), fullscreen above the TOP layer is only a flag, `OnDemand` focus on click (task 6), a popup grab on a layer surface and the `popup_done` on a refused grab (no input serial in the headless backend), a layer that unmaps with a null buffer keeps its exclusive zone (Smithay 0.7 does not reset a layer surface on a null buffer and the map is not changed for it; same as anvil), two exclusive layers at once (the last one mapped wins), the stacking of maximized windows after a usable area change is preserved by `move_window` but not asserted by a test; the rule 4 checks do not apply (Lister, hub, ipc, panel untouched).
- Feature (compositor, stage 1, task 4): xdg popups. `State::popups: PopupManager`; `new_popup` unconstrains (`State::unconstrain_popup`: target = output geometry relative to the popup's parent, i.e. minus the root position and `get_popup_toplevel_coords`, with the client's `slide_x | slide_y | flip_x | flip_y`) and tracks the popup; `reposition_request` applies the new positioner, unconstrains and answers `repositioned`; `grab` follows anvil (`PopupKeyboardGrab` + `PopupPointerGrab`, `Focus::Keep`) and is refused with `err_log!` (never a panic) for an unknown seat, a dead root, a root that is not a mapped toplevel, a seat without keyboard / pointer, or a foreign active grab. The commit handler calls the new `xdg::handle_popup_commit` (`PopupManager::commit` plus the initial configure on the first commit); `refresh` calls `PopupManager::cleanup`; popups get frame callbacks through `Window::send_frame` (it walks the window's popups). Hook for task 5: `State::root_position` finds the position of a popup root; a layer surface root returns `None` and logs for now. Harness: `TestClient::map_popup`, `popup_configure`, `TestPopup`, `PopupParent` (implemented by `TestWindow`; `TestLayer` in task 5 returns `None` from `parent_xdg_surface` and adopts the popup with `get_popup`). New test `a_popup_gets_configured_and_shown` (failed first: the stub ignored popups, the client got no configure). API differences from the plan: none in names; `PopupManager::grab_popup` needs the root as the seat's keyboard focus type (`WlSurface`), which works because `WlSurface: From<PopupKind>`. Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-compositor`, clippy `-D warnings`, the headless tests 5 times in a row. NOT verified: a grab (no pointer or keyboard input in the headless backend, so no serial to grab with: the dismiss on a click outside is untested), a real GTK menu, the output constraint of a popup near a corner (needs a parent at a known position: layer surface test in task 5), nested popups, `reposition_request`, a popup whose parent dies.
- Fix (compositor, stage 1, task 3 review): a toplevel that unmapped by committing a null buffer (GTK hide / show of a reused window or dialog) stayed in the space with the keyboard and `Activated`, kept getting frame callbacks, and never got the fresh initial configure the protocol requires, so the client waited forever with nothing in the log. Now the commit handler (`xdg::unmap_if_bufferless`) takes such a window out of the space, keeps its `windows` entry, puts it back in `unmapped` (its next commit gets the initial configure), gives the keyboard to the topmost window when it had it, and writes a `debug_log!`. Choice: a re-mapped window goes back to its previous position (new `WindowInfo::last_position`), as GTK hide / show behaves on Openbox; it does not take a new cascade slot. Also `State::refresh` now logs once with `err_log!` when the seat has no keyboard (it returned silently). Harness: `TestClient::unmap` (null buffer + commit) and `remap` (new initial commit, waits for the configure). Test: `a_toplevel_unmapped_by_a_null_buffer_maps_again` (failed on the old code with "no configure after the new initial commit"). Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-compositor` (10 unit + 5 integration) passes, clippy `-D warnings` clean, the integration tests passed 5 runs in a row; the `OPUSDM_DEBUG=1` trace shows map at 0,0, unmap at (0, 0), map again at 0,0 (not the next cascade slot 30,30). NOT verified: the focus move on unmap is not asserted by a test (no `wl_keyboard` in the harness yet), no real GTK client hide / show, the once-only keyboard log (no backend runs without a keyboard).
- Feature (compositor, stage 1, task 3): `opusdm-compositor --headless` runs. New `src/state.rs` (`State` with the Smithay states for compositor, xdg-shell, shm, seat, data device and output with xdg-output; `space`, `windows` keyed by the toplevel `wl_surface`, `startup`, `cascade_index`, `quit`, `start_time`; `WindowInfo { restore }`; `ClientState` whose `disconnected` writes a `debug_log!`; `usable_area` = the whole output until the layer shell; `send_frames`; `focus_window` / `focus_topmost`; `refresh`), `src/shell/mod.rs` (commit handler: `on_commit_buffer_handler`, `Window::on_commit`, initial configure, `space.refresh()`), `src/shell/xdg.rs` (toplevels: initial configure with no size, mapped at `cascade_position(usable_area, size, cascade_index)` on the first buffer commit, then raised, focused and `Activated`; `toplevel_destroyed` removes the window and, when it had the keyboard, gives it to the topmost window; `minimize_request` ignored with `debug_log!("minimize ignored: no tasklist before stage 2")`; popups logged with `err_log!` and left alone until task 4), `src/backend/headless.rs` (output `HEADLESS-1`, `--size` at 60 Hz, scale 1; `ListeningSocketSource::new_auto()`; prints `WAYLAND_DISPLAY=<name>` once the socket is bound, then starts `--startup`; 16 ms timer: quit check, frame callbacks, `reap` of the startup command, dead window cleanup, flush; SIGTERM / SIGINT through `signal-hook` end the loop with exit code 0) and `src/backend/nested.rs` (stub `Err("nested backend: not yet")` until task 7). `backend/mod.rs` holds the pieces both backends share (`LoopData`, socket and display sources, keyboard with `KEY_REPEAT_DELAY` 600 / `KEY_REPEAT_RATE` 25 and pointer, signals, startup). Test harness `tests/common/mod.rs` (`Compositor` with its own `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, the client connects to the socket path; `TestClient` on smithay-client-toolkit with `map_toplevel`, `roundtrip`, `wait_frame`, `last_configure`; `HasSurface`, `TestWindow::xdg`, `Rect`), `libc` added to the dev-dependencies. Differences from the plan: the initial configure goes out on the toplevel's initial commit (as in smallvil, the protocol order) instead of inside `new_toplevel`; `State` has one more field, `unmapped: Vec<Window>` (toplevels waiting for their first buffer, since `windows` holds only `WindowInfo`); the display is dispatched through a dup of its poll fd in `LoopData` (no `unsafe`, unlike smallvil); `last_configure` returns the size the client answered with (the requested size on an axis the configure left at 0); `HasSurface` is implemented only by `TestWindow` (popups and layer surfaces come with tasks 4 and 5); `Edges`, `resize_rect`, `MIN_WINDOW_SIZE` keep a scoped `#[allow(dead_code)]` until task 6, `WindowInfo::restore` until task 5. No extra Smithay feature was needed. Tests: `a_toplevel_gets_configured_and_frame_callbacks`, `a_client_that_disconnects_abruptly_does_not_take_the_compositor_down`, `minimize_is_ignored_and_the_window_keeps_getting_frames`, `sigterm_ends_the_compositor_cleanly`. Verified: the 4 tests failed first ("no backend yet"); `cargo test -p opusdm-compositor` (10 unit + 4 integration) passes, `cargo clippy -p opusdm-compositor --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, the integration tests passed 5 runs in a row and 20 more; with `OPUSDM_DEBUG=1` the trace shows the first client's window mapped at 0,0, destroyed on its disconnect, and the next one mapped at 30,30; by hand: `--headless --startup '...; exit 3'` started the command with `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` set, logged its exit status 3, and SIGINT ended the compositor with exit code 0; `--nested` exits 1 with "nested backend: not yet". NOT verified: no real toolkit client (GTK, foot) was run, nothing is rendered (headless has no renderer), keyboard focus is not checked by a test (no `wl_keyboard` in the harness yet), a mapped window that commits a null buffer stays in the space (unmap not handled), popups are ignored, the nested backend does not exist; the rule 4 checks do not apply (Lister, hub, ipc, panel untouched).
- Feature (compositor, stage 1, task 2): new module `crates/compositor/src/geometry.rs` with the pure window geometry, no Smithay types: `cascade_position` (new windows step by `CASCADE_STEP` = 30 px from the output origin and wrap before leaving the area; a window bigger than the area goes to its origin) and `resize_rect` (dragging the left / top edge moves the origin and keeps the opposite edge; the size stops at `MIN_WINDOW_SIZE` = 64x32, the opposite edge does not move; both edges set: left / top wins). Types `Rect` and `Edges`. Declared in `main.rs` with a scoped `#[allow(dead_code)]` until task 3 uses it. Tests (in `geometry`): `windows_cascade_from_the_area_origin`, `the_cascade_wraps_before_leaving_the_area`, `a_window_bigger_than_the_area_goes_to_its_origin`, `dragging_the_left_edge_keeps_the_right_edge`, `a_resize_stops_at_the_minimum_size`. Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-compositor` (10) passes, `cargo clippy -p opusdm-compositor --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean. NOT verified: nothing calls the functions yet (no window is placed or resized by the compositor), the binary was not run, the rule 4 checks do not apply (Lister, hub, ipc, panel untouched).
- Feature (compositor, stage 1, task 1): new crate `crates/compositor` (`opusdm-compositor`, branch `opus-wayland`): argument parsing (`--nested|--headless [--size WxH] [--startup COMMAND]`, exit code 2 plus usage on a bad command line), the child environment (`child_env`: `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` and `XDG_SESSION_TYPE=wayland` set; `DISPLAY`, `GDK_BACKEND`, `LABWC_PID`, `WAYFIRE_SOCKET` removed), `spawn_startup` / `reap` for the startup command, logging through `tracing-subscriber` (`warn`, `debug` with `OPUSDM_DEBUG`). `backend::run` is a stub returning `Err("no backend yet")` (logged with `err_log!`, exit 1) until task 3. Smithay 0.7 (`backend_winit`, `desktop`, `wayland_frontend`) and the client-side test dev-dependencies are in `Cargo.toml` but not used yet; no extra Smithay feature was needed to build. No other crate touched. Tests: `nested_with_size_and_startup`, `headless_takes_the_default_size`, `a_backend_is_required_and_bad_values_fail`, `children_see_only_this_compositor`, `reap_clears_the_slot_once_the_child_has_exited`. Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-compositor` (5) passes, `cargo clippy -p opusdm-compositor --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean. NOT verified: the binary was not run, no Wayland socket or window exists yet, the Smithay API is not exercised, and the rule 4 checks do not apply (Lister, hub, ipc, panel untouched).
- Design (branch `opus-wayland`): `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-25-opusdm-compositor-design.md`, the native Wayland compositor `opusdm-compositor` (Smithay 0.7, in five stages, nested sandbox first). Draft for the owner's review: approach A (Smithay) and the scope (all `openbox-rc.xml` window manager bindings built in, screen lock deferred to stage 5) decided by the owner; the other calls are listed at the end of the document. No code change. NOT verified: nothing built or run, the Smithay API was checked only through its documentation.
- Plan (branch `opus-wayland`): `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-25-compositor-stage1.md`, stage 1 of the native compositor (nested skeleton: crate, headless test backend, xdg-shell, popups, layer-shell, input, nested winit backend, `run-native.sh`). Stages 2-5 get their own plans after stage 1 runs. No code change. NOT verified: nothing built or run.

## 2026-11-23
- Version bump to **0.20.1** (`[workspace.package]` in `Cargo.toml`, inherited by all crates and `xtask`; `Cargo.lock` refreshed) with `scripts/release.sh`. No code change. Verified: `cargo build --release --workspace` passes. NOT verified: nothing else exercised, no behaviour touched.
- Fix (Open with...): typing the name of a command that is on `PATH` found nothing, because the dialog searched only the installed desktop entries (`.desktop`); `PATH` commands were a Run window feature only. Now the dialog adds the `PATH` commands to its catalog from a worker thread (`load_path_commands`, the suggestions refresh when the scan ends), and picking one starts `command [typed args] %F` through `gio::AppInfo::create_from_commandline` (program name shell-quoted), so the files are passed to it. `scan_path` / `path_commands` moved unchanged from `crates/runner/src/sources.rs` to `opusdm_apps::entries` (the Run window re-exports `path_commands`; its test still passes). Ownership: the scan future holds a strong `Rc<State>` until the scan ends (short), no cycle, the dialog cannot be dropped under it. Tests: `a_command_line_ends_with_the_file_list`, `the_program_name_is_quoted`; `scan_path_lists_executables_once_and_skips_the_rest` still passes. Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-fileactions -p opusdm-apps -p opusdm-run` passes, clippy clean, `cargo build -p opusdm-lister` builds. NOT verified: the dialog in a running Lister or the sandbox (a command typed, Enter, the file really opened by it), the Desktop "Open with", the Run window after the move of the scan, the running session. Lister, hub, ipc and panel code not touched, so the rule 4 checks were not run.

## 2026-11-22
- Version bump to **0.20.0** (`[workspace.package]` in `Cargo.toml`, inherited by all crates and `xtask`; `Cargo.lock` refreshed) with `scripts/release.sh`. No code change. Verified: `cargo build --release --workspace` passes. NOT verified: nothing else exercised, no behaviour touched.
- Look (panel start menu, Shutdown / Logout requesters): the session requesters are now "branded": the OpusDM logo (200 px wide, on the left) over a separator, the question large and bold, the detail dimmed under it, and a window about twice as wide as before (`BRANDED_MIN_WIDTH` 540 px of content). The confirm buttons say what they do ("Shut Down", "Log Out" instead of "Yes"); Cancel, Esc and closing the window still cancel, the actions run are unchanged (`systemctl poweroff`, `logout`). The window title is "OpusDM" instead of "Request". New `opusdm_ui::requester_branded` and `branded_markup` (`crates/ui/src/requester.rs`); it uses the existing `requester_rich_header`. The logo texture is scaled down with gdk-pixbuf and put in a horizontal row: a `gtk::Picture` sized only by the widget grows back to the 640 px of the file (height-for-width). The About window is unchanged (`LOGO_PNG` only made `pub(crate)`). The "Delete application" requester of the menu keeps the plain look. No ownership change. Test: `branded_markup_escapes_and_dims_the_detail`. Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-ui` (60) passes, clippy has no warning in the touched crates; the Shut down requester rendered in Xephyr through a throw-away example (removed, not committed) at each step, final look checked on the screenshot; the Log out requester ("Log out?", "Log Out" / "Cancel") rendered the same way and checked too. The panel start menu is the only place with these two requesters. NOT verified: the panel itself in the running session (needs `scripts/live-update.sh`), a light theme or a requester picture set in the theme, HiDPI (the prescaled logo may look soft at scale 2).
- Fix (Lister / Desktop context menu): "Delete..." on a selected entry asked for (and deleted) only the clicked entry, while the other menu items act on the whole selection. It now uses the selection when it holds the clicked entry (same rule as `targets_for`), else the clicked entry alone; the question is `Delete "name" permanently?` for one entry, `Delete N item(s) permanently?` for more. New `FileActionHost::own_selected_paths` (default: the selection): the Desktop leaves the left-out icons out of it, so a menu Delete never reaches the real file behind a left-out icon (the menu already offered no Delete on a left-out icon). Behaviour change, asked by the owner: with several files selected, the menu Delete now deletes all of them. Move (Source -> Dest) in the menu still moves only the clicked entry (not asked, not changed). No ownership change. Test: `delete_on_a_selected_entry_asks_for_the_whole_selection`. Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-fileactions -p opusdm-hub -p opusdm-lister` passes, clippy clean for the touched files; Xephyr sandbox: Lister, 3 files selected (click + Shift+click), right click on the middle one, Delete... showed "Delete 3 item(s) permanently?" with the 3 names, Delete removed all 3. NOT verified: the Desktop menu Delete with a left-out icon in the selection (covered by the existing `selectable_paths` test only), the running session.
- Look (delete requesters): the requester with a name list is about twice as wide (`LIST_MIN_WIDTH` 440 px for the name box) and the box spans the whole width inside the requester margins, names aligned to the left. Verified in the Xephyr sandbox: Desktop "Delete..." (1 name) and Lister Ctrl+A + Del (14 names), Cancel moved nothing. NOT verified: the running session.
- Look (delete requesters): the name list now sits in a box with inner padding (8px 12px), rounded corners and a background slightly different from the requester (`alpha(currentColor, 0.08)`, so it follows light and dark themes). `opusdm_ui` installs this one rule (`LIST_CSS`, class `opus-requester-list`) for the display the first time a list requester opens, because the hub (Desktop requesters) does not load the shared app CSS; the selector beats the "inner boxes transparent" rule of a requester picture. Other requesters unchanged. Test: `the_list_css_styles_the_list_class_inside_a_requester`. Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-ui` passes, clippy clean for the file; in the Xephyr sandbox the Desktop "Delete..." requester (1 name) and the Lister Ctrl+A + Del requester (14 names, "... and 4 more") show the box, Cancel moves nothing. NOT verified: a requester picture set in the theme, a light theme, the running session.
- Feature (delete requesters): the confirmation before a delete now lists the names of the entries, one per line in a monospace font under the question; past 10 names the list ends with "... and N more" (`LIST_MAX_NAMES`), and a long name is ellipsized in the middle. New `opusdm_ui::requester_list` and `list_lines` (`crates/ui/src/requester.rs`; `build_with_header` takes an optional `list` widget shown under the body, `None` for every other requester, so they are unchanged) and `opusdm_fileactions::keys::entry_names` (last path component, the whole path for `/`). Used by the Lister `actions::confirm_delete` (Del, Shift+Del, toolbar, context menu), the Desktop `desktop_ops::confirm_delete` and the Trash dialog "Delete" (names of the original paths). The question text, the buttons and what happens after them are not changed; "Empty the trash" keeps its plain requester. No ownership change. Tests: `a_short_list_is_shown_whole`, `a_long_list_ends_with_the_count_of_the_rest` (ui), `the_delete_list_shows_the_file_names` (fileactions). Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-ui -p opusdm-fileactions` passes, clippy has no warning in the touched files. In the Xephyr sandbox (debug build, private HOME, own D-Bus, no window manager): Lister with 14 files, Ctrl+A + Del showed "Move 14 item(s) to the trash?" with 10 names (the long one ellipsized) and "... and 4 more"; Cancel closed it and nothing moved; one file + Del showed its name, Trash moved it to the sandbox Trash; Desktop icon, context menu Delete... showed `Delete "desk_one.txt" permanently?` with the name, Delete removed it. NOT verified: the Trash dialog "Delete" (same call, not opened), Del on the Desktop by key (keys did not reach the Desktop window without a window manager), the running session, the global hotkeys and hub checks of rule 4 (not touched).
- Feature (Desktop, step 3): the lister file keys work on the Desktop selection. New `opusdm_fileactions::keys` (`FileKey`, `FILE_KEYS`, `resolve`, and `transfer_selection`, `delete_selection`, `rename_selection` with the Lister's messages); `desktop_host::attach_keys` puts a capture-phase key controller on the primary Desktop window (keys typed in a text field go through). `F2` opens the Rename requester (before, the icon field started its inline edit, whose result the Desktop never handled), `F3` `opusdm-viewer`, `F5` / `F6` Copy / Move to DEST, `Delete` asks and moves to the trash (as asked: like the Lister), `Shift+Delete` asks and deletes for good, `Ctrl+C` / `X` / `V` clipboard (same format as the Lister), `Ctrl+N` / `Ctrl+Shift+N` new folder / file. Keys that change files (`F2`, `F6`, `Delete`, `Shift+Delete`, `Ctrl+X`) skip left-out icons; with only left-out icons selected the Desktop says so. Ctrl+A, Esc, arrows, Enter and Space stay with the icon field. The Lister key table is not changed; `the_shared_file_keys_do_the_same_here_as_on_the_desktop` (lister) checks that it maps every shared key to the same command. Tests: `every_listed_key_resolves_to_its_file_key`, `alt_shift_f3_and_plain_letters_are_not_file_keys`, `the_delete_question_says_trash_or_permanently` (fileactions), `group_icons_are_not_files_to_act_on_and_left_out_ones_only_when_asked`, `only_the_keys_that_change_files_skip_left_out_icons` (hub). `docs/usage.md` 3.12 and 3.21 updated. Ownership: the key handler holds a strong `Rc<Widgets>` like the other Desktop window handlers (`Widgets` lives for the whole run). Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-fileactions -p opusdm-hub -p opusdm-lister` passes, clippy has no new warning. In the Xephyr sandbox (openbox): F2 on `note.txt` (Rename requester, renamed), Delete (question "Move 1 item(s) to the trash?", file in the sandbox Trash), Delete on a left-out icon (refused with a message, real file kept), Ctrl+N and Ctrl+Shift+N (folder and file made), F5 with no DEST (message), F3 (`opusdm-viewer` started), Ctrl+C on the Desktop (the path reached the clipboard, seen pasted into a Lister path bar), Ctrl+C in a Lister then Ctrl+V on the Desktop (file copied), Ctrl+A then Delete (7 of 8 icons counted: the left-out one skipped; cancelled), Right + Enter (folder opened in a Lister). Global hotkeys in the same run: Ctrl+backslash (terminal), Alt+2 / Alt+1 (desktops), Ctrl+Shift+space (run window), Ctrl+Print (shot overlay). NOT verified: the running session and its window manager (the Desktop window must get the keyboard focus when an icon is clicked; it does under openbox in the sandbox), Ctrl+V in a Lister whose focus is in the path bar pastes the text there (Lister behaviour, not changed), a function run from the hub.

- Feature (Desktop, step 2): a Desktop icon now has the context menu of a Lister file. `fill_icon_menu` (`crates/hub/src/desktop_menu.rs`) fills the shared `opusdm_fileactions::menu::fill_entry_menu` through the new `DesktopHost` (`crates/hub/src/desktop_host.rs`), after the Desktop's own Open, Put Away (left-out icons), Cut and Copy (clipboard), as before; program group icons keep their own menu. New items on the Desktop: Copy / Move (Source -> Dest), Change Permissions, the file type "Open With" entries, Open with, Compress, Extract archive / here, Encrypt / Decrypt, Open in Viewer (`opusdm-viewer`), Set as Desktop Background. A left-out icon still gets no Rename / Move / Delete (they would act on the real file elsewhere) and no Cut. Delete... still deletes for good, as the Lister menu item does. New `HubCmd::RunJobToDests { op, srcs }` (the server resolves the folders of the DEST Listers itself; no SOURCE role needed; a remote DEST is skipped and logged) and `HubCmd::SetBackground(path)` (same `apply_background_image` as the function step). The GTK side now keeps the DEST folders from `HubEvent::Roles` (`Widgets::dest_dirs`, ignored before) only to say "no DEST Lister set" up front. Desktop refusals are logged with `err_log!` and shown in a requester (the Desktop has no message line). `desktop_ops::confirm_delete` takes `permanent` (Trash or Delete, like the Lister's). Ownership: `DesktopHost` holds a strong `Rc<Widgets>`, like every other Desktop menu item; `Widgets` lives for the whole GTK run. Tests: `a_desktop_copy_goes_to_the_dest_lister_without_a_source`, `a_desktop_copy_with_no_dest_does_nothing` (server), `group_icons_are_not_files_to_act_on`, `copy_to_dest_needs_a_local_dest_folder`, `a_left_out_icon_is_a_target_that_lives_elsewhere` (desktop host). Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-hub` passes, clippy has no new warning. In the Xephyr sandbox: right click on a Desktop file (full menu), Copy (Source -> Dest) with no DEST (requester "no DEST Lister set" plus log line), then with a DEST Lister (file copied into its folder), Extract here on `box.zip` (folder `box` on the Desktop), Set as Desktop Background on `red.png` (background turned red), Open in Viewer (`opusdm-viewer` started, reaped after it was closed), Delete... (requester shown, cancelled), a left-out `a.txt` (Open, Put Away, Copy, no Cut / Rename / Move / Delete). Lister checks in the same run: Enter in the path bar, double click on a folder, parent button, Backspace, Alt+Left / Alt+Right, two Listers with SOURCE / DEST, a Copy job. NOT verified: the global hotkeys (not touched), a function run, Compress / Encrypt / Change Permissions / Open with started from the Desktop (same shared code as the Lister, checked there), a remote DEST, the running session. Known, not changed: the Desktop menu is drawn in the Desktop window, so a Lister window over the icon covers it (as before).

- Refactor (Lister, step 1 of "Desktop icons get the Lister menu and keys", plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-22-desktop-file-actions.md`): new crate `opusdm-fileactions` (`crates/fileactions`) with the `FileActionHost` trait (what a file action needs from its window), `MenuSink` (where menu items go), `fill_entry_menu` (the entry context menu, same items and order as the Lister menu before) and the dialogs it opens, moved unchanged from the Lister except for the host calls: Change Permissions (`permissions.rs`), Open with (`open_with.rs`), Compress, Encrypt / Decrypt, Extract. `menu_entries` (file type "Open With" entries) moved too. The Lister implements the host in `crates/lister/src/file_host.rs` (`ListerHost` holds the same strong `Rc<Ui>` the menu closures and dialogs held before, made when a menu or dialog opens: no ownership change); Leave Out on Desktop and Checksums stay Lister-only items (`extra_items`). The old Lister functions (`attr_dialogs::open_permissions_for`, `compress_dialog::open_compress`, `crypt_dialog::open_crypt`, `extract_dialog::*`, `open_with::open_with`, `filetypes_ui::menu_entries`) are thin wrappers, so the palette and toolbar callers are unchanged. The `open_with` log tag is now `file-actions` instead of `opusdm-lister`. Tests moved with the code (permissions, compress, crypt, extract) plus new menu tests on a fake host (`crates/fileactions/src/menu/tests.rs`: item order, archive / `.enc` / picture items, left-out entries, file type entries, DEST check, Delete, selection vs clicked entry). Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-fileactions` (15) and `-p opusdm-lister` (420, the 6 missing moved to the new crate) pass, clippy has no new warning. In the Xephyr sandbox (private HOME, own D-Bus): double click on a folder, right click on rows (same items as before), Extract here, Compress (zip made), Change Permissions (664 to 644), Open with (handlers listed), Rename, Delete (confirm, file gone), Open in Viewer by Enter. NOT verified in this step: Encrypt / Decrypt, Extract archive with a DEST, the "Open With: <function>" entries (no file type with menu entries in the sandbox), the running session.

## 2026-11-15
- Version bump to **0.19.3** (`[workspace.package]` in `Cargo.toml`, inherited by all crates and `xtask`; `Cargo.lock` refreshed) with `scripts/release.sh`. No code change. Verified: `cargo build --release --workspace` passes. NOT verified: nothing else exercised, no behaviour touched.
- Fix (keyring-watch): "Unlock with password..." said OK but the tray kept asking. The control socket unlocks only the `login` keyring and `unlock_and_verify` (`crates/keyring-watch/src/gnome.rs`) checked only `login`, while the health check looks at the `default` alias. On this machine `default` pointed to `Default_Keyring`, another (locked) collection, so every unlock "worked" and the next check raised the alert again, with nothing in the log. Now, after the password is accepted and `login` is open, a `default` that is a different, still locked keyring is unlocked with `Service.Unlock` without a prompt (new `Vault::unlock_default_without_prompt` in `probe.rs`: a returned prompt is dismissed, never shown); if it stays locked the window shows `DEFAULT_STILL_LOCKED` ("make login the default keyring ... or use Unlock") and both steps are logged with `err_log!`. A wrong password is still decided by `login` alone. The old test `the_login_collection_decides_not_the_default_one` encoded the bug (login open + default locked = success) and was replaced by `a_locked_login_is_a_wrong_password_whatever_the_default`, `a_distinct_locked_default_is_unlocked_without_a_prompt`, `a_default_that_stays_locked_is_not_a_success`, `an_open_default_is_not_unlocked_again`; `vault_error_after_unlock_is_reported` also covers an error on the default with `login` open. `docs/usage.md` 3.42 updated.
  - Also done by hand on the live keyring (not code): the `default` alias set to `login` (`Service.SetAlias`, old file kept as `~/.local/share/keyrings/default.bak-2026-11-15`), as the owner asked; `Default_Keyring` (plaintext) was unlocked by a D-Bus `Unlock`.
  - Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-keyring-watch` (55) passes, clippy clean for the crate. Live, against the running daemon, with a temporary ignored test (not committed) calling `unlock_default_without_prompt` and the alias pointed for a moment at `Default_Keyring` (locked first) and then at `Default_28`: both went from locked to unlocked with no prompt; the alias was set back to `login` and checked. NOT verified: the dismiss path for a keyring that really needs its own password (no such keyring found here, `Default_28` opened without one), the password window end to end with a real password, the installed watcher (`/opt/opusdm/bin`, needs `scripts/live-update.sh`), the tray alert clearing. Nothing in the Lister, hub, ipc or panel changed, so the rule 4 checks were not run.

## 2026-11-14
- Version bump to **0.19.2** (`[workspace.package]` in `Cargo.toml`, inherited by all crates and `xtask`; `Cargo.lock` refreshed) with `scripts/release.sh`. No code change. Verified: `cargo build --release --workspace` passes. NOT verified: nothing else exercised, no behaviour touched.
- Feature (Sidecar, audio): the Sidecar showed "Nothing to show" for audio files (`mp3`, `flac`, `ogg`, `oga`, `opus`, `wav`, `m4a`, `aac`, `wma`, `mka`, the list of the default "Audio file" type). As asked, it now plays them at once (like a video) and shows the cover (or a note icon), title, artist and album with the usual control bar; moving to another file stops it; Space / Left / Right / Up / Down / M work as on a video; the strip labels them `AUDIO`; a folder under the cursor brings its audio files too. Only the Sidecar: `kind_of` / `kind_or_image` never return the new `MediaKind::Audio`, `kind_for(path, audio)` adds it when the window is a Sidecar (`State::_current`, the strip), and `Gallery::from_targets(targets, audio)` adds audio files of a folder only for the Sidecar, so `opusdm-viewer` on its own behaves as before. `VideoView` (`crates/viewer/src/video_view.rs`) puts its frames in a stack with the audio view (`open_audio`) and fills it from the engine tags. Three commits:
  - `media`: `Engine` kept the merged tags of every file it ever opened, so the tags of the first file stuck to the next one (seen in the sandbox: a `.flac` showed the artist and album of the `.mp3` before it). `open()` now empties the tags and flushes the bus messages of the previous file. The player and the desktop background open one file per engine, so they never showed it. Test: a new scenario in `crates/media/tests/engine.rs` (two tagged WAV files on one engine), which fails without the fix.
  - `media-ui`: `AudioView` moved unchanged from `opusdm-player` to `opusdm-media-ui` (plus `reset`), shared by the player and the viewer.
  - `viewer`: the Sidecar audio support above. Tests: `audio_is_known_only_when_asked_for`, `a_folder_brings_its_audio_files_only_for_the_sidecar`, the Sidecar `showable` test and the slideshow test extended.
  - Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-media -p opusdm-media-ui -p opusdm-player -p opusdm-viewer` passes, clippy has no new warning. In the Xephyr sandbox (audio through the real PulseAudio socket, silent generated files, so nothing was heard): Lister on a folder with `song.mp3` (tagged), `other.flac` (title only), `pic.png`; Shift+F3 on `song.mp3` played it (00:03 / 00:30, pause button) and showed note icon, "Silent Test", "OpusDM Bot", "Sandbox Album"; `pic.png` showed the picture and the mp3 stream ended (`pactl`: one stream left); `other.flac` played with only "Second One" (after the engine fix); Ctrl+A gave the strip `AUDIO`, picture, `AUDIO`; Space paused; `opusdm-viewer` on the same folder alone showed only `pic.png`. NOT verified: a real cover image (no file with embedded art used), sound actually heard, audio inside an archive in the Sidecar (same path after the extraction), the player after the `AudioView` move on screen (only built and tested), the running session (needs `scripts/live-update.sh --all`).
- Feature (Sidecar, archives): inside an archive the Sidecar showed nothing. Now `sidecar::follow` (`crates/lister/src/sidecar.rs`) extracts the selected files (else the cursor file) of the archive to temporary copies and sends those. Extraction runs on a worker thread, one worker at a time; a selection change while it runs is followed again when it ends, and an answer that is out of date (newer selection, folder left) is not sent. Copies are kept per Sidecar (key: archive, entry, size, date), so walking back is instant. Folders of the archive are skipped, entries over 256 MiB are not extracted (`MAX_ARCHIVE_PREVIEW_BYTES`, so a big video is not unpacked just by moving the cursor), at most 20 files per update (`MAX_ARCHIVE_PREVIEWS`); both logged with `dlog!`. Extraction errors are logged with `err_log!`. Delete with the Sidecar open inside an archive no longer takes "the file on screen" (that is the temporary copy): it falls back to the usual Delete of the selection. Remote folders still show nothing in the Sidecar (as asked: archives only). `follow` and `job_done` now take `&Rc<Ui>` (every caller already had one); `Ui::selected_positions` is now `pub(crate)`. Test: `archive_previews_skip_folders_and_big_files_and_are_capped`. Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-lister` (426) passes, clippy has no new warning; in the Xephyr sandbox, inside `t.zip` with Shift+F3: `a.png` selected showed the picture, Down to `b.txt` showed its text, a fast run of Up/Down keys ended on `a.png` and the Sidecar showed `a.png`, double click on the archive folder `sub` showed `sub/c.txt` (cursor row, nothing selected), Backspace out of the archive to the local folder showed the local selection again. NOT verified: a big or slow archive (7z solid, many files) while walking fast, the 256 MiB and 20 files limits on screen (covered by the unit test only), Delete with the Sidecar inside an archive (not pressed: the guard is a plain early return), the running session.
- Fix (Lister, remote folders): a double click on a file of an FTP / SFTP folder handed the downloaded temporary copy to the system default application. It now goes through the same `open_temp_copy` (renamed from `open_extracted`, `crates/lister/src/window.rs`) as a file inside an archive: the double click function of its file type runs on the copy through `ToHub::RunFunctionOnPaths`, else the default application opens it. A download that gives no local file is now logged instead of doing nothing. No new test (the path is the GTK download callback; the hub side is covered by the tests of the archive fix). Verified in the Xephyr sandbox against a throw-away local FTP server (`pyftpdlib` in a scratch venv, `ftp://bob@127.0.0.1:2121/`, password asked by the Lister dialog): double click on `a.png` started `opusdm-viewer /tmp/opusdm-remote-.../a.png` (window shown), on `b.txt` `opusdm-viewer .../b.txt`, on `c.xyzq` (no file type) the default application (Zed) as before. NOT verified: SFTP (no key login to this machine; same code after the download), the running session (needs `scripts/live-update.sh --all`), the rule 4 hotkey and hub checks.
- Fix (Lister, archives): a double click on a file inside an archive always handed the extracted temporary copy to the system default application (`xdg`), skipping the user file types, so a `.png` opened in an external viewer instead of `opusdm-viewer` and a video not in `opusdm-player`. `Ui::activate_row` (`crates/lister/src/window.rs`) now calls the new `open_extracted`: it looks up the file type of the extracted copy (`filetypes_ui::type_for`, same rules as a local file) and runs its double click function on that copy; with no file type (or no double click action) it falls back to the default application as before. The run cannot go through `RunFunctionById`, which asks the Lister for its selection (paths inside the archive, not real files), so a new IPC message `ToHub::RunFunctionOnPaths { id, paths }` runs the function on the given local files; the hub routes it to the existing `run_on_paths` (used by the desktop), which now takes the requester so the Lister gets `FunctionFailed` / `FunctionDone` (sent through `start_run`, the busy spinner stops as for a local double click). A closed extraction channel is now logged instead of a silent return. Tests: `run_function_on_paths_runs_on_the_given_file_without_a_selection_query`, `run_function_on_paths_with_an_unknown_id_reports_failure_to_the_lister` (hub), the `ToHub` round trip (ipc). Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-hub -p opusdm-ipc -p opusdm-lister` passes, clippy has no new warning; in a Xephyr sandbox (private config with the owner's `filetypes.json`, own D-Bus session) a Lister opened on a folder with `t.zip`, double click on `t.zip` entered it, double click on `a.png` started `opusdm-viewer /tmp/opusdm-preview-.../a.png` (window shown, "a.png - 1x1"), on `b.txt` started `opusdm-viewer .../b.txt`, on `c.xyzq` (no file type) opened the default application (Zed) as before. NOT verified: the running session (needs `scripts/live-update.sh --all`: hub and Lister must be updated together, an old hub does not know the new message), video/audio in `opusdm-player` from an archive (same code path, not clicked), Enter in the path bar, Backspace / parent, Alt+Left/Right, F-keys, global hotkeys and the hub SOURCE/DEST and Copy checks of rule 4 of `CLAUDE.md` (not touched by the change). Files inside a remote (FTP/SFTP) folder still open with the default application (not asked, left as is).
- Version bump to **0.19.1** (`[workspace.package]` in `Cargo.toml`, inherited by all crates and `xtask`; `Cargo.lock` refreshed) with `scripts/release.sh`. No code change. Verified: `cargo build --release --workspace` passes. NOT verified: nothing else exercised, no behaviour touched.

## 2026-11-12
- Feature (applets): icon buttons and horizontal placement in the applet widget tree (`crates/applets/src/ui_tree.rs`, `widgets.rs`). A `button` takes `icon` (a theme icon name such as `view-refresh-symbolic`, letters, digits, `-`, `_`, `.` only) in place of `label` (giving both is an error) and an optional `tooltip`. New common props `hexpand` (bool) and `halign` (`"start"`, `"center"`, `"end"`, `"fill"`), accepted by every node and by `opus.ui.update`, as are `icon` and `tooltip` of a button. Only an explicit `hexpand: true` is set at build, so a box still follows the expansion of its children. Additive: every tree valid before is still valid and built the same way. Asked for by the FATTO Timer applet (a right-aligned refresh icon). Tests: `a_button_has_a_label_or_an_icon_and_an_optional_tooltip`, `hexpand_and_halign_are_common_props` (ui_tree), `an_icon_button_and_alignment` (widgets, GTK). Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-applets` (160 lib + integration tests) passes, clippy clean for the crate; the FATTO Timer applet run in `opusdm_applets::runtime` with a fake server, its tree built with these GTK widgets and all its updates applied (refresh button: icon `view-refresh-symbolic`, hexpand, halign End). `docs/usage.md` 3.41 updated. NOT verified: the running session (needs `scripts/live-update.sh`, left to the owner), the look on screen. No change in the hub, Lister, ipc or panel.
- Fix (thumbnails): many videos (and a PDF) on the Desktop never got a thumbnail; the hub log said `thumbnail failed for ...: No child processes (os error 10)`. The Desktop icon view runs inside the hub, whose SIGCHLD reaper (`waitpid(-1)`, `crates/hub/src/children.rs`) often collected the `ffmpeg` / `pdftoppm` child before `run_with_timeout` (`crates/iconview/src/thumbs/generate.rs`) polled it with `try_wait`, which then failed with `ECHILD`; the file was marked as failed and never asked again until a reload. `run_with_timeout` now waits through `opusdm_ipc::reaped::poll_exit` (and `reaped::wait` after a kill on timeout), which takes the status the reaper recorded, like the hub, compress and applet code already do. A failed kill or reap after a timeout is now logged. Test: `output_is_kept_when_another_reaper_collects_the_child` (a `waitpid(-1)` thread like the hub reaper; it failed with the same `ECHILD` before the fix, 20 of 20 runs pass after); the child-spawning tests of `generate.rs` now run one at a time (`child_lock`). New dev-dependency of `opusdm-iconview`: `libc` (already in the workspace). Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-iconview` (55 + the ignored real generator test), clippy clean for the crate, `ffmpeg` makes a frame of each of the 6 failing Desktop videos in 0.1-0.2 s (so no timeout is involved). Verified live: after `scripts/live-update.sh --all` (run by the owner) every mp4 and PDF on the Desktop has a cached thumbnail, the owner confirmed them on screen, and the new hub log has 0 `thumbnail failed` lines and 23 `reaped elsewhere` lines (generators whose status the reaper took, now recovered). NOT verified by me: the Lister, global hotkey and hub checks of rule 4 of `CLAUDE.md` (the change is limited to how the thumbnail generator child is waited for; the owner used the restarted session).
- Feature (run window): a `PATH` command that an installed app runs shows that app's icon instead of the generic `utilities-terminal` one (typing `opusdm-terminal` ranks the command row first, by exact name, and it had the generic icon). `sources::app_icons` (`crates/runner/src/sources.rs`) maps program name to the app icon on the GTK thread (`opusdm_apps::entries::program_name`, so entries without `Exec=` are skipped); `main.rs` moves the map into the `PATH` worker closure and `with_app_icons` fills the icon of each matching command. A program shared by apps with different icons (`env`, `flatpak`) gets no icon. `ui.rs` is unchanged (it already shows `Entry::icon` for any kind). Tests: `a_command_takes_the_icon_of_the_app_that_runs_it`, `a_program_shared_by_several_apps_gives_no_icon`. Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-run` (15) passes, clippy clean for the crate; on this machine a temporary ignored test (not committed) gave 92 programs with an app icon, 79 of the 4025 `PATH` commands with an icon, `opusdm-terminal` and `opusdm-settings` with their app icons, `env`, `ls`, `bash` without. NOT verified: the Run window on screen (not opened); an app started as `env VAR=1 prog` alone gives its icon to `env` (only a second such app with another icon removes it).
- Fix (run window): an app installed after `menu.json` was seeded (the panel writes it once, on first run) was not an application of `opusdm-run`, only a `PATH` command, so its row had the generic `utilities-terminal` icon. `sources::app_entries` (`crates/runner/src/sources.rs`) now lists the apps of `menu.json` in menu order, then every other installed app (`merge_apps`, each desktop id once); before, it used only `menu.json` when it listed any app. `menu.json` itself is not changed. Tests: `merge_apps_keeps_the_menu_order_then_adds_the_other_installed_apps`, `merge_apps_with_an_empty_menu_lists_every_installed_app`. Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-run` (13) passes, clippy clean for the crate; on this machine a temporary ignored test (not committed) calling `app_entries()` against the real `menu.json` (121 ids, dated 2026-10-30) gave 134 applications, with `org.opusdm.Terminal`, `org.opusdm.Settings` and `org.opusdm.Viewer` (not in `menu.json`) present with their icons. NOT verified: the Run window itself on screen (not opened). `docs/usage.md` 3.25 updated.
- Fix (panel, application menu): the "Filter applications" field matched only the app name, so typing a command name found nothing when the name differs (DreamOS: "DreamOS Installer" runs `dreamos-install`, and `dreamos-install` does not match "dreamos installer"). The filter now matches the name, the generic name, the keywords and the program name, the same texts the Run window searches (`matches_filter_any` and `filter_extra` in `crates/panel/src/appmenu.rs`; `opusdm_apps::entries::program_name` is now public so the menu reuses its `Exec=` guard). Test: `filter_matches_the_program_name_and_keywords`. Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-panel -p opusdm-apps` passes (194 + 18), clippy has no warning in the two crates. NOT verified: the filter in a running panel (the live panel was not replaced), the DreamOS ISO with the new panel.
- Version bump to **0.19.0** (`[workspace.package]` in `Cargo.toml`, inherited by all crates and `xtask`; `Cargo.lock` refreshed) with `scripts/release.sh`. No code change. Verified: `cargo build --release --workspace` passes. NOT verified: nothing else exercised, no behaviour touched.
- Fix (brightness): the Display Brightness slider of the battery tray showed "cannot access /sys/class/backlight/amdgpu_bl1/brightness: Permission denied (os error 13)". The file is `root:root 644` (no udev rule, the user is not in `video`), so the direct write failed. `Sysfs::set` (`crates/brightness/src/backend.rs`) still writes the file first; only on `PermissionDenied` it asks logind (`org.freedesktop.login1.Session.SetBrightness("backlight", name, raw)` on `/org/freedesktop/login1/session/auto`, system bus, blocking `zbus`, called on the brightness worker thread, never on the GTK thread), the same way `brightnessctl` does. When logind fails too the error names both causes. Reading is unchanged. New dependency of `opusdm-brightness`: `zbus` 5.19 (already used by the panel and other crates). Verified live on this laptop: `busctl ... SetBrightness` accepted from this x11 session; `cargo run -p opusdm-brightness --example probe` detected "backlight" at 70%, set 60%, restored 70% through logind; 25 crate tests pass, clippy clean for the crate and the panel. NOT verified: the slider in the running panel (needs `scripts/live-update.sh`, sudo, left to the owner), a session that is not active (logind refuses it, the error then shows in the popover).
- Feature (keyring-watch, hub): **keyring watch**, a new `opusdm-keyring-watch` binary (crate `crates/keyring-watch`) that checks the Secret Service every 30 s (first check after 20 s) and, after two bad checks in a row, shows a tray icon (StatusNotifierItem with a dbusmenu, no panel change) and one notification with the reason (`NoBus`, `NoService`, `NoCollection`, `Locked`, `Timeout`); one good check removes the icon; a reason change notifies again and replaces the old notification. Menu: reason line, Unlock (Secret Service `Unlock`, the provider prompts), Unlock with password... and Restart gnome-keyring... (a GTK window run as a child process of the same binary, `--ask-password unlock|restart`, also `--unlock` without a tray; the password goes only to `gnome-keyring-daemon --unlock` on stdin, in `Zeroizing` memory, never in args, env or logs), Test (write, read, compare, delete a probe item, deleted even on failure) and Check now. The check reads properties with `zbus` (no encrypted session, no prompt on a locked keyring) behind a `Vault` trait with a fake in tests, runs on a worker thread with a 15 s timeout, and the pure `Tracker` state machine decides raise and clear. gnome-keyring specifics (who owns the name, unlock, restart through `systemctl --user restart gnome-keyring-daemon.service` or `--replace` without the unit) are in `gnome.rs`, hidden when another program owns `org.freedesktop.secrets`. Single instance by the bus name `org.opusdm.KeyringWatch` (`DoNotQueue`, exit 3 when taken; the spec said the `opusdm_ipc::instance` socket, a bus name needs no stale socket file). NOT done from the spec: starting `gnome-keyring-daemon --start --components=secrets` before unlocking when no daemon runs. The hub starts it with `keyring_host::start()` (supervisor thread like `applet_host`: owns the Child, 1 s / 2 s / 4 s backoff, 3 restarts in 60 s, exit 3 waits 30 s and retries; every exit logged, "already running" at debug level only). New dependency `ksni` 0.3 (`async-io` + `blocking`, no tokio): the only maintained Rust implementation of StatusNotifierItem plus dbusmenu, nothing in the repo exported an item before; `zeroize` for the password. `build-release.sh` and `live-update.sh` ship and stop the binary. Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-03-keyring-watch-design.md`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-03-keyring-watch.md`, usage in section 3.42 of `docs/usage.md`.
  - Found by hand on this machine: `gnome-keyring-daemon --unlock` with a WRONG password exits 0, prints nothing and leaves the keyring locked. So `gnome::unlock_with_password` does not trust the exit code: it reads the lock state of the default collection afterwards and answers "Wrong password" when it is still locked (tests: `exit_zero_with_a_still_locked_keyring_is_a_wrong_password`).
  - Tests: 46 in `opusdm-keyring-watch` (tracker, probe, self test, gnome, menu, arguments), 7 new in the hub (`keyring_host`: restart after a crash, already running, give up, missing binary, spawn error, backoff, sibling path); `cargo test -p opusdm-hub` 339 + 13 passed; clippy clean for the new crate and the hub change.
  - Verified live in this session (the keyring `login` was locked, the session started before `libpam-gnome-keyring` was installed): the watcher raised the tray item and exactly one notification after about 50 s with the text "The keyring is locked...", the dbusmenu has Unlock, Unlock with password..., Test, Restart gnome-keyring... and Check now, "Check now" ran a check at once, a second watcher exited with 3 and logged it, the password window opened and showed its fields (screenshot), killing the watcher removed the tray item and its bus name. The watcher and the window were started by hand from the build folder and stopped again.
  - NOT verified: the hub starting and restarting the watcher (the running hub is the old one, `live-update.sh` was not run; no Xephyr or Xvfb on this machine, so no sandbox), a real password in the window (right or wrong: nothing could type in the window, only the daemon behaviour with a wrong password was checked by hand), the icon going away after an unlock (needs the password), Test and Restart against a real keyring, the reason changing when the daemon stops (the daemon was not stopped: it holds this session's secrets), the notification text rendering, Wayland, the Lister, hotkeys and hub checks of rule 4 of `CLAUDE.md` (nothing in them changed, but the hub change is one new thread started after `applet_host::start()`). The hub ownership answer (rule 2) is in the body of the commit "hub: supervise opusdm-keyring-watch". A probe that times out leaves its worker thread running until the call returns (at most one per 30 s check).
  - Review fixes (a fresh reviewer read the whole branch): every bus connection has a method timeout (`probe::session_bus`) and a stuck probe blocks new ones, so a stuck daemon costs one thread, not one per 30 s check; tray, notification and shutdown calls run bounded (10 s, logged) so a stuck panel or notification daemon cannot freeze the checker; menu actions run one at a time on their own thread (a click while one runs is logged and answered with a notification, "Check now" always works; seen live: Test opens the keyring's own prompt and used to block every later click); the password window cannot be closed or cancelled while the unlock runs (closing killed a restart half way); the wrong-password check looks at the `login` collection (the one the daemon unlocks), not the default one, and no keyring at all is an error; restart waits (10 s) for the new daemon to answer before it unlocks. Tests added: `a_stuck_probe_blocks_new_ones_until_it_ends`, `the_login_collection_decides_not_the_default_one`, `without_a_login_collection_the_default_decides`, `no_keyring_at_all_is_an_error_not_a_success`, `waiting_for_the_service_retries_then_succeeds`, `only_one_action_runs_at_a_time`, `a_stuck_call_does_not_block_the_caller`. Live after the fixes: Test on the locked keyring opened the keyring's prompt, "Check now" ran at once while it was open, a second Test was ignored and logged; dismissing the prompt gave "keyring test failed: prompt dismissed". NOT verified: the cancel guard of the password window (GTK, nothing could drive it), the wait after a restart. Deferred minors: a reason change needs two checks in a row too (flapping), the dead `last` field of `Tracker`, vault errors all shown as "no keyring is running", the tray icon is not recreated when the reason stays the same after the panel restarted, a dead command thread is not supervised, the notification is not closed on Clear, `restart_delay` is duplicated from `applet_host`.
  - Fix found by the owner, live: "Unlock with password..." said "Wrong password" for the right password and every try left a `gnome-keyring-daemon --unlock` process running. Cause: without `GNOME_KEYRING_CONTROL` (neither the hub nor the watcher has it) `--unlock` does not talk to the running daemon, it starts a second one and leaves it behind (`discover_other_daemon: 0`, seen in its own log). Now `--unlock` is run only when the control socket `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/keyring/control` answers a connect, and then with `GNOME_KEYRING_CONTROL` set to that folder; otherwise the window says it cannot reach the control socket and nothing is started. Tests: `no_control_socket_never_starts_a_daemon`, `a_stale_control_socket_never_starts_a_daemon` (the fake daemon leaves a marker, it must not exist), and the fake daemon test now checks the variable it receives. NOT verified: a right password against a live daemon (the control folder of this session was deleted by a stray daemon of my own manual test, so the live path cannot be exercised until gnome-keyring is restarted), and that `--unlock` with the control socket really hands the password to the running daemon and leaves no process (the isolated private-daemon experiment did not work out).
  - Fix (restart): "Restart gnome-keyring..." now restarts `gnome-keyring-daemon.socket` together with the service. The control socket (`$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/keyring/control`) belongs to the socket unit and the daemon inherits it as fd 3; restarting only the service hands it the same socket, so when the control folder was deleted (my manual test did that in this session) it would never come back. Test: `restart_stops_other_wallets_first` checks the step. NOT verified live: that restarting the socket unit recreates the folder and that the unlock then works with a real password.
  - Fix (unlock with password, second round): even with the control socket present `gnome-keyring-daemon --unlock` does NOT hand the password to the running daemon: each try started a full daemon of its own (own `control` and `pkcs11` sockets bound on the same paths, seen with `ss -xlp`) and left it running, and the keyring stayed locked. The `--unlock` call is gone. `gnome::unlock_with_password` now speaks the control socket protocol of `pam_gnome_keyring` (read from `gnome-keyring` `daemon/control/gkd-control-server.c` and `pam/gkr-pam-client.c`): connect, one credentials byte, then `[u32 length][u32 op = 1 unlock][u32 string length][password]` big endian, reply `[u32 8][u32 result]` with 0 ok, 1 denied (wrong password), others failed. No process is ever started to unlock; when the socket does not answer the window says so. After an accepted password the lock state of the `login` collection is still read. Tests with a fake control server: packet bytes, credentials byte first, denied is a wrong password, other codes and a wrong reply size are errors, no socket and a stale socket are errors, a denied password never reads the vault (52 tests in the crate). Verified live by the owner on this machine (09:18): with the right password in "Unlock with password..." the window closed, `login` went from locked to unlocked (`Locked` false) and only the real `gnome-keyring-daemon` was left (no `--unlock` process); the daemon needed nothing after the credentials byte. Before that, restarting `gnome-keyring-daemon.socket` and the service recreated `/run/user/1000/keyring` (the owner ran it by hand). NOT verified: a wrong password against the real daemon (not seen by me; only the fake server covers the denied code), Restart gnome-keyring... (the new socket unit restart and the unlock after it), the tray icon going away after the unlock (the tray showed no keyring item afterwards, the moment of removal was not watched), a logout and login with `libpam-gnome-keyring` (the keyring should then start unlocked and show nothing).
  - Build environment note, not in the repo: on this machine the `-dev` links of `libgtk4-layer-shell`, `libssl`, `libcrypto`, `libpulse`, `libarchive` and `libgstreamer-1.0` are missing, so the GTK crates do not link; the builds above used symlinks in a temporary folder through `LIBRARY_PATH`, the system was not changed.
- Version bump to **0.18.1** (`[workspace.package]` in `Cargo.toml`, inherited by all crates and `xtask`; `Cargo.lock` refreshed) with `scripts/release.sh`. No code change. Verified: `cargo build --release --workspace` passes. NOT verified: nothing else exercised, no behaviour touched.
- `scripts/release.sh` now takes the new version as a required argument (`scripts/release.sh 0.19.0 [-y]`) and does the whole release: checks first (X.Y.Z, greater than the current version, clean tree, branch, tag free locally and on `origin`), asks for confirmation, sets `[workspace.package] version` in `Cargo.toml`, adds a version bump line to `CHANGES.md`, runs `cargo build --release --workspace` (refreshes `Cargo.lock`), commits the three files as "Bump version to X.Y.Z", tags `vX.Y.Z` and pushes the branch and the tag. A failed build or commit puts the files back as in HEAD; a failed push prints the commands to retry. Verified in a throw-away clone with a local bare remote and a stub `cargo`: no argument, bad format, same and lower version, answer "n", failing build (files restored, tree clean), full run (commit, tag and push on the bare remote, `CHANGES.md` line under today's heading), second run refused. NOT verified: a real `cargo build` inside the script, a real push to GitHub and the release workflow it starts.

## 2026-11-11
- Network tray: the spinner of the row being joined now sits over the check label (`gtk::Overlay`) instead of a box sized from the text "MM", so the network name no longer moves right while connecting.
