Verified in the Xephyr sandbox (connect to a saved network: the spinner is in the check column, the name stays aligned with the other rows). NOT verified: the running session.

- Network tray feedback (`crates/panel/src/network_center.rs`, `network_model.rs`, `widgets/network.rs`): while the tray joins a Wi-Fi network, a spinner takes the place of the check mark of its row and of the panel icon (the icon also spins while a device is negotiating, so a connect started with `nmcli` shows too). A new status section between the network list separator and "Configure connections..." shows "Connecting to X", the seconds since the start (updated every second), the steps reached (done, current) and then the outcome: "Connected to X (address)" for 5 s, or "Could not connect to X" with the failed step and the reason in red for 20 s. The old "Connecting to X..." line at the top is gone; the red error line at the top stays for the other errors. The tooltip shows the attempt too. A failed connect is logged and sent as a desktop notification ("Could not connect to X" + reason); a refused password opens the password dialog again with a red line "The password was refused. Try again.". After "Connect" in the password dialog the popover opens again to show the progress. A connect with no end after 90 s is given up with a log line and "No answer from NetworkManager" (the backend waits 50 s). Pure logic in `network_model::Attempt` (steps kept in their natural order, the device signals of a fast connect can arrive out of order). Every failed request of the worker is now logged. Tests: `an_attempt_marks_the_current_step_and_ignores_repeats`, `a_connected_attempt_ends_with_every_step_done`, `a_failed_attempt_shows_where_and_why_and_only_the_first_end_counts`, `only_a_running_attempt_can_be_overdue`, `the_steps_of_a_connect_come_before_its_end`, `a_refused_password_ends_the_connect_with_the_reason`.
  - Verified in the Xephyr sandbox (the tray there drives the real NetworkManager, eth0 stayed up): disconnect and connect of a saved network from the tray (steps, "Connected to os3-guest (address)", section hidden after 5 s); a wrong password on a network never saved (spinner on the row and the icon, popover reopened, steps up to "Checking the password", NetworkManager reason `NO_SECRETS` turned into "The password was refused", dialog asked again with the red line, failed profile deleted, after Cancel the section shows the failure and the tooltip the reason; the notification went to the real session); `nmcli dev disconnect` and `nmcli dev wifi connect` while the popover is open: the list and the address follow within about 1.5 s. Lister: double click on a folder, Backspace, Alt+Left/Right, path bar Enter; Alt+1/Alt+2.
  - NOT verified: the running session (`live-update.sh`), the icon spinner during a slow connect (the connects tested took under a second), the 90 s give-up, a connect that times out, a saved network with a refused key kept by NetworkManager (`replace_stored_key`), the original freeze itself (the keyring was not slow during the tests, so the agent fix is not proven on a real hang), VPN texts, F-keys, the global hotkeys other than Alt+1/Alt+2 (sandbox limits), hub Copy job and second Lister. Sandbox note: closing the modal password dialog with `xdotool windowclose` destroys its X window behind GTK and leaves every hub window deaf to clicks; use the Cancel button.

- Network connect (`crates/network/src/nm.rs`, `model.rs`, `parse.rs`, `backend.rs`): `Backend::connect` takes a `progress` callback and reports each `ConnectStep` (looking for the network, keyring, starting, then the Wi-Fi device states: preparing, joining the access point, checking the password, getting an IP address, checking the connection, connected). The wait now follows the `StateChanged` signal of the Wi-Fi device too and keeps the NetworkManager reasons, so a failure says why (`failure_of`): "The password was refused" (`NO_SECRETS`, returned as the new `NetError::password_refused`, so the caller asks again), DHCP failure, out of range, access point not answering, cancelled, and for a VPN login failed / service failed. Unknown reasons keep the old texts. The connect timeout is 50 s (was 30 s; NetworkManager itself waits 45 s for DHCP). A password typed for a saved network whose key NetworkManager keeps itself (for example a profile made with `nmcli`) is now written into the profile (`replace_stored_key`, `Update`); before, it was ignored and the old refused key was tried again. A refused key that cannot be removed from the keyring is logged. New example `connect` (changes the network). The panel passes a no-op progress in this commit. Tests: `a_refused_key_asks_again_only_on_a_protected_network`, `the_device_reason_wins_and_the_active_reason_explains_a_vpn`, `an_unknown_reason_falls_back_to_the_old_texts`, `device_states_are_reported_once_and_the_failure_reason_is_kept`, `device_states_map_to_connect_steps`, `a_refused_password_asks_again_and_says_why`. Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-network -p opusdm-watch`, `cargo build --workspace`, clippy (no new warnings). NOT verified: a real connect, a real wrong password, the reason numbers against a live NetworkManager, the VPN texts.

- Network tray watcher (`crates/network/src/watch.rs`, `crates/watch/src/lib.rs`): the state can no longer go stale. New `opusdm_watch::wait_for_change` (used only by the network tray watcher; device, power, Bluetooth and the profiles watcher keep `drain_burst`): it reads the state again after 15 s without signals (`REFRESH`), and a burst of signals that never goes quiet ends after 2 s (`MAX_BURST`) instead of waiting forever. A failed state read is logged and tried again up to 3 times (`read_state`) before the session ends (before, one failed read hid the tray for 30 s). `NmBackend::devices` skips, with a log line, a device that vanishes between `GetDevices` and its `GetAll` (a docker or VPN interface going away), instead of failing the whole read. New example `cargo run -p opusdm-network --example watch` prints every watcher message with a time stamp. Tests: `no_tick_for_the_refresh_interval_wakes_anyway`, `a_burst_that_never_goes_quiet_ends_at_the_cap`, `a_short_burst_wakes_once_and_a_dropped_sender_closes`, `a_failed_read_is_tried_again_and_the_last_error_ends_it` (`FakeBackend` can now fail `state`). Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-network -p opusdm-watch`, the `watch` example on the real NetworkManager (states every few seconds, refresh without signals). NOT verified: the tray in the running panel, a real burst cap, a connect with `nmcli` while the tray is open.

- Network secret agent (`crates/network/src/agent.rs`): `GetSecrets`, `SaveSecrets` and `DeleteSecrets` are now `async` and run the keyring work on a short thread of their own (`off_bus`), with a 60 s limit (`KEYRING_TIMEOUT`, below the two minutes NetworkManager waits for an agent). Before, the keyring calls ran inside the executor of the system bus connection shared with the tray watcher and the connect worker: while the keyring was slow or showed an unlock prompt, every bus call and signal of the tray stopped, so "Connecting..." never ended and a connection made by hand with `nmcli` left the icon offline. Every agent failure is now logged. Tests: `keyring_work_answers_through_the_waiter`, `a_stuck_keyring_never_blocks_the_waiter`. Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-network`. NOT verified: a real connect that goes through the agent (a saved network with the key in the keyring), the network manager program and the Settings tab, which use the same agent.

## 2026-11-10
- Version bump to **0.18.0** (`[workspace.package]` in `Cargo.toml`, inherited by all crates and `xtask`; `Cargo.lock` refreshed). No code change. Verified: `cargo build --workspace` passes. NOT verified: nothing else exercised, no behaviour touched.

Called once in each of the 18 executables with an icon (hub, lister, dock, settings, run, shot, terminal, player, viewer, input-config, screen, apps-editor, network-manager, printer-manager, bluetooth-manager, polkit, applets, applet-browser); `opusdm-notify` has no window and no icon. `opusdm-shot` no longer draws its own camera window icon (the toolbar camera is unchanged).

Verified in Xephyr with a sandbox hub: the lister, hub and dock windows carry a 48x48 `_NET_WM_ICON`. NOT verified: the lister behaviour (Enter, double click, hotkeys) after the change.
  - Fixed two typos in `maps.txt` (`desktopA`, `apps-editor.svg` -> `app-editor.svg`).

The hub desktop menu has a new item under "Hide All": "Hide Dock" / "Show Dock" (label from `dock.json`, re-read on the I/O worker at every change of the file and brought back to the main loop through a channel; the write runs on the I/O worker). The `dock_*` fields left `DesktopSettings` (old keys in `desktop.json` are ignored and dropped at the next save of the file); the hub reads `dock.json` once at startup, which also moves the old keys before the supervisor or Settings look at them. `desktop-config` no longer depends on `opusdm-dock` (`apps-editor` still does, for the default menu).   - Verified in the Xephyr sandbox: the desktop menu shows "Hide Dock"; clicking it ends the dock process and the item becomes "Show Dock"; clicking that starts it again. `opusdm-settings`: no Dock tab, the row under PANEL; unchecked + Apply ends the dock, checked + Apply starts it, Apply with the box untouched does not rewrite `dock.json`. NOT verified: Wayland, the live session.

- Dock as a separate process, step 4 (**the switch**): the hub no longer embeds the dock. New `crates/hub/src/dock_host.rs`: thread `opusdm-dock-supervisor` starts `opusdm-dock` (next to the hub binary) only while `dock.json` says `visible`; exit `EXIT_DISABLED` parks it (no polling) until a wake finds `visible` again; `EXIT_ALREADY_RUNNING` retries every 30 s; any other exit restarts after 1 / 2 / 4 s, at most 3 times in 60 s, then it logs that it gives up and waits for the next change of `dock.json`; a missing binary is logged and ends the supervisor; an `EXIT_DISABLED` while `dock.json` says on is counted like a crash (no tight loop). After a start it pings the dock socket (100 ms, up to 5 s, logged if it never answers) and pushes the hidden state, so a dock restarted during Hide All comes back hidden. Thread `opusdm-dock-link` sends `SetHidden` for "Hide All" / "Reveal" (`apply_hidden` -> `widgets.dock.set_hidden`), never the GTK main loop; it also pushes once at hub start, so a dock that outlived the previous hub is revealed. The GTK side watches `dock.json` (`watch_dock_config`) only to wake the supervisor. Removed from the hub: the `opusdm-dock` dependency, `attach`, `set_dock_layout`, `dock_layout()`, `DesktopChanges::dock_layout` (the `dock_*` fields of `desktop.json` are now ignored by the hub). Not as in the spec: `applet_host.rs` was NOT made generic (working code left untouched, rule 1); `dock_host` reuses its `MAX_RESTARTS` / `RESTART_WINDOW` and has its own loop. Ownership (rule 2): the supervisor thread owns the dock `Child` and the wake receiver for the whole run; the link thread owns the link receiver; `DockHost` (senders + hidden flag) is owned by the hub `Widgets`; the `dock.json` monitor is in `Widgets::dock_watch`, its handler holds a `Weak<Widgets>` and logs when it cannot upgrade. Hub exit: both threads die, the dock keeps running, the next hub waits for it. Tests: 9 in `dock_host::tests` (steps, parked start and wake, give-up then wake, disabled-but-on counted, already-running without limit, link coalescing and survival); `desktop_diff` tests updated. `cargo test -p opusdm-hub` (332) and clippy pass.
  - Verified in the Xephyr sandbox (`:7`, Openbox, copy of the config, own `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`), full hub from `target/release`, driven by XTest: the dock runs as its own process started by the supervisor; Hide All hides panel and dock, Reveal shows them; `kill -9` of the dock during Hide All: restarted after 1 s and hidden again; `visible: false` in `dock.json`: the dock exits 4 and the supervisor parks, `visible: true`: started again at once; `SIGSTOP` of the hub: the dock still opens its menu and its Settings window and answers `Ping`; hub `SIGTERM` + restart with the dock alive: still one dock, the new one exits 3 and is retried every 30 s, Hide All / Reveal reach the old dock; killing the old dock: the new hub's supervisor starts a new one at the next retry. Rule 4: Lister double click on a folder, Backspace, Back / Forward / Parent buttons, Alt+Left / Alt+Right, path bar Enter (`/tmp`), global Alt+1 / Alt+2 (after releasing modifiers left stuck by XTest in Xephyr), second Lister (SOURCE / DEST swap), a Copy job (one file copied, identical, then removed).
  - NOT verified: Ctrl+backslash, Ctrl+Shift+space, Ctrl+Print in the sandbox (the tools are single-instance on the shared session D-Bus: the request likely went to the live session, nothing showed in the sandbox), a function run, Wayland, the owner's live session (`live-update.sh` not run).

The dock background menu has a new item "Settings..." under "Add Application..."; it opens "Dock Settings" (`crates/dock/src/settings_window.rs`) with the sections of the old Dock tab (edge and icon size, "Show the dock", background, zoom, pinned apps), Cancel and Apply. Apply writes them with `update_dock_config`; the window stays open and says "Applied." (or the error). A second "Settings..." raises the open window. `dock_ui.rs` moved from `crates/desktop-config` to `crates/dock/src/settings_ui.rs` (re-exported as `opusdm_desktop_config::dock_ui` until the Settings tab goes, step 5). Note: the hub still embeds the dock until step 4, and the embedded dock takes its layout from `desktop.json`: steps 3 and 4 must be deployed together. Test: `the_sections_write_back_what_they_were_seeded_with`.

NOT verified: Choose image, the zoom and opacity controls through the window, Wayland.

- Dock as a separate process, step 2 (`crates/dock`): `opusdm-dock` is now a complete process (`standalone.rs`; `main.rs` only calls `standalone::run`). It loads `dock.json` (exits at once with `EXIT_DISABLED` when `visible` is off, before opening a display), owns the single-instance socket `dock.sock` (`instance.rs`, `Ping` probe; another live dock: exit `EXIT_ALREADY_RUNNING`; bind failure: logged, the dock runs without Hide All), applies the theme mode of `settings.json`, and follows `dock.json` (layout, style; `visible` off -> exit `EXIT_DISABLED`), `desktop.json` (panel edge only) and `settings.json` (theme mode) with file monitors. `server.rs`: thread `opusdm-dock-ipc` accepts `Ping` / `SetHidden`, hands them to the GTK main loop through an `async_channel` and answers within 800 ms (`Error("the dock is busy")` otherwise, so a client never hangs). GTK app id `org.opusdm.Dock`, `NON_UNIQUE` (the socket decides about a second instance). New `is_hidden` / `sync_visibility` in the library. The hub still embeds the dock as before: nothing changes in the session yet. Ownership (rule 2): the file monitors are owned by a thread-local of the main thread for the whole run; the shared `Follow` state by the monitor handlers and the request future; the server thread owns the listener and the sender; a closed channel ends the thread with a log line. Tests: `instance` 3, `server` 4 (state round trip, 50 requests after a garbage and an empty client, busy main loop, gone main loop), `standalone` 1, integration test `tests/disabled.rs` (the binary with `visible: false` and no display exits 4 without binding the socket).
  - Verified in a Xephyr sandbox (`:7`, Openbox, copy of the config): the dock shows with the pinned apps and the migrated edge; `SetHidden(true)` hides it, `SetHidden(false)` shows it, `Ping` answers the state; a second `opusdm-dock` exits 3; editing `dock.json` moves the dock (right -> bottom), changes the icon size, and `visible: false` ends the process. A too long socket path was logged and the dock ran without Hide All.
  - Seen ONCE, not reproduced in 3 tries: right after a combined edge + icon size change, a screenshot 2 s later showed the dock still on the right edge with one big icon cut (likely an in-between state of the Openbox resize; the same `set_layout` code the hub uses). NOT verified: Wayland, a theme mode change live, a panel edge change live, the hub (unchanged).

- Dock as a separate process, step 1 (`crates/ipc/src/dock.rs`, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-08-dock-process-design.md`): `dock.json` grows from the pinned apps to every dock setting (`apps`, `position`, `icon_size`, `visible`, `style`, `layout_migrated`). The first load copies the `dock_*` keys of `desktop.json` once (read as raw JSON; a missing key keeps the default). `apps` is now optional: unset means "never chosen", the dock fills in its default set (an empty list stays empty). Every write goes through `update_dock_config` (read fresh, change own fields, atomic save), so two writers never undo each other. CHANGE: a malformed `dock.json` is no longer replaced by the defaults (it was backed up to `.bak` and overwritten): it is logged and kept, the dock runs with the defaults. New `DockRequest` / `DockReply` protocol and `dock_socket_path` (`dock.sock` next to the hub socket, `OPUSDM_DOCK_SOCK`), `EXIT_DISABLED` (4) in `opusdm_ipc::instance`; not used yet. The embedded dock and the Settings Dock tab now write the apps with `update_dock_config`; the layout still comes from `desktop.json` as before. Tests: 12 in `dock::tests` (migration, once only, malformed files untouched, two writers, clamping, protocol over a socket, timeout). `cargo test` of ipc, dock, theme-config, desktop-config passes. NOT verified: the running app (no behaviour change intended; the migration writes the layout into `dock.json`, nobody reads it yet).

- New `scripts/freeze-report.sh`: run it during a freeze (from a terminal opened with the global hotkey, which keeps working) to save, for every `opusdm-*` process (or the PIDs given), the per-thread wchan/syscall, picom/Xorg/openbox state, the tail of `~/.xsession-errors` and of the journal, and the gdb (else eu-stack) stacks of every thread, in `~/.local/state/opusdm/freeze-reports/<time>/`. Read-only: gdb attaches and detaches.
  - Verified on the live session while the hub was frozen (2026-11-08 08:02): report written for hub, polkit, notify, applets and a terminal; the processes kept running after it.
  - Finding of the 2026-11-08 07:46 hub freeze (old binary, no watchdog): the GTK main thread waits forever in the GL renderer, `gsk_renderer_render` -> EGL -> Mesa `loader_dri3_query_buffer_age` -> `xcb_wait_for_special_event` (a DRI3/Present event from the X server that never arrives). No OpusDM code on the stack. The wifi drop at 07:45 is probably a coincidence (the 2026-11-07 14:58 freeze had no network event). Claude Code terminals froze too (Openbox offered to kill them), likely the same GL wait, NOT verified (they were closed before the stacks were taken). No fix applied yet.

- Group windows (`crates/hub/src/group_window.rs`): the members are read (`entry_for_path`, a `stat` per member) on a worker thread (`opusdm-group-read`) instead of the GTK main thread, so a member on a slow or dead network mount no longer freezes the hub. Same pattern as the desktop folder (`read_entries_async`): thread + `async_channel`, result shown back on the main loop. Used by the three paths that read members: first open, open of an already open window (raise), `refresh` after an add / remove / rename. A ticket from a counter shared by every window drops a result older than the newest read, or meant for a window closed and opened again. A read still running after 5 s is logged (`reading the members takes more than 5s`), and so is its late return; a reader that ends without a result or cannot start is logged. CHANGE: a new window opens empty and fills when the read is back (milliseconds normally). Ownership (rule 2): the worker owns the paths and the sender; the future on the main loop owns the receiver and an `Rc` of the open windows map until the result arrives; no new owner of the windows, no `Weak`. Test: `members_keep_their_order_and_a_missing_one_is_broken`. `cargo test -p opusdm-hub` and clippy pass.
  - Verified in the Xephyr sandbox with `--config-dir` on a copy of the config plus a test group (file, missing file, folder), driven by XTest: double click opens the window with the three members (missing one broken); right click, "Remove from Group" refreshes it to two members, and `strace -f` shows the member `statx` calls on a worker thread, not the main thread; a double click on the icon with the window open raises it with the members. Nothing logged.
  - NOT verified: the slow-read log lines (no slow mount to test), dropping files on a group window (add path, same `refresh`), the live session, the rule 4 checks (Lister, hotkeys, jobs).

- Panel CPU / network sparklines (and the graphs in their details popups): the newest sample is now always on the right edge (new `sample_x`), so in the first minute after a start, while the history is not full, the line enters from the right instead of growing from the left edge. A full history (60 samples) is drawn exactly as before. Not a regression of the previous entry: the draw code was the same, the owner noticed it after the hub restart. Test: `the_newest_sample_is_on_the_right_edge`. Verified in the Xephyr sandbox: screenshot about 10 s after the start shows both short traces on the right edge. NOT verified: the details popup graphs by eye (same draw function), the live session.

- Panel CPU / network meters (`crates/panel/src/widgets/sysmon.rs`): the `/proc/stat`, `/proc/meminfo`, `/proc/net/dev` (and `/proc/loadavg` with the CPU details open) reads moved off the GTK main thread, after the hub freeze of 2026-11-07 (reading `/proc/net/dev` asks the network drivers for their counters; a driver blocking there froze the whole hub). Each tick starts the read with `gio::spawn_blocking` (same pattern as the existing process scan) and the meters are updated when it is back. While a read is still running the tick is skipped, so at most one worker can be stuck; one blocked for 5 s is logged once (`sysmon: reading /proc is blocked for Ns, the CPU and network meters are not updated`) and its return too (`... is back after Ns`). A panicked reader is logged. The computation moved into a pure `Sampler` (first reading only sets the base; `/proc/stat` that cannot be parsed keeps the old CPU base, as before) and `ReadWatch` (one read in flight, report once). CHANGES of behaviour: rates are divided by the real time between two readings instead of a fixed 1 s (same values normally, no false spike after a late read); the first values show about 1 s later when a bar is built (no synchronous read in `build()` any more); the per interface rates are computed every tick (shown only with the popup open, as before). Ownership (rule 2): the widgets and state are in an `Rc<Meters>` owned by the 1 s timer until the bar is destroyed (`root()` gone, `Break`, as before) and by the reading in flight until it is back; no cycle (the popover handlers hold only `ProcWatch`). Tests: `Sampler` 5 (base, measured values, late reading over the real interval, unparsable stat, load only when asked), `ReadWatch` 2, real `/proc` files 1. `cargo test -p opusdm-panel` (186) and clippy (no warning in the file) pass.
  - Verified in the Xephyr sandbox (`:5`): `strace -f -e openat` shows the three files opened by a worker thread, not the main thread; two screenshots 3 s apart show CPU and network values and graphs changing and the memory bar; no `sysmon` or watchdog line in the log.
  - NOT verified: the blocked-read path in the running app (no way to block `/proc/net/dev` on demand; covered by the `ReadWatch` tests only), the details popups opened by a click (no xdotool), a monitor change rebuilding the bar, the owner's live session, the rule 4 interactive checks (Lister, hotkeys, second Lister, jobs; not driven).

- Hub: main-loop watchdog (new `crates/hub/src/watchdog.rs`), after the freeze of 2026-11-07 14:58 (indicators, menu and dock dead, nothing in `~/.xsession-errors`, no crash, so no evidence). A 1 s `glib::timeout_add_local` on the GTK main loop stamps a heartbeat; the `opusdm-watchdog` thread checks it every second. Silent for 10 s: `err_log!` "main loop stalled for Ns" and a report in `~/.local/state/opusdm/hub-stalls/stall-<time>-<pid>.txt` with one line per thread (`/proc/self/task/*/comm`, `wchan`, `syscall`) and the stacks of all threads (`gdb -p <pid> -batch "thread apply all bt"`, fallback `eu-stack`; waited with `reaped::poll_exit` because of the SIGCHLD reaper; SIGTERM after 60 s). When the loop runs again: "main loop is running again after a stall of about Ns". At most 5 reports per run, further stalls are still logged. Started at the end of `activate` (after `build_ui`, so the first UI build is not a stall), once. Ownership (rule 2): the heartbeat `Arc` is held by the never-ending timeout and by the watchdog thread for the whole run; if the timeout is gone the heartbeat ages and a stall is logged. No existing code path changed. Tests: `StallTracker` 3, heartbeat 1, thread summary 2, report path 1; ignored `the_report_holds_the_stacks_of_this_process` (real gdb on the test process) run by hand and passing. `cargo test -p opusdm-hub` and clippy (no warning in the new code) pass.
  - Verified in the Xephyr sandbox (`:5`, `XDG_STATE_HOME` in a scratch folder): no stall reported at rest; an X server grab of 30 s by a test client (realistic block: the main thread waits in `XGetGeometry` from `gdk_display_get_monitor_at_surface`) gave the stall line at 10 s, a report with 100+ thread stacks and that main-thread stack, then the recovery line; the hub kept running after. A gdb `call sleep(20)` on the main thread also gave a report, but only after the release (gdb stopped the watchdog thread too).
  - NOT verified: the owner's live session (not installed with `live-update.sh`), the rule 4 interactive checks (Lister path bar, double click, Backspace, Alt+Left/Right, F-keys, global hotkeys, second Lister, Copy job, function run; no xdotool in the sandbox), `ptrace_scope` 1 (the gdb attach would fail: the report then has the gdb error and the `/proc` thread lines only).
  - Read-only review of the main thread for the freeze cause (no fix): no D-Bus, subprocess or client round trip found on the main thread; left are `/proc` reads every second in `panel/src/widgets/sysmon.rs` (`/proc/net/dev` touches the wifi driver: fits the DHCP lease / rekey at 14:57-14:58, not proven) and X server round trips (`with_x11`, GDK). The next freeze report will tell.

## 2026-11-06

- Version bump to **0.16.0** (`[workspace.package]` in `Cargo.toml`, inherited by all crates and `xtask`; `Cargo.lock` refreshed). No code change. Verified: `cargo build` passes. NOT verified: nothing else exercised, no behaviour touched.

## 2026-11-05

- opusdm-shot, editor: new **Rectangle** tool (filled rectangle in the colour of the tool bar). It is a normal annotation (`Shape::Rect`): select, move, delete, undo/redo; Shift is ignored for it. Tests: `rect_hit_bounds_and_translate_ignore_the_corner_order`, `a_rect_is_filled_inside_and_untouched_outside`. See the next entry for what was verified.

- opusdm-shot, editor: new **Pixelate** (mosaic) and **Blur** tools, next to Rectangle. Same annotation model (`Shape::Pixelate`, `Shape::Blur`): select, move, delete, undo/redo. The Small / Medium / Large buttons set the strength (blocks of 6 / 12 / 24 px; gaussian sigma 4 / 8 / 16). New `effects.rs` (pure `pixelate`, `blur` on `RgbaImage`, using `image::imageops::blur`, no new crate). The effect takes its pixels from the base image under the rectangle (rounded out to whole pixels, cut to the image), so it also hides an annotation drawn below it, and a blur only sees pixels inside its rectangle. `render::draw` now takes the base image and a `PatchCache` (kept in the editor `Ui`, filled per place/effect/size, emptied at 32 entries) so a repaint does not blur again; export uses a fresh cache, same `draw`, so the saved file equals the preview. A failed patch is logged (`err_log!`) instead of skipped silently. Shift (angle lock) is ignored for the three rectangle-like tools. No lifetime or ownership change (one new `RefCell` field in `Ui`). Tests: `effects` 5, `render` 4 (pixelate, blur, covers what is below, cut outside the image), `annotations` 2.
  - Verified in a Xephyr + openbox sandbox with a private `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and no session bus (the binary run directly, because the hotkey exits at once there, see the sandbox notes): overlay selection, editor opens, the three tools draw, Select moves one, Ctrl+Z undoes, Quick save writes a PNG equal to the preview.
  - NOT verified: the Ctrl+Print hotkey path on the real session, Wayland, redo and Delete by eye, zoomed canvas (mouse wheel) with the effects, performance of Blur on a very large area at 4K (the patch is cached, but the first paint of a big area is computed on the UI thread), the Lister, hub and the other global hotkeys of rule 4 (those crates are not touched).

- Screenshot (`opusdm-shot`): new Save requester. The bottom bar now has only **Delay (s)** on the left and **Copy** and **Save** on the right: the Format drop-down (with quality and lossless), the "Save size" field with its percentage and the **Quick save** button are gone (CHANGE: no more one-click save to `~/Pictures/Screenshots`). **Save** opens a modal requester (new `crates/shot/src/save_dialog.rs`): file name proposed as `YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS` (new `export::timestamp_stem`, no `shot-` prefix), format drop-down with quality slider (JPG, lossy WebP) and Lossless switch (WebP), a "Size" section with Width and Height in px (default: the image after the crop; 1 px to 400 %); typing in one field recalculates the other with the aspect ratio (`export::size_for_width` / new `size_for_height`). Its Save (or Enter) opens the system file dialog with `name.ext` (new `export::file_name`, no double extension) and the format filter; Cancel, Esc and closing the window do nothing. The image is resized to exactly the width and height chosen (`export::scale_to_size` replaces `scale_to_width`). Format, quality and lossless of a confirmed requester are offered again at the next Save of the same window (`Ui.save_options`). ## 2026-10-30

- Settings > Advanced, new entry "Firewall" (after Bluetooth) to configure ufw, see `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-30-firewall-design.md`. New library crate `crates/firewall-config`, no binary; `crates/theme-config` only gets one more entry in `advanced.rs`. Picking the entry reads the firewall, so `pkexec` asks the password then (first pick of a Settings run; coming back shows the last read, Reload reads again). Header with the state and Enable or Disable; tabs IN and OUT with the rule count, the default policy and the table (Port, Proto, Action, From or To, Notes); "Add rule..." opens a form (direction, port, tcp, udp or both, allow or deny, optional address or CIDR). Every change goes in a queue: a removal is struck through, an addition is marked "to add", a second click takes it back. Apply runs the queue with one password, in the order removals, additions, on or off, stops at the first failure, keeps what did not run, shows the output of ufw, and never rolls back. A cancelled password changes nothing and keeps the queue. A failed or cancelled first read shows the reason and Retry, never an empty list. No ufw: a message, no password. The whole run is one `pkexec /bin/sh -c SCRIPT` with a constant script: rule words are positional arguments, never evaluated (test with `;`, `$(...)`, quotes on a fake ufw). Rules come from `ufw show added`, not `ufw status`: on the real ufw 0.36.2 `status` lists nothing while the firewall is off, and `show added` gives the exact words that delete a rule (`route delete` for route rules), so no line numbers. Ownership (rule 2): `FirewallTool` owns `Rc<App>`, `Advanced.tools` owns the tool for the whole run of Settings; the result loop and the buttons hold it weakly and log with `err_log!` when it is gone. Tests: rule parsing with the real lines, the form, the queue, the script, and GTK tests on a fake backend (the page is alive after the first read and main loop iterations and Apply still works; cancelled read and Retry; missing ufw; failed and cancelled Apply; Save and Discard from the host). `cargo test -p opusdm-firewall-config -p opusdm-theme-config` passes, clippy shows no new warning. Seen by hand: the real `opusdm-settings` in a private Xephyr with a stand-in `pkexec` that runs the real `/usr/sbin/ufw` without privileges on a private `--rootdir`: list, removal of a commented rule and of a route rule, an addition, Apply (one password call), a failed Apply, a cancelled password, Disable, the OUT tab, the form error, the "Unsaved Changes" question with Save, Network still opening, coming back with no new password. NOT verified: the real `pkexec` and the OpusDM polkit dialog, the live firewall (enable, disable and `ufw status` were faked, because they need root and iptables), a rule with the stale-message fix on screen (covered by code only), the live session and the Lister, hub and hotkey checks of rule 4 (those crates are not touched, `crates/ui` neither). Known limit: a new rule goes last and ufw uses the first match, so a deny after a wider allow has no effect; the form says so.

## 2026-10-27

- Media control bar (`crates/media-ui/src/controls.rs`, used by the viewer and the player), the slider froze after a click on it. Seen in the viewer: after a click on the progress bar the time label went on but the slider handle stayed where it was, so on the next video the bar did not go back to 0 (the time did). Cause, seen with debug prints on a real click in Xephyr: the slider claims the press, so the capture `GestureClick` gets `pressed`, `end` and `stopped` but never `released` (nor `cancel`); the `dragging` flag set by `pressed` was cleared only by `released`, so it stayed set and `set_position` never moved the slider again. Fix: `stopped` clears the flag. Nothing else changed: the seek on click still comes from the slider's `change-value` (still the fast key frame one, as before), the final accurate seek of `released` still does not run when the slider claims the press (not changed on purpose, out of scope). No ownership change, one more closure holding a clone of the same `Rc<Cell<bool>>`. Regression check by hand (a GTK gesture cannot be driven by a unit test): the real `opusdm-viewer` in a private Xephyr, `xdotool` click on the bar, then a click on the next video in the strip: before the fix the handle stayed at the old place with the time at 00:02, after it follows the time and starts from 0. `cargo test -p opusdm-media-ui -p opusdm-viewer -p opusdm-player` and clippy pass. NOT verified: the player app itself (it uses the same bar, only compiled and tested), dragging the handle with the mouse held, the owner's live session.

- Viewer, video thumbnails in the strip. Before, a video button showed the text `VIDEO`. `crates/viewer/src/strip.rs`: the worker thread now makes a video thumbnail with the code the Listers use (`opusdm-iconview`, new dependency of the viewer): the freedesktop cache (`~/.cache/thumbnails/normal`) first, else a frame from `ffmpegthumbnailer` or `ffmpeg` stored in the cache, then scaled to the strip size. `OPUSDM_NO_THUMBS` is honoured. A failure is logged with `err_log!` and the button keeps its `VIDEO` label (no `?`). No ownership, channel or handler change: same worker and same result path as the picture thumbnails. Test: `a_video_gets_a_thumbnail_and_a_cache_entry` (a real clip made with `ffmpeg`, skipped when `ffmpeg` is missing; checks the size, the cache entry and the second call). `cargo test -p opusdm-viewer` passes, clippy shows no new warning. Seen in a private Xephyr with the real `opusdm-viewer` and three clips (red, blue, green): the three thumbnails show with the right colours. NOT verified: `ffmpegthumbnailer` (only `ffmpeg` is installed here), big or slow videos (20 s generator timeout, the strip worker is one thread so a slow video delays the thumbnails after it), the owner's live session.

- New applet `applets/fatto-tasks` (last step of the Fatto Tasks work, read only). Two dropdowns (workspace, board), a Refresh button and the list of the open cards of the board (every card not in the last column, DONE, and not archived or deleted), in column then card order, each row `CODE  title  [column]`; a click loads the full card (`kanban_card_get`) and opens a detail window with `opus.ui.openWindow` (code and title, column, assignees, due date, tags, description cut at 3000 characters, checklists as `[x]` / `[ ]` lines, and an "Open in Fatto" button that opens `<baseUrl>/kanban/<id_board>`); a card whose window is open is not opened twice. Settings: `baseUrl` (default `https://fatto.os3.services`) and `apiKey` (plain text in the applet state file, as for every string setting). It uses the MCP endpoint `POST /api/mcp-kanban` with `Authorization: Bearer` (the only route an API key opens, see the Fatto spec) through `opus.http.post`: `kanban_workspace_list`, `kanban_board_list`, `kanban_board_get_full`, `kanban_card_get`. Errors (no key, 401, HTTP error, JSON-RPC or tool error, network) go to the status line and `console.error`, with the key replaced by `***` if it ever appears in a message. Refresh keeps the selected workspace and board when they still exist; the choice is not remembered across restarts (settings are read only for the script). Tests (`crates/applets/tests/fatto_tasks.rs`, against a fake local MCP endpoint): the open cards are listed in order without the DONE and archived ones, the right tools are called with the key, the detail window has the card fields and is not opened twice, a refused key is shown and the key is never in the UI events, and without a key nothing is sent. - Applets, `opus.ui.openWindow` (third step of the Fatto Tasks applet). `opus.ui.openWindow({ title, width, height, tree, onClose })` opens a secondary window with its own widget tree and returns `{ id, update(nodeId, props), close() }`; at most 8 open at once, no canvas in them. New module `crates/applets/src/secondary.rs` (`OpenSpec::from_json` validates the request, `SecondaryWindows` owns the windows), ops `ui.window.open|update|close` in `runtime.rs` (validated in the script call, then an `Event::Ui` with op `openWindow`, `updateWindow` or `closeWindow`), the handler bookkeeping per window in `prelude.js` (`finishWindow` forgets the window's handlers and runs `onClose` once), and `Applet.secondary` in `host.rs`. Ownership (rule 2): the applet owns its `SecondaryWindows` (a field dropped before the main window), which owns each window and its built tree; the `close-request` closure holds only a `Weak<Applet>`, the widgets hold the runtime's dispatcher (a `Weak` of the runtime); when the applet goes (disable, reload) `Drop` disconnects the handlers and destroys the windows without calling JavaScript. A failed `Weak::upgrade` in the close handler is logged with `err_log!`. A window closed by the user is taken out of the map, then `onClose` is dispatched; a window closed by the script never dispatches it twice (the script side forgets it first). A tree that fails to build is shown in the main window and `onClose` is called so the script forgets the window. Tests: `secondary.rs` (request parsing), `open_window_validates_in_the_script_and_limits_the_count` (runtime), and the GTK section `secondary_windows_live_with_their_applet` (window alive after 50 main loop iterations and after the main tree is shown twice again, its handler fires 3 times, user close and `close()` call `onClose` once each, an update of a closed window changes nothing, the ninth window is an error, a reload destroys them; seen on the display of the session, windows never presented). `docs/usage.md` has the API row. `cargo test -p opusdm-applets` and clippy pass. NOT verified yet: a secondary window actually shown on screen with a window manager (placement, decorations, focus), the owner's live session.
