Tests: `package` 3 (removes only that folder, a symlink keeps its target, names that leave the folder are refused), `model` 1, `browser_gtk` 2 sections (a cancel keeps the applet and sends nothing, a yes closes it through the process then removes the folder and lists again, the other applet stays; a refused close keeps the folder and shows the error). `cargo test -p opusdm-applet-browser` (Xephyr :7) and clippy pass. By hand in the private Xephyr :7 (same setup as the Export / Import check): Weather Clock enabled (its window on the desktop), Delete, the confirmation named the folder, Delete again: the applet window closed, the folder was gone, the row disappeared, "Deleted Weather Clock". NOT verified: the live session, a Cancel in the real dialog (only in the GTK test), deleting an applet while the applets process is busy (the 7 s timeout path).

- Applets, export / import as `.tar.gz`, part 2: the Export and Import buttons in `opusdm-applet-browser` (`crates/applet-browser/src/window.rs`). Every row gets an `Export` button (`export:<id>`), the header an `Import` button (`import-button`) before Refresh. Export: `gtk::FileDialog::save` with the suggested name `<id>.tar.gz`, the extension is added when missing (an existing file that only the added extension would overwrite is refused, because the dialog asked about the other name), the archive is written on a thread, the window says "Exported X to <path>". Import: `gtk::FileDialog::open` filtered on `.tar.gz` / `.tgz`, the archive is unpacked and checked on a thread, then `gtk::AlertDialog` asks "Import X 1.0?" with the permissions of the archive's own manifest (Cancel is the default button) and says if an installed applet will be replaced; only "Import" installs. The applet arrives disabled (its state file is not part of the archive); a replaced applet is reloaded with a `Reload` request so a running one does not keep the old code. A cancel of any dialog shows nothing and installs nothing. Failures show the error line and `err_log!`. The applets process is not touched: `List` already reads the folder from disk, so Refresh shows the new applet. Ownership: `Browser` gets the window only as a `glib::WeakRef` (parent of the dialogs); no new strong reference, handlers and completions hold the `Weak<Browser>` and log a failed upgrade (window closed while a dialog or the file work was open). The dialogs are answered by `Answers` (canned, tests only: `window::open_in(.., Some(answers))`), `open_with` is unchanged. The staging folder of a cancelled or failed import is removed. Tests: 7 new sections in `browser_gtk.rs` (export writes the archive and a cancel does nothing; a failed export shows the error; import installs disabled after the confirmation and lists again; a declined import leaves nothing; a bad archive shows the error; importing over an installed applet replaces and reloads it; the window closed during an export and an import). `cargo test -p opusdm-applet-browser` (with a private Xephyr :7), `cargo clippy -p opusdm-applet-browser --all-targets` pass. Checked by hand in a private Xephyr :7 with Openbox, a debug `opusdm-applets` on a private socket and a copy of both samples, real dialogs: Export of Weather Clock to `<dir>/wc` gave `wc.tar.gz` with `weather-clock/`, `applet.json`, `main.js`, and the notice; Import with Cancel copied nothing; Import after deleting the applet by hand showed "Import Weather Clock 1.0?" with "Permissions requested: Network access", and Import installed it disabled and listed it. NOT verified: the live session (`live-update.sh` not run), replacing an applet that is running, an archive that asks for `exec` seen in the real dialog (only in unit tests), a very large archive, Wayland.

- Version: OpusDM goes from 0.12.0 to 0.15.0. `[workspace.package] version` in `Cargo.toml` is the only source (every crate uses `version.workspace = true`); `Cargo.lock` follows (the 46 workspace crates, no third-party crate touched). The version shown by `opusdm_ui::version::VERSION`, the hub script call and the applets `opus` surface come from `CARGO_PKG_VERSION`, so they change by themselves. `cargo check --workspace --all-targets` passes. NOT done: no release build, no .deb, no live update.

- Applets, export / import as `.tar.gz`, part 1: the logic, no window change yet. New `crates/applet-browser/src/package.rs` (pure, no GTK): `export_applet` writes the applet folder as `<id>/...` into a `.tar.gz` (regular files and folders only, a symlink is skipped with a log line; settings and position are not part of the folder, so they are not in it; a failure removes the partial file); `stage_archive` unpacks into a hidden `.import-*` folder inside the applets folder (same filesystem, and names starting with `.` are not applets) and checks it: one top-level folder (its name is the id), no absolute path or `..`, no file at the top level, symlinks and devices skipped, at most `MAX_UNPACKED_BYTES` (64 MB), a valid `applet.json` (the existing `manifest::parse`) and the entry script present; `Staged::install` moves it into place, keeping a replaced applet aside and putting it back if the move fails; dropping a `Staged` removes the staging folder. `model.rs`: texts of the import confirmation (`import_question`, `import_detail`, `permissions_text`: Network access / Read and write files / Run commands, from the manifest of the archive). `opusdm_applets::discovery::MANIFEST_FILE` is now `pub`; the crate gets `tar`, `flate2` and `tempfile` as dependencies. Tests: 15 new unit tests, 12 in `package` and 3 in `model` (round trip, unsafe paths, wrong layout, bad manifest, missing entry, symlinks, size cap, replace, drop). NOT verified: nothing in the window yet (part 2), so no button exists; nothing run in the sandbox or the live session.

## 2026-10-26
- Applets: long text no longer pushes an applet window off screen. Found in a final sandbox check: `site-news` grew to 519 px wide (GTK labels without ellipsizing have a minimum width equal to their whole text) at the default x 1236, so its right edge was at 1755 on a 1600 px screen and the edit handle and gear were unreachable; the clamp ran only at open and on monitor changes. (1) `crates/applets/src/widgets.rs`: `list` row labels ellipsize at the end (`pango::EllipsizeMode::End`), stay left-aligned, `hexpand` and get the full text as tooltip (also after an `items` update); `label` nodes wrap (`set_wrap(true)`, `WrapMode::WordChar`) with their alignment unchanged; button labels unchanged. (2) `crates/applets/src/applet_window.rs`: the read-back goes through the new `_accept_geometry` (also `AppletWindow::accept_read_back`, for tests): a geometry inside the monitors behaves as before; one partly outside is clamped with `placement::clamp_to_monitors` on the current monitors, made the shared target, moved with `_prepare_at` (the `reclamp` path) and saved once through `on_moved`, logged with `debug_log!`; when the clamped rect is already the target nothing is moved or saved again (no save every tick for a stuck window). The placement retries after map first call `_fit_allocation`: when GTK allocated the window larger than the target, the target becomes the real size clamped to the monitors (pure `_fitted`), before it is pinned; not saved there. No new closures: the retry closure (already a `WeakRef` of the window, failed upgrade logged) now also holds the applet id string. Tests: `gtk_widget_checks` gets `long_text_does_not_widen_the_window` (row ellipsize/xalign/hexpand/tooltip, label wraps WordChar, centered kept, 500 char list and label minimum width < 200 px), unit tests for `_fitted`, and `host_gtk.rs` gets `a_read_back_outside_the_monitors_is_clamped_and_saved_once` (bare window and through the host: clamped target, one save, same read-back again does nothing, no monitor = not clamped). Verified: `cargo test -p opusdm-applets`, `cargo clippy -p opusdm-applets --all-targets` (no warnings), `cargo build --release -p opusdm-applets`; private Xephyr :7 1600x1000 with Openbox and both samples (site-news on hnrss.org): site-news 340x360 at +1236+64 with ellipsized titles, weather-clock 260x320 at +1284+96, both inside; edit mode on through the socket: handle and gear visible on screen; `xdotool windowmove` of site-news to x 1450 then activate: logged "read back at (1450, 64, 340, 360), not inside ... clamped to (1260, 64, 340, 360)", window at +1260, state saved once; a temp applet with a 600 px canvas and a 300 px manifest opened at x 1212 and was clamped after map to (1000, 128, 600, 200); a site-news error text with a long URL wraps inside 340 px. NOT verified: the live session (`scripts/live-update.sh`), the hub and Lister (not touched), a window that grows after its placement retries while never active (the read-back runs only while the window is active, so such growth is still only corrected on the next activation or restart), a window whose minimum size is larger than the monitor.
- Applets, final branch review fixes. (1) `crates/applets/src/host.rs`: `save_geometry`, `set_enabled` and `disable_from_window` read the state with `state::read_in` (as `apply_settings`), so a state file that exists but cannot be read is never replaced by a default state (which dropped the settings and the position, and made `save_geometry` write `enabled = false`): `save_geometry` and `SetEnabled` return the error (logged by the geometry handler and `Host::handle`) and change nothing, `disable_from_window` logs it and still closes the applet; a missing file is still created; `save_geometry` of an open applet always writes `enabled = true`. (2) `applets/site-news/main.js`: a numeric reference outside the Unicode range (`&#99999999;`) is kept as raw text instead of failing the whole feed, and the empty feed URL branch bumps `fetchSerial` first, so an answer in flight cannot replace the "Set the feed URL" message. (3) `settings_form.rs`: a number field emptied (or blank) keeps its previous value instead of becoming 0 when Apply takes the focus (`connect_input` returns the current value; not an input error, because with the default `Always` update policy GTK would use an unset value). (4) `crates/applet-browser/src/window.rs`: a failed `SetEnabled` keeps the flip-back and error line, then lists again (as a failed Reload), so a change the busy process applied after the client timeout shows its real state. (5) `opusdm_jscore::http`: `timeoutMs` is clamped to `MAX_TIMEOUT_MS` (120000), documented in `docs/usage.md`; this applies to the Lister scripts too (same `request_from_json`). (6) `scripts/live-update.sh`: without `--all`, `opusdm-applets` is stopped with `opusdm-hub` (the new hub's supervisor starts it again). (7) `host_gtk.rs` and `browser_gtk.rs` check the display before `gtk::init` and write `SKIPPED: no display ...` straight to stderr (an `eprintln!` of a passing test is captured by libtest). (8) The three runaway runtime tests (loop, uncatchable loop, recursion) get their own 20 s deadline; the elapsed assertion is gone. Tests: new host_gtk sections `an_unreadable_state_file_is_never_overwritten` (mode 000 state file, or a folder when running as root) and `an_emptied_number_field_keeps_its_value`, browser_gtk section `a_failed_set_enabled_lists_again_and_shows_the_real_state`, samples tests for the bad entity and the emptied feed URL, jscore `request_from_json_clamps_a_huge_timeout`; each was RED before its fix. `cargo test --workspace`, clippy of the three crates and the release build pass. NOT verified: nothing was run in the live session or the sandbox (no applet moved, enabled or closed by hand, no settings window used with the keyboard, the browser not opened against the real process); `scripts/live-update.sh` was not run (only `bash -n`); the err_log lines of the unreadable state paths were seen in the test output, not asserted.
- Applets, Task 16: "Edit Applets" in the desktop menu and in Settings, packaging lists, documentation. Hub (`crates/hub/src/desktop_menu.rs`): new `APPLET_BROWSER_BIN`, pure `applet_browser_bin_in(dir)` (`opusdm_ipc::instance::sibling_bin_in`: next to the hub, else `PATH`) and `launch_applet_browser()` (spawns it detached, a spawn failure is logged with `err_log!`); `build_desktop_menu` appends `Edit Applets` after `Edit Theme`, so it is the last entry; nothing else in the hub changed (no ownership or lifetime change: a spawn on click; the hub does not depend on the applet crates). The entry is shown on every session; on Wayland the browser says "The applets process is not running". Settings (`crates/theme-config/src/tools.rs`): the existing `TOOL_BUTTONS` under the Advanced list get a sixth button, `Edit Applets`, `Action::Spawn("opusdm-applet-browser", &[])`, started by the existing `spawn` / `sibling_bin` (next to `opusdm-settings`, else `PATH`). Scripts: `opusdm-applet-browser` added to `BINARIES` of `build-release.sh` (and its header comment now lists `opusdm-applets` and `opusdm-applet-browser`), to the hidden desktop entries (`org.opusdm.AppletBrowser.desktop`, for the Wayland icon like the other apps) and to `PROCESSES` of `live-update.sh` (`printf '%.*s' 15` cuts it to `opusdm-applet-b`, the kernel name, checked with `ps`). `build-deb.sh` and `Dockerfile.deb` copy `dist/bin/*` and `xtask` has no binary list, so nothing to change there. Docs: `docs/usage.md` new 3.41 Applets (folders, manifest fields, state file, X11 only, the Applet Browser, Alt + drag / Alt + right drag, edit mode, closing disables, the applet `opus` surface, widget types and props, canvas methods and what is not supported, entry `onChange` and the intermediate `""`, sample install), `opus.http.get` row in the 3.39 Lister scripts table, Edit Applets in 3.28b; `docs/FEATURES.md` (the feature highlights; the root `FEATURES.md` is the Directory Opus gap list, not touched) 6.8 Applets and a feature-grid line; `OpusDM-Features-Guide.md` Step 17.5 (in Italian, as the rest of that guide). Tests: `the_applet_browser_binary_is_looked_up_next_to_the_hub_then_on_path` (hub) and `edit_applets_is_the_last_button_and_starts_the_applet_browser` (settings), both seen failing first. The hub tests have no GTK harness for `build_desktop_menu`, so the menu order was checked by hand. Checked in a private sandbox (Xephyr :7 1280x800 + Openbox with `openbox-rc.xml`, branch release build, the hub under `dbus-run-session` with a copied config, both samples enabled in a temporary state dir, private hub and applets sockets): the hub started `opusdm-applets` and both samples showed; Alt + left drag moved site-news and the new geometry was saved in its state file; right click on the desktop shows `Edit Applets` as the last entry, under `Edit Theme`, and opens the browser; the Edit mode switch shows and hides the dashed border, handle and gear on both applets; Settings > Advanced shows `Edit Applets` as the last button under the list and it opens the browser, and with the browser minimized it raises the same window (the second process exits), the same from the desktop menu; closing the browser ends its process. Rule 4 in the sandbox: Lister opened with Alt+n, Enter in the path bar, double click on a folder, Backspace, Alt+Left / Alt+Right, a second Lister with SOURCE / DEST, an F5 Copy job between two scratch folders, Alt+2 / Alt+1 (`_NET_CURRENT_DESKTOP` 0 -> 1 -> 0) and a global `script:` hotkey (Ctrl+F9) all worked. NOT checked: Ctrl+backslash, Ctrl+Shift+space and Ctrl+Print (their tools exit at once in Xephyr, known sandbox limit), the gear's settings window and Alt + right drag resize (not exercised this time), Wayland. The owner waived the live-session check: the running session was NOT updated or checked (`live-update.sh` not run). Seen in the sandbox, not changed here: the desktop menu opens under an applet that covers the click point (applets stack above the desktop window), and at 1280x800 the Settings window is taller than the screen on the Advanced tab with the Network tool open (845 px; the new button adds about 40 px of it).
- Applets, Task 15: `opusdm-applet-browser`, the window that lists the applets and drives the applets process over its socket. New crate `crates/applet-browser` (package `opusdm-applet-browser`, lib `opusdm_applet_browser`, bin `opusdm-applet-browser`, added to the workspace members; it depends on `opusdm-applets` for the protocol types instead of copying them). `model.rs` (pure): `display_name` (manifest name, else the folder id), `title`, `describe` ("Clock 1.2 - Shows the time (broken: ...)"), `host_unreachable_text()` ("The applets process is not running") and `empty_text(dir, home)` ("No applets in ~/.config/opusdm/applets"). `window.rs`: `open(app)` / `open_with(app, socket_path, present)` build one row per applet (name or id with the version, the description, an enabled `gtk::Switch`, a Reload button and the manifest or runtime error in red), an "Edit mode" switch (`GetEdit` / `SetEdit`), a Refresh button, an error line and, instead of the rows, the "not running" message or the empty-folder hint. Every socket request runs on its own `std::thread` (`request_at` blocks up to 1 s / 7 s) and comes back through `async_channel` + `glib::spawn_future_local`; while it is in flight its control is insensitive; a failure re-enables it, flips a switch back to its previous value (without sending a request), shows the error and logs it with `err_log!`. A failed Reload also lists again, so the row shows the script error. Each user action clears the previous error. Ownership: the window's destroy handler holds the only strong `Rc<Browser>`; handlers and completions hold a `Weak` (and `glib::WeakRef`s to their widgets) and log a failed upgrade (window closed with a request in flight). `main.rs`: application id `org.opusdm.AppletBrowser`, unique, `window::activate` opens the window or only `present()`s the open one; closing the browser does not change the edit mode. Tests: 5 pure model tests and one GTK test process (`tests/browser_gtk.rs`, fake server on a temp `/tmp` socket, windows never presented) covering the list, `SetEnabled`, Reload, `SetEdit`, the empty hint, no server, a refused change (switch flips back, error shown, a refused Reload lists again), activate twice (one window), and a window destroyed with requests in flight; removing the owner of `Rc<Browser>` makes the GTK test fail (checked by hand). Verified in a private Xephyr :7 with Openbox, the release `opusdm-applets` and both samples in a temp config: the list, enabling Weather Clock (window appears) and disabling it (window goes), enabling Site News, Reload of a broken and then fixed Site News script (error in the error line and in red in the row, then gone), edit mode on (dashed border, handle and gear on both applets) and off, a second launch (exits at once, still one window), and Refresh after killing `opusdm-applets` (the "not running" message). NOT verified: the real session (no install, no panel or menu entry yet), a busy applets process (the 7 s timeout path), Wayland.
- Applets, Task 14 fix round 1: the applets supervisor polls slowly and backs off before restarts. In `crates/hub/src/applet_host.rs` the real supervisor waits with `_wait_polling(child, SUPERVISOR_POLL)` (500 ms, on `opusdm_ipc::reaped::poll_exit`) instead of `reaped::wait` (10 ms), so it no longer wakes 100 times a second for the whole session (`reaped.rs` unchanged). Each counted restart now sleeps `restart_delay(n)` first (1 s, 2 s, 4 s for the 1st, 2nd and 3rd restart in the window, `RESTART_BACKOFF_BASE` doubled) through the injected `sleep`; already-running retries keep their 30 s. `start()` is gated on `opusdm_ipc::session::is_x11()` instead of `!is_wayland()`, so a hub without a display (TTY, ssh) does not spawn and give up; the skip is still one debug log. No ownership change. New tests `restart_delay_doubles_from_one_second`, `the_supervisor_polls_slowly`, `wait_polling_returns_the_exit_code`; the supervisor tests now assert the recorded backoff sleeps (RED with stubs, 7 failed, then GREEN). `cargo test -p opusdm-hub` green, no new clippy warning, `cargo build --release -p opusdm-hub` green. Not checked: no sandbox run in this round, the X11 gate is checked by reading the code only (`is_x11` reads the environment once, there is no unit test for it), and the owner's live session check is pending.
- Applets, Task 14: the hub starts and supervises `opusdm-applets`. New `crates/hub/src/applet_host.rs`: `start()` (one call in `main.rs`, right after the `opusdm-notify` start; X11 only, on Wayland it only logs at debug level; `--no-startup-apps` does not disable it) spawns the detached thread `opusdm-applets-supervisor`, which owns the `Child` (started with `sibling_bin_in` next to the hub, no arguments, the hub's environment) and waits with `opusdm_ipc::reaped::wait`. Every exit is logged with `err_log!` and the status; a crash (or a spawn error other than "not found") is restarted at once while `may_restart` allows it (`MAX_RESTARTS` 3 in `RESTART_WINDOW` 60 s), then it logs once that it gives up and the thread ends; a missing binary is logged once and the thread ends. An exit with `EXIT_ALREADY_RUNNING` (restarted hub, old `opusdm-applets` still alive) is not counted: logged once per streak ("already running, waiting for it"), retried every `ALREADY_RUNNING_RETRY` (30 s) without limit, and a child that lives past a 2 s grace after such a streak logs that it took over. The decision is the pure `next_action(status, history, now) -> Action { Restart, WaitAndRetry, GiveUp }`; the loop `supervise(spawn, wait, sleep, timing)` takes them injected. The hub never waits for the thread and holds no handle to it or to the child; when the hub exits the child keeps running. `scripts/build-release.sh` `BINARIES` and `scripts/live-update.sh` `PROCESSES` gain `opusdm-applets` (`build-deb.sh` copies `dist/bin/*`, no list). 9 new tests (RED then GREEN), `cargo test -p opusdm-hub` green, no new clippy warning, release build green. Sandbox check in a private Xephyr :7 with Openbox, the branch hub, a temp config with both samples enabled and private sockets: both applets shown; `opusdm-applets` killed by PID came back; killed 4 times quickly, the hub logged three restarts and then "giving up", the supervisor thread was gone and the hub kept working (Alt+N opened a Lister: double click on a folder, Backspace, Alt+Left, Alt+Right, Enter in the path bar all worked; Alt+2 / Alt+1 switched desktops); hub killed while `opusdm-applets` lived, the new hub logged "already running, waiting for it", the old `opusdm-applets` killed and the new hub's one "took over" 29 s later with both applets back. Not checked: the owner's live session (`scripts/live-update.sh --all`, pending, done by the owner), Wayland (only the debug log path), Ctrl+backslash / Ctrl+Shift+space / Ctrl+Print (sandbox limit), a second Lister, SOURCE/DEST, Copy job and a function run were not exercised in this sandbox run.
- Applets, Task 13: the settings window generated from the manifest. New `crates/applets/src/settings_form.rs`: `build(defs, values, on_apply)` makes a normal decorated `gtk::Window` (not the applet X11 recipe, transient for nothing) with one `opusdm_ui::labeled_row` per setting (`gtk::Entry` for string, `gtk::SpinButton` for number with range -1e9..1e9, 0 digits or 2 when the default or the value is fractional, `set_numeric(true)`; `gtk::Switch` for bool; `gtk::DropDown` of the choices), a hidden red status line and Close + Apply; with no setting it says "This applet has no settings" and has only Close. Apply reads the fields into `(key, text)` pairs and the pure `collect(defs, raw)` validates them (finite numbers, `true`/`false`, a choice inside its list; the error names the key); a validation error is shown in the window and logged. Close emits `close-request` (as the title bar close) then destroys. `Host::open_settings` (the gear) replaces the Task 12 stub: at most one settings window per applet (`settings_windows` map with its `close-request` handler), a second gear click presents the same window, title "<applet name> settings", presented only when `HostConfig.present_windows`. Apply goes to `Host::apply_settings`: the new `state::read_in` re-reads the state file (a file that exists but cannot be read is an error: nothing is written, the error is shown in the window and logged, the window stays open; a malformed one is backed up and gives the default as `load_in`), merges the new values into `settings` (keeps `enabled`, geometry and undeclared keys), saves, then `Runtime::deliver_settings(effective_settings(..))` when the runtime runs. Ownership: the host map owns the windows; Apply and the `close-request` handler hold only a `Weak<Host>` (failed upgrade logged); disable, reload and the applet window close destroy the settings window (handler disconnected first), `Drop for Host` destroys the rest. Found while testing: in GTK 4.20 `gtk_window_destroy` emits `destroy` only when the last reference goes, so the host forgets a closed window in `close-request`, not in `destroy`. `Applet` now keeps its `Manifest`. Tests: `collect` (3 lib tests), `state::read_in` (1), and GTK sections in `tests/host_gtk.rs`: `the_form_shows_the_current_values`, `apply_saves_the_state_and_calls_on_settings`, `an_invalid_value_is_shown_in_the_window_and_not_saved`, `apply_with_an_unreadable_state_file_writes_nothing`, `an_applet_with_no_settings_says_so_in_the_window`, `the_gear_twice_opens_one_window`, `disabling_the_applet_closes_its_settings_window`. Verified by hand in a private Xephyr :7 + openbox with both samples: gear opens "Weather Clock settings" with Rome/C/30; a second gear click opens no second window; city Milan + refresh 15 + Apply: the clock shows Milan 11 C after its fetch and the state file keeps enabled/x/y; letters cannot be typed in the number field; Close, the title bar X, Reload and SetEnabled false all close the settings window and the gear opens a new one. NOT verified: the running session (not deployed), the site-news Apply with a real feed URL, a fractional number setting by hand, the window look under the user's theme. Known: an emptied number field becomes 0 (GTK parses it on focus-out before Apply reads it); the value 0 is visible in the field before Apply.
- Applets, Task 12: edit mode with a dashed border, a drag handle and a settings gear (variant A of the design). New `crates/applets/src/edit_mode.rs`: `EditMode` (an `Rc<Cell<bool>>` plus listeners; `set` calls them only on a change, from a copy so a listener may unsubscribe; `subscribe` / `unsubscribe` / `listener_count`), owned by the host for the whole run and shared with every window; the socket's `SetEdit` reaches it through the request loop (`Host::set_edit`), so `SetEdit` / `GetEdit` now work end to end. Not saved: off after a restart. `applet_window::open` now takes the `EditMode` and a `WindowHooks { on_close, on_moved, on_settings }`; the content sits in a `gtk::Overlay` with a dashed frame (`can_target(false)`) and, top right, a small box with two flat 16 px icon buttons (`list-drag-handle-symbolic`, gear `emblem-system-symbolic` else `preferences-system-symbolic`, a text label when the theme has neither; tooltips "Move", "Settings"). Outside edit mode both are `set_visible(false)` and the class `opusdm-applet-edit` is off the overlay. Style in the new `applet_window.css` (`include_str!`, one `CssProvider` per display at APPLICATION priority, rules scoped to the applet edit classes, parse errors logged): 2 px white dashes with a 1 px dark line inside, dark pill behind the icons. Each window subscribes one listener (weak refs to its widgets, `err_log!` if gone) and unsubscribes it in `Drop`. Handle drag: a `GestureDrag` (capture phase, claims the press) whose closures hold only a `Weak` of the window's drag state; the pointer is read in root coordinates (`x11_window::pointer_position`, `QueryPointer`), the rect is `placement::drag_on_monitors` (new: `dragged` + `clamp_to_monitors`), the window is moved with the new `x11_window::move_to` (`pin_geometry` only), the target follows on every step, the Task 11 read-back is paused during the drag, and `on_moved` (the host's `save_geometry`) runs once at the end (not for a press without a move). The gear calls `Host::open_settings(id)` through a `Weak<Host>`, only while edit mode is on: KNOWN STUB, it only logs at debug level until Task 13. New `Host::edit_mode`, `Host::with_window`. Tests: 4 `EditMode` unit tests and `a_handle_drag_moves_by_the_delta_and_stays_on_the_monitors` (RED with stub bodies, then green); 5 new `host_gtk.rs` sections (hidden outside edit mode and a hidden gear does nothing, edit on shows border and icons incl. `HostMessage::Edit` through the request loop, off hides them and a closed window unsubscribes, the drag steps update the target, clamp and save once incl. the state file through the host, a window opened or reloaded in edit mode starts in it). All 140 lib + host_gtk (17 sections) + 3 samples tests pass on a private Xephyr; clippy clean; release build ok. Checked by hand in a private Xephyr :7 with Openbox (`openbox-rc.xml`) and both samples: `SetEdit(true)` showed the border and icons on both windows; a plain (no Alt) xdotool drag of the clock handle moved it from (964, 96) to (564, 396) and saved that once; a drag past the bottom right corner stopped at (1020, 480) (clamped) and was saved; a gear click logged the stub line; `SetEdit(false)` hid icons and border, and a click where the gear was did nothing; Alt + drag still moved and saved the clock; after a restart `GetEdit` is false and the clock reopened where it was left. NOT verified: the owner's real session, multi-monitor, Wayland, the settings window (Task 13).
- Applets, Task 11: saved position and size, clamped to the monitors. Strategy A from the Task 10 spike: Openbox does Alt + left drag (Move) and Alt + right drag (Resize) of the applet windows itself (its `Frame` mouse bindings; GTK gets no button event), so the app does not track the pointer: it reads the geometry back. New `x11_window::current_geometry(window)` (frame top-left on the root = client origin by `TranslateCoordinates` minus the left/top `_NET_FRAME_EXTENTS`, client size by `GetGeometry`; logs with `err_log!` on failure, once per process when not X11). `applet_window` reads back on every `is-active` change and every `READBACK_INTERVAL_MS` (500 ms) while the window is active (a glib timeout holding a `WeakRef`, removed when the window deactivates or is destroyed, logs if the window is gone); a geometry different from the current target becomes the target and goes to the host, which writes `x, y, width, height` into the state file only when they changed (`Host::save_geometry`, at most once per read-back). Read-backs are skipped (debug log) until the last placement retry after a map, so the window manager's first placement is never saved. The realize/map handlers and the 80/400/1000 ms retries now pin the shared current target (`Rc<Cell<Rect>>`), not the open-time geometry: a re-map or a monitor change no longer undoes a user move (a drag in the first second after open still snaps back to the open position, checked). The plan's own Alt + right drag resize is NOT built: Openbox resizes; `resizable: false` keeps GTK's fixed size hints (min = max) so Openbox does not resize it, and `minWidth`/`minHeight` are now a GTK size request on the window and a floor on the saved size. New pure `placement` module: `clamp_to_monitors` (inside the monitor with most overlap, else the first; shrinks to fit; unchanged with no monitor), `dragged` (kept for the Task 12 handle drag, unused here), `at_least`. The geometry is clamped at open (saved geometry against the current GDK monitors) and on every monitor change (`Host::watch_monitors` at startup, through `opusdm_ui::watch_monitors`: list `items-changed` and each monitor's `geometry`; handlers hold a `Weak<Host>`, a failed upgrade is logged); a clamp moves the window but does not rewrite the saved position. `x11_window::default_position` now takes the size and keeps y above the bottom of the first monitor. New dependency `x11rb` in `opusdm-applets` (already in the workspace; `Cargo.lock` +1 line). Tests: 7 `placement` unit tests, a `default_position` y test, two `host_gtk` sections (`the_host_saves_a_changed_geometry_once`, `a_monitor_change_clamps_the_open_windows`); RED then GREEN, clippy clean, release build ok. Verified by hand in a private Xephyr :7 (1280x800) with Openbox and the repo `openbox-rc.xml`, both samples, temp config/state: open at the default places and no write after settling or after a click; Alt + left drag saved the exact new x/y; `Reload` and a kill/restart of `opusdm-applets` kept the position; Alt + right drag on Site News saved the new size and stopped at the minimum height 160 (width at the content minimum 286); the non-resizable Weather Clock could not be resized and nothing was saved; an Openbox restart kept a moved window; saved x 5000 opened inside the screen (1020, 480) without rewriting the file; `xrandr -s 1024x768` on :7 re-clamped the clock to (764, 448) live. NOT verified: the owner's real session, multi-monitor (only the unit tests), Wayland.
- Applets, Task 10: X11 window recipe for applet windows, from a spike in a private Xephyr :7 (Openbox with the repo `openbox-rc.xml`, picom with the repo `picom.conf`, the branch hub with a temp copy of the config, `opusdm-applets` with both samples enabled). New `crates/applets/src/x11_window.rs`: `prepare(window, x, y, w, h)` writes `_NET_WM_WINDOW_TYPE_NORMAL` and the states `BELOW`, `STICKY`, `SKIP_TASKBAR`, `SKIP_PAGER` as properties, sends them again as EWMH messages with the all-desktops request, then pins the geometry (`ConfigureWindow` + `_NET_MOVERESIZE_WINDOW`), the same recipe as the dock; not realized, not X11 (logged once per process, so Wayland is unchanged) or an X error is logged with `err_log!`, never a panic. `applet_window::open` calls it on realize, on map and on the `PLACEMENT_RETRY_MS` retries after map (weak window reference, `err_log!` if gone), at the saved `x` / `y` or, without one, at `default_position`: top right area of the first monitor (24 px from the right, 64 px from the top), cascaded 32 px per window the application already has. `opusdm-applets` now depends on `opusdm-ui` (as the dock and notify do). Spike results (xprop, wmctrl, xwininfo, xdotool, screenshots): (a) above the hub's wallpaper and icon field: worked; (b) below normal windows: worked (an xterm stays above after clicking the applet; the applet is raised only inside the BELOW layer); (c) on every desktop: worked (`_NET_WM_DESKTOP` 0xFFFFFFFF, still viewable on desktops 3 and 5 while the xterm was unmapped); (d) no taskbar entry: worked (the panel taskbar listed both applets before the recipe and none after; not in the pager either); (e) transparent background: works but is not X11 work (the window already has a 32 bit ARGB visual; a throwaway `background: transparent` CSS showed the desktop through the clock under picom), not wired, `manifest.window.transparent` is still unused; (f) click-through on transparent areas: the mechanism works (a throwaway `gdk::Surface::set_input_region` of the left half let a click on the right half select the desktop icon below) but not kept, since the region would have to follow the painted alpha on every redraw; (g) Alt + left drag: Openbox moves the window itself (moved by exactly the drag delta, with and without the recipe, states kept); GTK got no button event during the drag (a plain click did reach it), only `is-active` false at the grab and true at its end. Task 11 strategy: A, read the position back from X11 after Openbox moves the window (`TranslateCoordinates` of the xid, what `xwininfo` showed), not B (own pointer tracking, which never sees the press). Test: new `host_gtk.rs` section `prepare_on_a_window_without_an_x11_surface_does_not_panic` (failed to compile before the module existed), two unit tests for `default_position`; all crate tests pass, clippy clean. Concerns: applets share the BELOW layer with the dock, so one can cover the other; picom's `inactive-opacity` dims applets that are not focused (almost always); picom draws its shadow around the full window rect of a transparent applet; the 1 s placement retries snap a window dragged within its first second back. NOT verified: the owner's real session (pending, Task 16), multi-monitor placement, Wayland (only the not-realized path ran in the test).
- Applets, Task 9: the two sample applets in `applets/` (`site-news`, `weather-clock`, each `applet.json` + `main.js`, for the owner to copy into `~/.config/opusdm/applets/`). `site-news`: settings `feedUrl`, `maxItems` (8), `refreshMinutes` (15); an empty feed URL shows a label asking to set it (no fetch); RSS `<item>` and Atom `<entry>` parsed with regular expressions (CDATA, the five XML entities, numeric `&#39;` / `&#x27;`, items without a link skipped), channel title label plus a `list` whose click calls `opus.applet.open(link)`; fetch error, non-2xx or no items show a label and `console.error`; `setInterval` refresh (minimum 1 minute), `onSettings` re-fetches at once, a stale answer is ignored. `weather-clock`: settings `city` (Rome), `units` (C/F), `refreshMinutes` (30); one 260x320 canvas redrawn every second (radial-gradient face, marks, hour/minute/second hands, then a panel with city, temperature, WMO text and three daily high/low); the clock draws without weather, an error shows "weather unavailable" and `console.error`; transparent window. New `crates/applets/tests/samples.rs` (3 tests: both manifests parse, the real `site-news/main.js` against a local fake feed gives the expected list items and channel title with the link-less item skipped, HTTP 500 gives an error label and no list); they failed before the applets existed. 127 tests in the crate, clippy clean. Verified by hand in a private Xephyr :7 (no window manager, temp XDG dirs): both applets enabled through state files, site-news showed items of `https://hnrss.org/frontpage` and weather-clock showed the clock with Rome weather from the real Open-Meteo (screenshot looked at; the text panel was resized after a first screenshot showed the third day clipped). NOT verified: the owner's real feed, a click on an item (not clicked, it would open the real browser), the transparent window over a compositor (Xephyr has none, windows stacked at 0,0 so site-news was partly covered), `units` F, a city that is not found, the live session. The Rust crate was not changed.
- Applets, Task 8c fix round 1: the applet window close disables the applet; client timeouts. A window-manager close (Alt+F4) of an applet window now means "disable this applet": the close-request handler holds only a `Weak<Host>` (failed upgrade logged), saves `enabled = false` (a save failure is logged, the applet still closes), stops the runtime and lets the host drop and destroy the window; it returns `Propagation::Stop` so the host stays the only owner; logged at debug level. `Host::new` / `Host::with_config` now return `Rc<Host>` (built with `Rc::new_cyclic`, the host keeps a `Weak` of itself for these handlers); `applet_window::open` takes an `on_close` callback. Timeouts: `protocol::request_at` keeps the 1 s `CLIENT_TIMEOUT` for the probe (`GetEdit`) and uses the new `REQUEST_TIMEOUT` (= `server::REPLY_TIMEOUT` + 2 s = 7 s) for every other request (`client_timeout(req)`), so a real client now gets "the applets process is busy". A request the client gave up on is marked (`PendingRequest::is_abandoned`, an `AtomicBool` set by the connection thread on timeout) and the host skips it with a debug log instead of running a late `SetEnabled` / `Reload` (also skips a late `List`). `main` runs `app.run_with_args::<&str>(&[])` so GApplication never parses argv. Tests: server `a_stalled_host_gives_busy_through_request_at` (RED: WouldBlock after 1 s) and `a_request_the_client_gave_up_on_is_marked_abandoned`, protocol `only_the_probe_has_the_short_client_timeout` (the old silent-listener test now probes with `GetEdit`), and 3 new `host_gtk` sections: a close request (window realized, never mapped) disables the applet (state saved, runtime and window gone, `List` enabled=false); a disabled applet's `setInterval` stops firing (each tick hits a local HTTP server; checked that the test fails when the runtime is leaked on close); a button whose `onClick` calls `opus.ui.show` replaces the tree from inside its own signal emission (no panic, new tree shown, the new button works; no bug found there). 125 tests (124 lib + 1 GTK with 9 sections), clippy clean, release build OK. Verified by hand in a private Xephyr :7 with Openbox: `wmctrl -c Demo` closed the window, the state file got `enabled: false`, `List` said enabled=false running=false, the process stayed up, `SetEnabled true` brought it back; the binary started with a stray `--some-stray-arg`; a 1 s interval ticked at 1 s (Task 5 fix). NOT verified: Alt+F4 on the owner's session, the busy answer against the real binary (only in the test), the abandoned skip on the real main loop (only the flag is tested; the host skip is a one-line check in `serve`).
- Applets, Task 5 fix: timer delays read from JS floats. `op_timer_set` read the delay with `as_u64()`, but the prelude's `Math.floor` reaches Rust as a JSON float (`1000.0`), so every delay was 0: `setInterval(fn, 1000)` fired every `MIN_INTERVAL_MS` (4 ms) and `setTimeout(fn, 5000)` fired at once. New `delay_ms` accepts any non-negative finite JSON number (integer or float), floors it and caps it at `MAX_DELAY_MS` (24 days); anything else is still 0; the interval minimum is unchanged. The same bug was in the notify `timeout` (`as_i64()`, so a computed timeout fell back to -1): new `notify_timeout` floors floats, clamps to `-1..=i32::MAX`. The `http.get` `timeoutMs` is not read in this crate (not checked in `opusdm-jscore`). 5 new runtime tests (timeout waits its delay, interval keeps its period, float and string delays with NaN/negative/text meaning 0, `delay_ms` unit test, notify timeout floats); they failed before the fix (interval fired 229 times in 1 s). NOT verified: not run in the owner's session; only `cargo test` and clippy.
- Applets, Task 8c: the host process with plain applet windows. `opusdm-applets` now runs a `gtk::Application` (id `org.opusdm.Applets`, `NON_UNIQUE` because the socket is the single-instance authority and a D-Bus unique name would turn a second process into a silent remote) with `app.hold()`, so it stays up with every applet disabled. New `host` (`Host::new` / `Host::with_config(HostConfig { applets_dir, state_dir, present_windows })`, `load_enabled`, `handle(Request) -> Reply`, `spawn_request_loop`): `List` (one `AppletStatus` per folder, `error` = manifest error or last start error), `SetEnabled` (saves `enabled`, opens or closes the applet, unknown id is an `Error` naming it), `Reload` (close, re-read manifest and script, open if enabled). New `applet_window` (undecorated `ApplicationWindow`, title = manifest name, size = saved width/height else manifest, resizable from the manifest, content slot + red wrapping error label hidden while empty; a window-manager close request is refused and logged; dropping it destroys the window; no X11 hints, no position, geometry not saved). Sink per applet (holds a `Weak`): log -> debug log, error -> `err_log!` + error label, `show` -> parse + build + replace the content, a failed show empties the content and shows the error, `update` / redraw errors -> label + log; `Ui`/`Redraw` events sent while the script starts are queued and applied once the runtime exists; a script that throws at start leaves the window open with the error. `widgets::set_error_listener` (new canvas replay errors after the dedupe, every canvas with or without id) -> error label via an idle callback. `server`: one thread per connection (`opusdm-applets-conn`); `GetEdit`/`SetEdit` answered in the connection thread from a shared `AtomicBool` (SetEdit also tells the host), every other request goes to the main loop through `async_channel` and waits at most 5 s for the reply (`Error("the applets process is busy")` after that, `Error` too if the host channel is closed, all logged). `state::load_in` / `save_in` / `states_dir` are now public. Ownership: `main` keeps the only `Rc<Host>` until `app.run()` returns; startup closure, request loop, sinks, canvas listeners and widget dispatchers hold only `Weak`s and log a failed upgrade. Tests: 10 server tests (incl. a held-open connection does not delay a probe, a request stuck on the main loop does not delay a probe, busy after the timeout, closed channel; they replace the 3 placeholder-era tests), 1 headless widgets test plus a `gtk_widget_checks` section, and `tests/host_gtk.rs` with one GTK test of 6 sections (ran on DISPLAY :0, windows built but never presented): enable/disable starts and stops runtime and window and saves the state, a broken applet is listed with its error while the others run, the window shows the start error and later update errors, a bad show removes the old widgets, reload picks up a changed script and manifest, and after 500 main-loop iterations the interval still updates the window, button clicks still reach the script, and List / SetEdit / SetEnabled over the real socket are answered; dropping the host destroys its windows. 117 tests in the crate (116 lib + 1 GTK integration test), clippy clean, release build OK. Verified by hand in a private Xephyr :7 (temp XDG dirs, socket under `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/opusdm-8c-test`): a demo applet showed its label and a red canvas rectangle with a blue border; a broken applet's window showed the red wrapped error; `List` over the socket gave both with their errors and running flags; SetEdit/GetEdit, SetEnabled off/on, Reload and an unknown id answered as expected; with every applet disabled the process stayed alive; a second instance exited 3 in 16 ms while another client held a connection open. Found, NOT fixed (Task 5, outside this task): `setInterval(fn, 1000)` fires about every 4 ms, very likely because `Math.floor` gives a float that `op_timer_set` reads with `as_u64()` (-> 0 -> clamped to 4 ms). NOT verified: on the owner's session (no live update), with a window manager (no WM in Xephyr, windows stack at 0,0), a real mouse click (only `emit_clicked` in the test), a canvas replay error reaching the label (never triggered), the busy timeout on the real binary; the protocol client timeout (1 s) is shorter than the 5 s reply timeout, so a slow main loop gives a client an I/O timeout rather than the busy answer. No hub, Lister or hotkey code touched.
- Applets, Task 8b: the socket protocol and the single instance. New `opusdm_applets::protocol` (`Request { List, SetEnabled, Reload, SetEdit, GetEdit }`, `Reply { Applets, Edit, Ok, Error }`, `AppletStatus`, serde default tagging; `socket_path()` = `$OPUSDM_APPLETS_SOCK` else `applets.sock` next to the hub socket; `request` / `request_at` client with 1 s timeouts, a missing socket, a timeout or an EOF is an `io::Error`), `instance` (`acquire` / `acquire_at` over `opusdm_ipc::instance::acquire_socket`; live only if `GetEdit` is answered by `Reply::Edit`, silence or garbage is a stale file that gets replaced; `Exit { AlreadyRunning, Failed }`) and `server` (thread `opusdm-applets-accept` owns the listener for the whole run, one connection at a time with 5 s timeouts, a bad frame or timeout is logged with `err_log!` and drops only that connection, a fatal `accept()` error is logged and exits 1; `answer` is a placeholder: `List` -> empty list, `GetEdit` -> `Edit(false)`, the rest `Error("not ready")`, Task 8c replaces it with the host). `main.rs`: acquire, accept thread, join; already running logs and exits 3, failure logs and exits 1. 12 new tests (108 in the crate), clippy clean. Verified by hand: with `OPUSDM_APPLETS_SOCK` in a private dir the second `opusdm-applets` logged "already running at <path>" and exited 3; after killing the first by PID a new one started over the stale socket file. NOT verified: no client talked to the running binary beyond the probe, the real host is not wired (Task 8c), GTK/hub/Lister/hotkeys untouched and not run.
- Applets, sandbox check after Tasks 2 and 8a (builder, 2026-10-26, own Xephyr on :5 with Openbox NOT running, copied config, private `XDG_STATE_HOME` and hub socket). Exercised and working with the branch's release hub: a second `opusdm-hub` hands off ("already running; asked it to raise its toolbar", exit 0) and the first stays alive; after `kill -9` of the hub the stale socket is replaced and a new hub starts; Lister: Enter in the path bar, double click on a folder, Backspace, Alt+Left/Right; a second Lister with SOURCE/DEST roles; a Copy job between two scratch folders; a global hotkey `Alt+h` running a script (`script:httptest`) where `opus.http.get("https://example.com")` returned status 200 and `ftp://x` threw `unsupported scheme`. NOT checked here: Alt+1/Alt+2 (no window manager in this Xephyr, so no desktops), F-keys, Ctrl+backslash; Ctrl+Shift+space and Ctrl+Print start their tool, which exits at once with status 0, exactly as with the installed `/opt/opusdm` hub (A/B done), so it is a sandbox limit. Seen in both the branch hub and the installed hub (not a regression): `thumbnail failed ... No child processes (os error 10)` for the videos and a PDF on the Desktop. The running session was not updated: the owner said the Task 2 behaviour was fine; Task 8a still needs the owner's live check.
- ipc: single-instance socket and sibling lookup shared with the tools. New `opusdm_ipc::instance`: `acquire_socket(path, is_live)` returning `Acquired { Bound, Live, Failed }` (ensure the socket dir, probe, remove a stale file, bind; same steps, same order, same error strings as the hub's old code; a live owner leaves the socket file untouched), `sibling_bin_in` (moved unchanged) and `EXIT_ALREADY_RUNNING` (3, for `opusdm-applets` and the hub's applet supervisor). Hub: `children::acquire_socket` is now a thin match over it and keeps the `RaiseToolbar` hand-off and `AcquireOutcome`; `children` re-exports `sibling_bin_in`, so `main.rs` and `desktop_ops.rs` are unchanged. The hub behaviour is meant to be identical; no ownership or lifetime change. 5 new tests in `instance.rs` (4 on real sockets in temp dirs, plus the moved `sibling_bin_in` test); the hub's other tests unchanged. NOT verified: the rule 4 checklist (Lister, global hotkeys, hub roles, a second hub raising the toolbar and exiting 0, a stale socket after a kill) was NOT run by the implementer; the controller runs it with the owner.
- Applets, Task 7: the canvas, Canvas 2D style drawing over cairo. New `src/canvas.js` (evaluated by `runtime.rs` and handed to the prelude): the `ctx` given to a canvas `draw(ctx)` is a recorder with `save restore translate rotate scale beginPath closePath moveTo lineTo arc rect fill stroke fillRect strokeRect clearRect fillText strokeText drawImage createLinearGradient createRadialGradient` (gradient `addColorStop`), properties `fillStyle strokeStyle lineWidth lineCap globalAlpha font textAlign textBaseline` (getters follow `save`/`restore`), `ctx.width`/`ctx.height`/`ctx.canvas`; every call becomes a JSON command `{ "op": "fillRect", "x", "y", "w", "h" }`, NaN/infinity travel as `null`. `prelude.js` tracks the canvases of the shown tree (a canvas with no id gets `__opusCanvas<n>`), paints every canvas right after `opus.ui.show` (a throwing draw is reported, the others still paint), `__opusInternal.redraw(id)` / `opus.ui.redraw` / `Runtime::redraw` run the draw synchronously and send `Event::Redraw { id, commands }` through the new op `canvas.redraw`; an unknown canvas is a reported `Error`, a throwing draw sends nothing (the previous picture stays), more than 100000 commands throw a `RangeError`, an update of `draw` is used by the next redraw. New `opusdm_applets::canvas`: `Command` (serde, tagged by `op`), `Style`, `Gradient`, `parse` (array, at most `MAX_COMMANDS` 100000, an unknown command names itself), `replay` / `replay_with` (with a per-canvas `Images` cache: images loaded with `gdk::Texture`, kept by path, dropped when no longer drawn, a failed load not cached, absolute paths only, at most 4096x4096 pixels), `parse_color` (`#rgb`, `#rgba`, `#rrggbb`, `#rrggbbaa`, `rgb()`, `rgba()`, `transparent`, 15 names), `parse_font` (`[bold] [italic] <size>px <family>`). The replay keeps going past a failing command (bad colour keeps the previous style, bad font, missing image, zero scale) and returns an `Err` naming the first failure and the count; a command with a NaN or infinite number is a no-op; a `restore` without `save` is ignored; `globalAlpha` works for colours and gradients. Text uses cairo's toy text API (fontconfig falls back on a default font for an unknown family; no new dependency, pangocairo is not in the tree). `widgets.rs`: a canvas `DrawingArea` gets a draw function that clips to the canvas size and replays the last commands through a `Painter` (holds the commands `Rc<RefCell<Vec<Command>>>`, the image cache and the last error, no widget); a replay error is logged with `err_log!` only when it differs from the last one logged for that canvas. New `widgets::set_canvas_commands(tree, id, commands)`: stores and `queue_draw()`s; unknown id, non-canvas id or too many commands is an `Err` and keeps the previous picture. Changed test: `redraw_calls_the_js_hook` now expects the reported "no canvas" error instead of a silent no-op (decision 1). 35 new tests (96 in the crate): 24 headless `cairo::ImageSurface` replay tests, 10 runtime tests (paint on show and on redraw, every ctx method parses, generated id, unknown id, throwing draw, too many commands, update of draw, a new tree forgets the old canvases), 1 headless log dedupe test, and new sections in `gtk_widget_checks` (ran on DISPLAY :0, no window created): `set_canvas_commands` stores and refuses, and script `show` -> `Event::Ui` + `Event::Redraw` -> `parse` -> `set_canvas_commands`. Clippy clean. NOT verified: no window exists yet (Task 8c), so the GTK draw function itself never ran and nothing was seen on screen (pixels were only checked on offscreen cairo surfaces), `queue_draw()` was called but its repaint was not observed; text was only checked to paint pixels, not by eye; an image changed on disk under the same path is not reloaded; unknown ctx properties (`lineJoin`, `measureText`, ...) are not supported; the app was not run (no hub, Lister or hotkey code touched).
- Applets, Task 6: the declarative widget tree. JS `opus.ui.show(tree)`, `opus.ui.update(id, props)`, `opus.ui.redraw(canvasId)` in `prelude.js`: every function prop is registered with `__opusInternal.registerHandler` and sent as `{ "handler": <id> }`; `show` forgets every handler of the previous tree, `update` forgets the handlers the updated prop had (button `onClick`, list `items`, ...). New host ops `ui.show` / `ui.update` send `Event::Ui({ "op": "show", "tree" })` and `Event::Ui({ "op": "update", "id", "props" })`. New `opusdm_applets::ui_tree` (`Node`, `Kind`, `ListItem`, `parse`, pure, `MAX_DEPTH` 32; types box, label, button, entry, switch, list, image, canvas plus `id`, `class`, `visible`; unknown types or props, wrong prop types, duplicate ids, relative image paths are errors naming the type, id and prop) and `opusdm_applets::widgets` (`Dispatch` trait, `build(node, dispatch) -> BuiltTree { root, .. }`, `apply_update(tree, id, props)`, `BuiltTree::widget` / `handler_of`): gtk Box, Label, Button, Entry, Switch, ListBox (one activatable label per item, each with its own handler), Picture (texture loaded at build, a missing or bad file is an `Err`), DrawingArea of the given size (no drawing yet, `draw` handler kept for Task 7). Args: click `null`, entry change the text, switch toggle the state, list row `null`. An update validates every prop before applying any; setting an entry text or a switch state from an update blocks its signal handler, so it does not echo to `onChange` / `onToggle`. New `Runtime::dispatcher()`: an `Rc<dyn Dispatch>` holding only a `Weak` of the runtime; a widget event after the runtime is dropped is logged with `err_log!` (applet and handler named) and ignored. 14 new tests (61 in the crate): 7 pure `parse` tests, 6 runtime tests for `opus.ui` and the dispatcher, and one GTK test `gtk_widget_checks` (ran here on DISPLAY :0, no window created) that also goes end to end: script `opus.ui.show` -> `Event::Ui` -> `parse` -> `build` with `rt.dispatcher()` -> click -> the script's handler logs; a click after drop logs and does nothing. Clippy clean. NOT verified: no window shows the widgets yet (Task 8c), so nothing was seen on screen, clicked with a real mouse or typed into; the app was not run (no hub, Lister or hotkey code touched); GTK replaces a non-empty entry text with a delete then an insert, so `onChange` can see an intermediate `""`; if Rust refuses an update after JS swapped a handler, the widget keeps the old (forgotten) handler and a click reports "no handler N".
- Applets, Task 5: `opusdm_applets::runtime`, the Boa runtime of one applet (`Runtime::start` / `start_with_notifier`, `deliver_settings`, `dispatch`, `redraw`, `stop`; `Event { Log, Error, Ui, Redraw }`, `Sink`, `Notifier`) and `src/prelude.js` (`setTimeout`/`setInterval`/`clearTimeout`/`clearInterval`, `console.log/warn/error`, `opus.version`, `opus.log`, `opus.http.get` returning a Promise, `opus.fs.exists/readText/list`, `opus.notify` returning a Promise, `opus.applet.{id, dir, name, settings, onSettings, open, assetPath, readAsset}`, internal `__opusInternal.registerHandler` / `redraw` for Tasks 6-7). Ownership: the caller owns the `Runtime`; it holds the only `Rc<Inner>`, timer tasks, HTTP/notify completions and queued calls hold a `Weak` and log with `err_log!` when it is gone; `stop()` (also on drop) aborts every timer task and refuses later calls, an async answer after `stop()` is dropped with a debug log. Timers and completions run on the thread-default glib main context (`spawn_local`), the blocking GET and the D-Bus notify run on a thread per call. A call that finds the `Context` busy is queued on the main loop instead of panicking. Runaway code is cut by Boa `RuntimeLimits` (`LOOP_ITERATION_LIMIT` 10 000 000 per call frame, `RECURSION_LIMIT` 512, uncatchable by the script); unhandled rejections are found with Boa's `HostHooks::promise_rejection_tracker`. `opus.applet.open` accepts only http, https and file URLs (gio default handler); assets must stay in the applet folder (lexical check, then the canonical path must be inside it). New deps: boa_engine 0.22, gtk4, async-channel, zbus, opusdm-jscore. 29 new tests (47 in the crate, about 3 s, the runaway tests take most of it in a debug build), clippy clean. NOT verified: nothing consumes the runtime yet (no window, no host process) and the app was not run; the real D-Bus notifier and `opus.applet.open` with a valid URL were never called (tests inject a fake notifier and only check the refused schemes); no real HTTPS request from an applet; the loop limit counts every loop of one call frame together, so a single callback cannot run more than 10 000 000 iterations in total.

NOT verified: nothing consumes the crate yet; the app was not run (no hub, Lister or hotkey code touched).
- Applets, Task 3: new crate `opusdm-applets` (`crates/applets`, lib `opusdm_applets`, bin `opusdm-applets`) with `manifest::parse` for `applet.json` (`Manifest`, `WindowSpec`, `SettingDef`, `SettingKind`). Defaults: entry `main.js`, window 300x200, min 50x50 (capped to the window size), not resizable, not transparent. Unknown top-level fields are ignored, permissions limited to `net`/`fs`/`exec` (not enforced), entry must stay inside the folder, setting defaults are type checked. 10 unit tests pass, clippy clean. Also fixes `request_from_json` so an integral float `timeoutMs` (a JS number such as `1500.0`) is accepted. - Feature (scripts): .deb packages for Ubuntu 26.04 and 26.10. New `scripts/build-deb-docker.sh [release...]` (default `26.04 26.10`) builds the package inside an `ubuntu:<release>` container (`scripts/Dockerfile.deb`, same build dependencies as the release-deb workflow, rustup toolchain), so the Depends written by `dpkg-shlibdeps` match each release. Output `target/deb-docker/opusdm_<version>~ubuntu<release>_<arch>.deb`: the suffix makes the two versions distinct for apt (26.10 sorts after 26.04). `scripts/build-deb.sh` got the optional `DEB_VERSION_SUFFIX` variable (empty by default: the CI build and `build-deb.sh` alone are unchanged). The source is copied into the container (target, dist and .git left out), the checkout is never written; one persistent cargo target per release under `target/docker-cache/`. No Rust code touched. `scripts/build-apt-repo.sh` now publishes one apt suite per release, named after the codename (`resolute` = 26.04, `stonking` = 26.10), with its own pool `pool/<suite>/main/o/opusdm`, so apt on 26.04 never sees the 26.10 build. The suite comes from the `~ubuntuNN.NN` suffix of the .deb version; a .deb without the suffix goes to the legacy suite `stable`, which also receives the 26.04 build (existing sources lists keep working); an old single pool is moved under `pool/stable`. `index.html` explains the sources line with `$VERSION_CODENAME`. Checked: both packages built (0.12.0~ubuntu26.04 and 0.12.0~ubuntu26.10, Depends differ: libc6 2.43/2.44, libssl3t64/libssl4); each installs with apt in a clean ubuntu:26.04 / ubuntu:26.10 container, 17 ELF binaries with no missing library; `build-apt-repo.sh` run in a 26.04 container with a throwaway key built resolute, stonking and stable with the right package in each and a valid InRelease. NOT checked: the app itself on 26.04 / 26.10 (GUI, hub, hotkeys: only install and ldd), `apt install` from a real published repository, a repository already holding old packages (the pool migration was not exercised). The workflow `release-deb.yml` is NOT changed: CI still builds one unsuffixed package on 26.04, which lands in `stable` only; publishing both releases from CI needs a matrix calling `build-deb-docker.sh`.

- Feature (shot): "Save as" now opens in the folder of the last image saved with it (`last_dir` in `~/.local/state/opusdm/shot.json`, outside the config; new `opusdm_ipc::settings::state_dir`), falling back to the Pictures folder when none is stored or it no longer exists. The folder is stored only after a successful write (`config::remember_dir`, via `save_and_remember`). Quick save is unchanged (fixed folder, not remembered). No ownership or lifetime change. Tests: new config round trip and old-file tests; `cargo test -p opusdm-shot` ok, build ok. NOT checked: the Save as dialog in a running session or the X11 sandbox, the installed `/opt/opusdm` binary (`live-update.sh`), the CLAUDE.md rule 4 checklist (Lister, global hotkeys, hub).

## 2026-10-23
- Feature (bluetooth, panel, settings): Bluetooth like KDE Plasma 6, branch `bluetooth`. Design `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-23-bluetooth-design.md`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-23-bluetooth.md`. New crate `opusdm-bluetooth` (no GTK): BlueZ over the system bus with zbus (`GetManagedObjects` parsed into `BtState`: first adapter, its devices, battery from `Battery1`, rfkill from `/sys/class/rfkill`), requests through `BluezBackend`, rfkill soft block cleared by writing `/dev/rfkill` (writable by the owner, no password), a watcher thread that follows BlueZ signals and ends its session when the owner of `org.bluez` changes (a restarted BlueZ forgets the agents: the next session registers again), and the pairing agent `org.bluez.Agent1` (`KeyboardDisplay`; PIN, passkey, code to type on a keyboard, code to confirm, authorize, authorize service; a request nobody answers is rejected and logged; calls not from BlueZ are refused). New crate `opusdm-bluetooth-config`, program `opusdm-bluetooth-manager` (`--pair` opens "Add device" at once) and entry **Bluetooth** in Settings / Advanced: the adapter switch, "Visible to other devices" (Discoverable and Pairable together for 180 s), the name of the computer, the paired devices (connected first, battery) with details (rename, trusted, blocked, Connect/Disconnect, Remove with confirmation), and "Add device...": turns Bluetooth on if off, Pairable + scan, the devices found update live, Pair then trust and connect; closing it cancels a pairing in progress, stops the scan and turns Pairable off. Panel: Bluetooth icon after the battery icon (hidden without BlueZ or adapter, faded when off, blue dot with a connected device), popover with the on/off switch, the paired devices with Connect/Disconnect, "Add device..." and "Configure..." (start the manager); the panel serves the default agent, so a phone can pair to the PC while it is visible, with the dialogs shown by the panel; notifications ("Bluetooth") for a paired device connected / disconnected and a battery at or below 20 % (once per crossing). Out of scope by choice: headphone audio profiles, file transfer. Calls made by the builder after the design review (to check): the tray opens the standalone manager, not the Settings page (Settings cannot open on a chosen Advanced page today); the adapter is never left pairable for good. Found live and fixed: after an rfkill unblock BlueZ powers the adapter by itself and answers `Busy` meanwhile, so the backend waits for it. Ownership: see the commit bodies (`attach` owns `BluetoothCenter` like `PrinterCenter`; the manager window owns its `App`, everything else holds it weakly and logs). Tests: 15 in `opusdm-bluetooth` (parser, model, rfkill event bytes and sysfs read, agent answers incl. a dropped receiver and a dropped reply, fake backend, error words) plus 2 live tests run by hand against the real BlueZ (`--ignored`: read the adapter; the watcher sends backend and state and registers an agent; passed); 11 in `opusdm-bluetooth-config` incl. 4 GTK tests (the window keeps the app over 200 main loop turns and a real close drops it; Connect reaches the backend; closing "Add device" while pairing cancels, stops the scan and Pairable; an agent Cancel closes the dialog and rejects), checked to really run (a mutated assertion fails); 5 in the panel (icon look, tooltip, notes: none on the first state, connect/disconnect, battery once per crossing). Clippy: no new warning. Checked by hand against the real BlueZ (adapter `hci0`, no paired device): manager in Xephyr: state read, Bluetooth off/on (Powered seen with busctl), Visible on/off (Discoverable, Pairable and both timeouts 180 seen), "Add device" (Discovering and Pairable true, false again after Close), `rfkill block bluetooth` then the switch: unblocked and powered with no error. Settings in Xephyr (copy of the config): Advanced shows Bluetooth after Printers, it opens, Network and Printers still open. Hub in the X11 sandbox (`run-x11.sh`, copy of the config): the icon shows, the popover, off/on from the switch (icon faded while off), "Add device..." started the release manager with `--pair` and the scan; the panel agent registered with no error; a call to it from a plain process is refused by the bus policy. CLAUDE.md rule 4 in the sandbox: double click on a folder opens a Lister, Backspace, Alt+Left/Right, Enter in the path bar, Alt+2/Alt+1, Ctrl+Shift+space (run), Ctrl+Print (shot). NOT checked: any real pairing, connect, disconnect, rename, trust, block, remove, battery or notification with a real device (no Bluetooth device was in range: the scan found nothing), the agent dialogs with a real request (only through the GTK test), a phone pairing to the PC while visible, `systemctl restart bluetooth` with the panel running (needs sudo), a hardware rfkill switch, Rename of the adapter, monitor hotplug with the Bluetooth icon, Ctrl+backslash and Alt+backslash (do nothing in the sandbox), F-keys, SOURCE/DEST with two Listers, a Copy job, a function run, the running session (`scripts/live-update.sh --all` needs sudo: to do by the owner), the installed `/opt/opusdm` binaries. Note: during the checks a mistaken `opusdm-bluetooth-manager --version` opened the manager for a moment on the real display; it was closed at once.
- Fix (printing, printer-config), from the final review of the printer manager: (1) the add wizard and `CupsBackend::add` refuse a name that differs from an existing queue only by case: CUPS compares queue names without case and `lpadmin -p` on an existing queue changes it, so typing `c364series` would have replaced the device and driver of `C364Series` (shown live: without the guard the probe test's queue was changed); names starting with `-` or holding `?` or `@` are refused too. (2) Every failed job of the manager is logged with `err_log!`, not only shown. (3) Every failed `Weak` upgrade in the manager and wizard handlers logs what was ignored (`alive()` helpers). (4) New GTK test `the_program_window_owns_the_app`: with only the window holding the app, the loops keep following CUPS after 200 main loop turns, a close waiting for the unsaved changes answer keeps it, a real close drops it. (5) A Save chosen while switching printers marked the next printer's edits as saved when its answer came; it now marks only the printer it saved (`a_save_answer_for_another_printer_keeps_the_edit`). (6) Emptying the address field of the wizard forgets the typed address (Next was left enabled with an address no longer shown). NOT checked by hand: the log lines themselves (read in the code).
- Feature (panel, printing): printer icon in the panel tray (before the network icon, primary bar only). It shows only while a job is in a queue or a printer is in error: a badge with the jobs (blue), grey while the printer holding them was disabled by the owner, red with the count or `!` on an error (a `-error` state reason, a stopped job, or a printer CUPS stopped with a message while jobs wait). The popover lists the printers with jobs or problems and their jobs with Hold/Resume, Retry (stopped job or printer) and Cancel, run on a worker thread (`opusdm-panel-printer`); "Manage printers..." starts `opusdm-printer-manager`. Notifications (`org.freedesktop.Notifications`, app "Printers") once per error episode, and once when a printer has not answered for 2 minutes with jobs waiting; a 10 s timer looks again at a printer that does not answer, because the watcher sends nothing while the state stays the same (found live: without it the 2 minute note never came). Ownership: `attach` owns `Rc<PrinterCenter>` for the whole panel run, like the network tray; the watcher and failure loops and the timer hold it strongly; the loops log with `err_log!` if their channel closes. Tests: 4 new in the panel (action worker on the fake, failures as text, the worker pokes the watcher, badge text) and `a_timer_is_needed_only_while_a_printer_does_not_answer` in `opusdm-printing`. Checked live in the X11 sandbox (`run-x11.sh`, copy of the config) with a probe queue: no icon with an empty queue; grey badge 1 for a disabled printer with a job; Hold from the popover (seen in `lpstat`); `cupsdisable -r "Unable to send data to printer."` made the badge red and sent exactly one notification (watched with `dbus-monitor`, none more in the next 12 s); Retry enabled the printer; Cancel emptied the queue and the icon went away; a job on an unreachable printer sent one "Not answering" note after 2 minutes and no other; "Manage printers..." opened the manager; the network and devices popovers still open. CLAUDE.md rule 4 in the sandbox: double click on a folder opens a Lister, Backspace goes to the parent, Enter in the path bar, Alt+Left/Right, Alt+1/Alt+2 switch desktops. Alt+backslash (new Lister) and Ctrl+backslash (terminal) open no window in the sandbox, the same with the installed `/opt/opusdm` hub (A/B on a second Xephyr), so it is a limit of the sandbox, not of this change. NOT checked: the running session (`scripts/live-update.sh --all` needs sudo: to do by the owner), Ctrl+Shift+space, Ctrl+Print, F-keys, SOURCE/DEST with two Listers, a Copy job, a function run, monitor hotplug with the printer icon. Note: the sandbox shares the session D-Bus, so the two test notifications ("Printer opusdm_probe") also reached the notification server of the real session.
- Feature (printing, settings): printer manager, like the Printers module of KDE Plasma. New crate `opusdm-printing` (no GTK): a small IPP client over the CUPS socket (`/run/cups/cups.sock`, peer credentials, no password) reads the printers, the default and the queue; the CUPS tools (`lpadmin`, `lpinfo`, `lpoptions`, `lp`, `cancel`, `cupsenable`/`cupsdisable`/`cupsaccept`) run with `LC_ALL=C` for the changes (checked: the owner is in `lpadmin`, no password asked). A watcher thread polls CUPS (2 s with jobs, 5 s without) and sends only changes. New crate `opusdm-printer-config`, program `opusdm-printer-manager` and entry **Printers** in Settings / Advanced: the printers on the left (star on the default, state, jobs), tabs **General** (description, location, model, address, state; Set as default, Enable/Disable, Print test page; Save/Revert), **Options** (the driver's default options, common ones first, the others under "More options"; an option the driver gives no default shows "(not set)", the Konica `PageSize` has none) and **Queue (n)** (Cancel job, Hold, Resume, Retry, Cancel all). `+` opens the add printer wizard: device found on the network (already added ones marked) or typed address, driver (driverless for IPP devices, driver list with search, or a PPD file), name (checked against the CUPS rules and the names taken), then Done with Print test page. Defaults are system wide (`lpadmin -o`, `lpadmin -d`). Tests: 37 in `opusdm-printing` (IPP codec, model, parsers, transport incl. chunked answers, fake backend, watcher, tray rules) plus 1 live test run by hand against the real CUPS with a probe queue (`--ignored live_probe_queue`, passed); 19 in `opusdm-printer-config` incl. 3 GTK tests (the window stays alive after many main loop turns and follows the backend; a Cancel in the queue reaches the backend; the wizard adds a printer), checked to really run (a mutated assertion fails). Checked by hand in Xephyr against the real CUPS with temporary `opusdm_probe` queues: list, the three tabs on the Konica, Hold/Resume (seen in `lpstat`), Save of the description, the unsaved changes question on switching printer and inside Settings (Save there saved to CUPS), Remove, the wizard (bizhub found and marked already added, typed `socket://` address, driver search, invalid name refused, printer added, test page queued), the other Advanced entries still open.
